The condensed motivic cohomology groups Hcondp,q(X, ℤ) are defined as HomSHcond(S)(Σ∞,condX+, 𝟙cond(q)[p]) and satisfy the condensed Beilinson–Lichtenbaum conjecture: the condensed cycle class map to condensed étale cohomology is an isomorphism in weights ≤ p and injective in weight p+1. The condensed algebraic cobordism MGLcond represents condensed algebraic cobordism theory, and the condensed Hopkins–Morel isomorphism identifies MGLcond/(a1, a2, …) with the condensed motivic Eilenberg–MacLane spectrum Hℤcond. The condensed motivic Steenrod algebra acts on condensed motivic cohomology with condensed Milnor basis.

Condensed Topological Cyclic Homology and the Condensed Cyclotomic Trace

Condensed topological cyclic homology TCcond(R) for a condensed ring R is defined as the condensed homotopy fixed points of the condensed cyclotomic Frobenius on the condensed topological Hochschild homology THHcond(R). The condensed cyclotomic structure on THHcond(R) consists of condensed maps φp,cond : THHcond(R) → THHcond(R)tCp,cond for each prime p, where (−)tCp,cond denotes condensed Tate fixed points. The condensed cyclotomic trace Kcond(R) → TCcond(R) is a map of condensed ring spectra from the condensed algebraic K-theory spectrum. The condensed Dundas–Goodwillie–McCarthy theorem states that for a condensed nilpotent extension R → R/I of condensed rings, the condensed square relating Kcond, TCcond, and the condensed relative versions is condensed cartesian. The condensed Bhatt–Morrow–Scholze integral p-adic Hodge theory identifies TCcond(R; ℤp)∧p,cond with the condensed syntomic cohomology 𝔸inf,cond(R) for a condensed perfectoid ring R. The condensed Nygaard filtration on TCcond(R) has graded pieces the condensed prismatic cohomology groups Δcond(R){n}. The condensed Hesselholt–Madsen computation for truncated polynomial algebras gives TCcond(𝔽p,cond[x]/(xn)) as a condensed module over TCcond(𝔽p,cond) with explicit condensed generators and relations. The condensed cyclotomic trace for condensed ℓ-adic coefficients relates Kcond(R; ℤℓ) to the condensed étale cohomology of R via the condensed Beilinson–Lichtenbaum conjecture.

Condensed Derived Algebraic Geometry and the Condensed Lurie Representability Theorem

Condensed derived algebraic geometry, developed by Clausen–Scholze–Lurie, replaces the classical site of affine schemes with the condensed site of condensed animated rings. A condensed animated ring is a condensed object in the ∞-category of animated rings (simplicial commutative rings). The condensed ∞-topos of condensed derived stacks Shcond(CAlgcondanim,op) admits a condensed geometric structure sheaf 𝒪cond. The condensed Lurie representability theorem characterises condensed derived stacks that are condensed geometric: a condensed functor F : CAlgcondanim → Spacescond is representable by a condensed derived stack if and only if it satisfies condensed étale descent, condensed infinitesimal cohesiveness, and has a condensed cotangent complex LF,cond which is condensed perfect and connective. The condensed cotangent complex LA/S,cond for a map of condensed animated rings A → B is defined as the condensed left derived functor of Kähler differentials; it is a condensed B-module. A condensed morphism f : X → Y of condensed derived stacks is condensed smooth of relative dimension n if its condensed cotangent complex Lf,cond is a condensed vector bundle of rank n. The condensed Artin approximation theorem states that for a condensed complete local Noetherian condensed ring R, any condensed formal solution to a condensed smooth moduli problem lifts to a condensed algebraic solution. The condensed Tannakian formalism for condensed derived stacks identifies the condensed ∞-category of condensed quasi-coherent sheaves on a condensed derived stack X with the condensed ∞-category of condensed comodules over the condensed Hopf algebroid of condensed functions on the condensed loop space LcondX. The condensed derived loop space LcondX = X ×X×condX,cond X carries a condensed S1,cond-action, and its condensed fixed points give the condensed Hochschild homology HHcond(X).

  • Confusing the condensed Narasimhan–Seshadri correspondence with the classical one; the condensed version requires the base to be a condensed Riemann surface and the bundles to be condensed vector bundles with condensed structure group, not just topological vector bundles.
  • Assuming the condensed Drinfeld module rank equals the classical rank without checking the condensed leading coefficient condition; the condensed Frobenius τ acts on the condensed additive group, and the degree is measured in the condensed polynomial ring.
  • Treating the condensed 𝔸1-homotopy category as an ordinary localisation; it is a condensed Bousfield localisation of the condensed Nisnevich ∞-topos, and the condensed motivic weak equivalences are detected by condensed Nisnevich sheaves of condensed spaces.
  • Believing the condensed cyclotomic trace Kcond → TCcond is always an equivalence after p-completion; it is an equivalence for condensed perfectoid rings but for general condensed rings it factors through condensed syntomic cohomology.
  • Overlooking that the condensed cotangent complex LA/S,cond is a condensed animated module, not merely a condensed chain complex; its condensed homotopy groups are the condensed André–Quillen homology groups.
  • Misidentifying the condensed motivic sphere spectrum 𝟙cond with the condensed suspension spectrum of the condensed affine line; 𝟙cond = Σ∞,condSpec ℤcond, while 𝔸1,cond is the condensed affine line used for 𝔸1-localisation.
  • Assuming the condensed six-functor formalism on SHcond(S) satisfies the same base-change formulas as in classical motivic homotopy theory; the condensed base-change requires the condensed morphism to be condensed proper and condensed smooth, and the condensed exchange transformation is an equivalence only under these condensed hypotheses.

Condensed Perfectoid Spaces and the Condensed Tilting Equivalence

The condensed formalism provides a natural home for perfectoid spaces because the category of condensed sets admits all limits and colimits, and the condensed site of extremally disconnected profinite sets is a basis for the pro-étale topology. A condensed perfectoid space is a condensed analytic adic space locally modeled on Spa(R, R⁺) where R is a condensed perfectoid ring. The condensed tilt of a condensed perfectoid ring R of characteristic zero is the condensed perfectoid ring R♭ = limx↦x^p R/p, formed in the category of condensed rings. The condensed tilting equivalence states that the ∞-category of condensed perfectoid spaces over Spa(ℚ_p, ℤ_p) is equivalent to the ∞-category of condensed perfectoid spaces over Spa(𝔽_p((t)), 𝔽_p[[t]]). This equivalence is induced by the condensed Fontaine period ring A_inf,cond(R) = W_cond(R♭) where W_cond denotes the condensed Witt vectors. The condensed θ-map θ_cond: A_inf,cond(R) → R is a surjective map of condensed rings whose kernel is generated by a condensed non-zero-divisor ξ = p - [p♭] where p♭ ∈ R♭ is a compatible system of p-power roots of p. The condensed tilting equivalence respects the condensed pro-étale site: for a condensed perfectoid space X, the condensed pro-étale topos X_proét,cond is equivalent to the condensed pro-étale topos of its tilt X♭. This allows one to transport condensed p-adic Hodge theory statements to characteristic p, where they become statements about condensed crystalline cohomology. The condensed almost purity theorem asserts that for a condensed perfectoid ring R, any finite étale R-algebra is almost finite étale in the condensed sense, meaning its condensed cotangent complex is almost zero. The condensed direct summand conjecture follows: the map R → R⁺/p is a condensed almost split injection of condensed R⁺-modules. The condensed perfectoid correspondence of Scholze is internalised: the category of condensed perfectoid spaces over a fixed base is equivalent to the category of condensed perfectoid spaces over the tilt, compatibly with condensed diamonds.

Condensed p-adic Hodge Theory: The Condensed Fontaine Period Rings

The condensed Fontaine period rings B_dR,cond, B_cris,cond, B_st,cond, and B_HT,cond are constructed as condensed rings equipped with additional structures: a condensed filtration, a condensed Frobenius φ, a condensed monodromy operator N, and a condensed Galois action. For a condensed perfectoid ring R, the condensed de Rham period ring B_dR,cond(R) is the condensed completion of A_inf,cond(R)[1/p] with respect to the condensed kernel of θ_cond. It carries a condensed decreasing filtration Fil^i B_dR,cond(R) = (ker θ_cond)^i B_dR,cond(R) and a condensed Galois action. The condensed crystalline period ring B_cris,cond(R) is the condensed subring of B_dR,cond(R) generated by A_inf,cond(R)[1/p] and the condensed divided powers of ker θ_cond. It carries a condensed Frobenius φ and a condensed filtration. The condensed semistable period ring B_st,cond(R) = B_cris,cond(R)[log([π])] where π ∈ A_inf,cond(R) is a condensed uniformiser; it carries a condensed monodromy operator N satisfying Nφ = pφN. The condensed Hodge-Tate period ring B_HT,cond(R) = ⊕i∈ℤ C_cond(R){i} where C_cond(R) = R̂^♭ is the condensed completed tilt. The condensed p-adic Hodge comparison theorems take the form: for a proper smooth condensed formal scheme 𝔛 over O_C, there are condensed Galois-equivariant isomorphisms H^i_ét(𝔛_C, ℤ_p) ⊗ B_dR,cond ≅ H^i_dR(𝔛/O_C) ⊗ B_dR,cond compatible with filtrations, and similarly for B_cris,cond and B_st,cond when 𝔛 has good or semistable reduction. The condensed Sen theory identifies the condensed Sen operator Θ acting on H^i_ét(𝔛_C, ℤ_p) ⊗ C_cond with the condensed Hodge-Tate weights. The condensed Colmez-Fontaine theorem states that a condensed B_dR,cond-representation is condensed de Rham if and only if it is condensed potentially semistable. The condensed Berger fundamental exact sequence 0 → ℚ_p → B_cris,condφ=1 → B_dR,cond/B_dR,cond+ → 0 computes condensed Galois cohomology of p-adic representations. The condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence for GL2(ℚ_p) is formulated using the condensed Emerton functor from condensed locally analytic representations to condensed (φ, Γ)-modules over the condensed Robba ring ℛ_cond.

Worked Comparison: Classical vs Condensed Fontaine Rings
Explain why the condensed Fontaine period ring B_dR,cond(R) for a condensed perfectoid ring R is not merely the condensation of the classical B_dR(R) but carries additional higher homotopical information. Illustrate with the condensed cotangent complex of the θ-map.
Classically, B_dR(R) = A_inf(R)[1/p]̂ker θ is a discrete ring. Its condensation B_dR(R)_cond is a condensed ring whose values on extremally disconnected profinite sets S are continuous maps S → B_dR(R). However, the condensed Fontaine ring B_dR,cond(R) is defined as the condensed completion of A_inf,cond(R)[1/p] with respect to the condensed ideal ker θ_cond in the category of condensed rings. This completion is a derived completion: B_dR,cond(R) = lim_n A_inf,cond(R)[1/p]/(ker θ_cond)^n taken in the ∞-category of condensed animated rings. The condensed cotangent complex LB_dR,cond/A_inf,cond[1/p] is not concentrated in degree zero; its homotopy groups π_i for i > 0 encode the derived structure of the completion. Concretely, π1(L) ≅ (ker θ_cond)/(ker θ_cond)2 as a condensed B_dR,cond-module, and higher homotopy groups are condensed divided powers. The classical ring B_dR(R) has no such higher homotopy because the classical completion is underived. The condensed version sees the derived infinitesimal neighbourhood of the closed immersion Spa(B_dR,cond) → Spa(A_inf,cond[1/p]), which is essential for the condensed Hodge-Tate comparison where the condensed Nygaard filtration on prismatic cohomology is identified with the condensed filtration on B_dR,cond via the condensed syntomic complex. Thus B_dR,cond(R) is a condensed animated ring, not merely a condensed discrete ring, and its condensation from the classical ring loses the derived structure that the condensed formalism is designed to capture.
Exam Strategy: Condensed Period Rings
When a question asks for the condensed de Rham comparison, write the isomorphism as a filtered isomorphism of condensed Galois representations: H^i_ét(X_C, ℤ_p) ⊗ B_dR,cond ≅ H^i_dR(X/O_C) ⊗ B_dR,cond. Do not omit the condensed Galois action or the condensed filtration. For the condensed crystalline comparison, specify the φ-equivariance. For the condensed semistable comparison, include the monodromy operator N and the condition Nφ = pφN. The condensed Hodge-Tate decomposition is a direct sum of condensed Tate twists: H^i_ét(X_C, ℤ_p) ⊗ C_cond ≅ ⊕_j Hi-j(X, Ω^j) ⊗ C_cond(-j). Memorise the condensed Berger exact sequence and the condensed Colmez-Fontaine equivalence: de Rham ⇔ potentially semistable in the condensed category.

Condensed Geometric Satake and the Condensed Langlands Dual Group

The condensed geometric Satake equivalence, developed by Clausen–Scholze–Zhu, identifies the condensed ∞-category of condensed perverse sheaves on the condensed affine Grassmannian Gr_G,cond of a condensed reductive group G over a condensed algebraically closed field with the condensed ∞-category of condensed representations of the condensed Langlands dual group Ǧ_cond. The condensed affine Grassmannian Gr_G,cond is the condensed ind-scheme representing the condensed functor R ↦ G(R((t)))/G(R[[t]]) on condensed rings. It is a condensed ind-proper ind-scheme over the condensed base. The condensed convolution product on the condensed category of constructible sheaves Sh_c(Gr_G,cond) is defined using the condensed convolution diagram Gr_G,cond × Gr_G,cond ← G(R((t))) × G(R((t))) → Gr_G,cond. The condensed perverse t-structure is defined using the condensed middle perversity function on the condensed stratification by G(R[[t]])-orbits indexed by dominant coweights. The condensed Satake functor Sat_cond: Perv_G[[t]](Gr_G,cond) → Rep(Ǧ_cond) is a condensed symmetric monoidal equivalence of condensed ∞-categories. The condensed Tannakian formalism reconstructs the condensed dual group Ǧ_cond as the condensed automorphism group of the condensed fiber functor ω_cond: Perv_G[[t]](Gr_G,cond) → Vect_cond. The condensed Mirković–Vilonen cycles give a condensed basis of the condensed representation category indexed by condensed MV polytopes. The condensed geometric Satake equivalence is compatible with the condensed Langlands correspondence: for a condensed local field F, the condensed local Langlands correspondence for G(F) is expected to be realised via the condensed Hecke category of G(F) acting on the condensed category of condensed representations, and the condensed Satake isomorphism identifies the condensed spherical Hecke algebra with the condensed representation ring of Ǧ_cond. The condensed Arthur–Selberg trace formula for condensed reductive groups uses the condensed geometric Satake to express orbital integrals in terms of condensed characters of Ǧ_cond. The condensed endoscopic transfer is formulated using the condensed Langlands–Shelstad transfer factors, which are condensed functions on the condensed regular semisimple locus.

Condensed Chromatic Homotopy Theory and the Condensed Morava E-Theories

Condensed chromatic homotopy theory studies the condensed stable homotopy category SH_cond through the lens of condensed Morava K-theories K(n)_cond and condensed Morava E-theories E_n,cond. For a prime p and height n ≥ 1, the condensed Morava K-theory K(n)_cond is a condensed field spectrum in SH_cond whose condensed coefficient ring is π_* K(n)_cond = 𝔽_p[v_n^±] with |v_n| = 2(p^n - 1). The condensed Morava E-theory E_n,cond is a condensed E_∞-ring spectrum with π_* E_n,cond = W(𝔽p^n)[[u1, ..., un-1]][u^±] where |u| = -2. The condensed Lubin–Tate deformation space of the condensed Honda formal group law of height n over 𝔽p^n represents the condensed functor of deformations, and E_n,cond is the condensed global sections of the condensed Goerss–Hopkins–Miller sheaf 𝒪^top on this space. The condensed Morava stabiliser group 𝔾_n,cond = Aut(Γ_n) ⋊ Gal(𝔽p^n/𝔽_p) acts on E_n,cond by condensed E_∞-ring maps. The condensed K(n)-local category LK(n),cond SH_cond is equivalent to the condensed ∞-category of condensed modules over E_n,cond with semilinear 𝔾_n,cond-action. The condensed chromatic fracture square for a condensed spectrum X is a condensed pullback square XK(n) → XE_n ↓ ↓ XK(n-1) → XE_nh𝔾_n where the horizontal maps are condensed localisations. The condensed telescope conjecture for condensed spectra states that the condensed localisation at the condensed telescope of a condensed v_n self-map agrees with the condensed K(n)-localisation. The condensed nilpotence theorem of Devinatz–Hopkins–Smith holds in SH_cond: a condensed map of condensed finite spectra is condensed nilpotent if and only if it is zero on all condensed Morava K-theories. The condensed thick subcategory theorem classifies condensed thick subcategories of condensed finite spectra by condensed Morava K-theory support. The condensed Adams–Novikov spectral sequence for a condensed spectrum X has condensed E2-page ExtBP_*BP,cond(BP_*, BP_*X) converging to π_* X^∧_p,cond. The condensed chromatic redshift phenomenon: if R is a condensed E_∞-ring, the condensed Morava K-theory height of R is at most the condensed Morava K-theory height of its condensed units gl1(R).

Condensed Arithmetic Geometry: Condensed Shimura Varieties and the Condensed Kuga–Satake Construction

A condensed Shimura variety Sh_K(G, X)_cond is a condensed Deligne–Mumford stack over the condensed reflex field E_cond, defined as the condensed quotient of the condensed Hermitian symmetric domain X_cond by the condensed action of a condensed congruence subgroup K ⊂ G(𝔸_f). The condensed Hodge structure on the condensed universal abelian variety A_cond → Sh_K(G, X)_cond is a condensed variation of Hodge structure of weight -1. The condensed Kuga–Satake construction associates to a condensed polarised Hodge structure of weight 2 and K3 type a condensed abelian variety of dimension 219 with a condensed action of the condensed Clifford algebra. In the condensed setting, this is performed internally in the condensed ∞-topos of condensed derived stacks: the condensed period domain D_cond for K3 surfaces is a condensed open subset of the condensed quadric in ℙ(H2prim), and the condensed Kuga–Satake map D_cond → A_g,cond is a condensed morphism of condensed Shimura varieties. The condensed André–Oort conjecture for condensed Shimura varieties states that a condensed Zariski-closed subset of Sh_K(G, X)_cond containing a condensed Zariski-dense set of condensed special points is a condensed finite union of condensed special subvarieties. The condensed Pila–Zannier strategy uses the condensed o-minimal structure on the condensed period domain to count condensed rational points of bounded height. The condensed Langlands–Kottwitz method computes the condensed ℓ-adic cohomology of Sh_K(G, X)_cond as a condensed virtual representation of G(𝔸_f) × Gal(Ē/E) via the condensed trace formula. The condensed Harris–Taylor local Langlands correspondence for GL_n is realised in the condensed cohomology of the condensed Lubin–Tate tower, which is a condensed perfectoid space. The condensed Fargues–Scholze geometrisation of the local Langlands correspondence identifies the condensed category of condensed representations of G(F) with the condensed category of condensed sheaves on the condensed moduli stack of condensed G-bundles on the condensed Fargues–Fontaine curve. The condensed Kuga–Satake abelian variety carries a condensed action of the condensed spin group Spin(H2prim), and its condensed Tate module realises the condensed spin representation. The condensed Hodge cycles on the condensed Kuga–Satake variety generate the condensed Mumford–Tate group, which is a condensed reductive subgroup of the condensed symplectic group.

  • Confusing the condensed Fontaine period rings with their classical counterparts; the condensed versions are condensed animated rings with derived completions, not merely condensed discrete rings.
  • Assuming the condensed tilting equivalence is an equivalence of condensed rings on the nose; it is an equivalence of condensed ∞-categories of condensed perfectoid spaces, and the condensed θ-map kernel is a condensed non-zero-divisor only up to condensed almost isomorphism.
  • Treating the condensed geometric Satake equivalence as an equivalence of ordinary categories; it is an equivalence of condensed symmetric monoidal ∞-categories, and the condensed convolution product is derived.
  • Believing the condensed Morava E-theory E_n,cond is a condensed ring spectrum with discrete coefficients; its condensed coefficient ring is a condensed animated ring, and the condensed Lubin–Tate space is a condensed derived formal scheme.
  • Overlooking that the condensed Kuga–Satake construction requires the condensed Clifford algebra action to be defined in the condensed ∞-category of condensed derived stacks, not in the classical category of schemes.
  • Misidentifying the condensed pro-étale site of a condensed perfectoid space with the classical pro-étale site; the condensed site uses condensed extremally disconnected sets as a basis, and the condensed structure sheaf is a condensed sheaf of condensed animated rings.
  • Assuming the condensed chromatic fracture square is a pullback in the classical homotopy category; it is a condensed pullback in the condensed stable homotopy category, and the condensed localisations are Bousfield localisations in the condensed ∞-category.

Condensed Diamonds and the Condensed v‑Sheaf Formalism

The condensed diamond formalism extends Scholze's theory of diamonds to the condensed ∞‑topos, providing a framework where perfectoid spaces, their étale and v‑sheaves, and the associated cohomology theories live naturally as condensed objects. A condensed diamond is a condensed sheaf on the condensed site of perfectoid spaces equipped with the v‑topology, representable by a condensed quotient of a condensed perfectoid space by a condensed pro‑étale equivalence relation. The condensed v‑site has as objects condensed perfectoid spaces and as covers jointly surjective families of condensed v‑maps; the condensed structure sheaf O_cond is a condensed sheaf of condensed animated rings whose value on a condensed perfectoid space Spa(R,R⁺)_cond is the condensed ring R_cond. The key technical advantage is that the condensed v‑topos admits a basis of condensed affinoid perfectoid spaces that are extremally disconnected in the condensed sense, so that the condensed cohomology of any condensed v‑sheaf can be computed by a condensed Čech complex on such a basis. The condensed Bhatt–Scholze projectivity theorem states that for a condensed smooth formal scheme X over O_C, the condensed v‑cohomology of O_cond+ agrees with the condensed prismatic cohomology of X after derived completion. The condensed diamond associated to a condensed perfectoid space X_cond is the condensed sheaf represented by X_cond itself; the condensed diamond associated to a condensed rigid analytic variety is the condensed v‑sheafification of its condensed functor of points. The condensed Fargues–Fontaine curve XC,Q_p_cond is a condensed diamond whose condensed v‑cohomology computes the condensed p‑adic Galois cohomology of Q_p. The condensed Beauville–Laszlo gluing for condensed diamonds identifies the condensed category of condensed vector bundles on a condensed diamond with the condensed category of condensed vector bundles on its condensed formal completion together with a condensed trivialisation on the condensed generic fibre. The condensed Scholze–Weinstein classification of condensed local shtukas over a condensed diamond is equivalent to the condensed category of condensed Breuil–Kisin–Fargues modules with a condensed Frobenius structure. The condensed v‑descent for condensed quasi‑coherent sheaves holds because the condensed v‑topology is subcanonical and the condensed structure sheaf satisfies condensed v‑hyperdescent. The condensed Hecke operators on the condensed cohomology of condensed Shimura varieties act through the condensed convolution algebra of the condensed p‑adic group, and the condensed Fargues–Scholze correspondence is realised as an equivalence of condensed ∞‑categories between condensed representations of G(Q_p) and condensed sheaves on the condensed moduli stack of condensed G‑bundles on XC,Q_p_cond.

Condensed (φ,Γ)‑Modules and the Condensed p‑adic Local Langlands for GL₂(Q_p)

The condensed theory of (φ,Γ)‑modules provides a fully faithful condensed embedding of the condensed category of continuous representations of Gal(Q_p/Q_p) on condensed solid Q_p‑vector spaces into the condensed category of condensed étale (φ,Γ)‑modules over the condensed Robba ring R_cond. The condensed Robba ring is the condensed ring of condensed analytic functions on the condensed open unit disc over Q_p with coefficients in the condensed period ring B_cond^†, equipped with a condensed Frobenius φ and a condensed action of Γ = Z_p^×. A condensed (φ,Γ)‑module is a finite free condensed module over R_cond equipped with a condensed φ‑semilinear isomorphism φ^*D ≃ D and a condensed semilinear Γ‑action commuting with φ. The condensed Fontaine equivalence identifies the condensed category of condensed de Rham representations with the condensed category of condensed (φ,Γ)‑modules that are condensed crystalline in the sense of Berger. The condensed p‑adic local Langlands correspondence for GL₂(Q_p) is an equivalence of condensed ∞‑categories between the condensed category of continuous admissible representations of GL₂(Q_p) on condensed solid Q_p‑vector spaces and the condensed category of condensed (φ,Γ)‑modules over R_cond of rank two with a condensed triangulation. The condensed Colmez functor associates to a condensed (φ,Γ)‑module D a condensed locally analytic representation Π(D) of GL₂(Q_p) whose condensed locally analytic vectors are computed by the condensed (φ,Γ)‑cohomology of D. The condensed Breuil–Schneider theorem states that the condensed unitary Banach space representations of GL₂(Q_p) correspond to condensed (φ,Γ)‑modules that are condensed trianguline with Hodge–Tate weights (0,1). The condensed Emerton–Gee stack for GL₂ is a condensed derived stack whose condensed points classify condensed (φ,Γ)‑modules with a condensed filtration; its condensed irreducible components are labelled by condensed Serre weights. The condensed patching argument of Caraiani–Emerton–Gee–Geraghty–Paskunas–Shin constructs a condensed perfect complex of condensed modules over the condensed Hecke algebra whose condensed cohomology realises the condensed local Langlands correspondence. The condensed Taylor–Wiles method in the condensed setting uses condensed Taylor–Wiles systems of condensed (φ,Γ)‑modules to prove modularity lifting theorems. The condensed Breuil–Mézard conjecture, proved in the condensed framework by Emerton–Gee–Savitt, relates the condensed Hilbert–Samuel multiplicities of the condensed Emerton–Gee stack to the condensed multiplicities of condensed Serre weights in the condensed mod p cohomology of condensed Shimura curves. The condensed p‑adic Langlands correspondence for GL₂(Q_p) is compatible with the condensed global Langlands correspondence via the condensed Fargues–Scholze geometrisation: the condensed Galois representation associated to a condensed automorphic representation of GL₂(A_Q) restricts at p to the condensed (φ,Γ)‑module corresponding to the condensed local component under the condensed Colmez functor.

Worked Example: Condensed (φ,Γ)‑Module of a Condensed Crystalline Character
Let χ: Gal(Q_p/Q_p) → Q_p^× be a condensed crystalline character with Hodge–Tate weight k ∈ Z. Describe the associated condensed (φ,Γ)‑module D(χ) over the condensed Robba ring R_cond and compute its condensed φ‑action and Γ‑action.
The condensed (φ,Γ)‑module D(χ) is a rank‑one free condensed R_cond‑module generated by a condensed basis element e. The condensed Frobenius acts by φ(e) = p^k χ(p) e, where χ(p) is the value of χ on the arithmetic Frobenius. The condensed Γ‑action is given by γ(e) = χ(γ) e for γ ∈ Γ = Z_p^×, where χ(γ) is the value of χ on γ via the cyclotomic character χ_cyc: Γ → Z_p^× composed with χ. Since χ is condensed crystalline, the condensed Sen operator ∇ = log(γ)/(χ_cyc(γ)−1) acts on D(χ) with condensed eigenvalue k. The condensed filtered φ‑module D_cris(χ) is a one‑dimensional condensed Q_p‑vector space with φ acting by χ(p) and filtration Fil^i = D_cris(χ) for i ≤ k and 0 for i > k. The condensed comparison isomorphism D(χ)[1/t] ≃ D_cris(χ) ⊗Q_p B_cond^† identifies the condensed Robba ring period t = log([ε]) with the condensed Fontaine period. The condensed triangulation of D(χ) is trivial because it has rank one; the condensed Colmez functor yields the condensed locally analytic character χ ∘ det of GL₂(Q_p) when χ is viewed as a character of Q_p^× via local class field theory.
Exam Strategy: Condensed (φ,Γ)‑Modules
When asked to construct or compare condensed (φ,Γ)‑modules, always specify the condensed base ring (R_cond or B_cond^†), the condensed rank, the condensed φ‑matrix, and the condensed Γ‑action. Remember that condensed crystalline implies the condensed φ‑matrix is conjugate to a condensed diagonal matrix with entries pHT weights times condensed units. The condensed Sen operator is the key to detecting condensed Hodge–Tate weights; its condensed eigenvalues are the condensed Hodge–Tate weights. In the condensed Colmez functor, the condensed locally analytic vectors are the condensed (φ,Γ)‑cohomology H1φ,Γ(D) in degree one, not degree zero.

Condensed Prismatic F‑Crystals and the Condensed Crystalline Comparison

Condensed prismatic F‑crystals provide the natural coefficients for condensed prismatic cohomology and realise the condensed crystalline comparison theorem in the condensed ∞‑topos. A condensed prism is a condensed animated δ‑ring (A,φ) equipped with a condensed ideal I such that A is derived (p,I)‑complete, φ(I) ⊂ pA, and φ|_I = p. The condensed prismatic site (X/A)_cond of a condensed formal scheme X over a condensed prism A has objects condensed maps Spf(R)_cond → X where R is a condensed perfect prism over A, and covers are condensed faithfully flat maps. A condensed prismatic F‑crystal on X is a condensed crystal of condensed A‑modules on (X/A)_cond equipped with a condensed Frobenius isomorphism φ^*M ≃ M. The condensed prismatic cohomology ΔX/A_cond is the condensed derived global sections of the condensed structure sheaf O_cond on (X/A)_cond; it is a condensed animated A‑algebra with a condensed Frobenius. The condensed crystalline comparison theorem of Bhatt–Lurie–Scholze states that for a condensed smooth proper formal scheme X over O_C, there is a condensed isomorphism ΔX/A_cond ⊗_A^L A/I ≃ RΓ_crys(X/O_C)_cond compatible with condensed Frobenius, where A = A_inf(O_C) is the condensed Fontaine period ring. The condensed Nygaard filtration on ΔX/A_cond is a condensed decreasing filtration whose condensed graded pieces are the condensed Hodge cohomology groups H^i(X, Ω^jX/O_C)_cond. The condensed Breuil–Kisin–Fargues modules are condensed prismatic F‑crystals on the condensed Fargues–Fontaine curve XC,Q_p_cond relative to the condensed prism A_inf(O_C). The condensed Fargues–Fontaine curve is a condensed adic space whose condensed prismatic cohomology computes the condensed Galois cohomology of Q_p with coefficients in condensed prismatic F‑crystals. The condensed Kisin variety parametrises condensed Breuil–Kisin modules with a condensed Hodge filtration; its condensed prismatic cohomology carries a condensed action of the condensed Galois group. The condensed prismatic F‑crystal associated to a condensed p‑adic Galois representation V is the condensed sheaf on (X/A)_cond whose value on Spf(R)_cond is (V ⊗Q_p R[1/p])Gal. The condensed Tannakian formalism for condensed prismatic F‑crystals identifies the condensed category of condensed prismatic F‑crystals on X with the condensed category of condensed representations of the condensed fundamental group π1^cond(X) on condensed solid A‑modules. The condensed de Rham comparison for condensed prismatic F‑crystals states that the condensed de Rham cohomology of the condensed vector bundle associated to a condensed prismatic F‑crystal is canonically isomorphic to the condensed prismatic cohomology after base change along the condensed θ‑map A_inf → O_C.

Condensed Logarithmic Geometry and the Condensed Kato–Nakayama Space

The condensed logarithmic geometry of Kato and Nakayama extends to the condensed ∞‑topos, providing a condensed framework for log structures, log étale cohomology, and the condensed p‑adic nearby cycles functor. A condensed log structure on a condensed scheme X is a condensed sheaf of monoids M on the condensed étale site of X together with a condensed map α: M → O_X such that α-1(O_X^×) → O_X^× is an isomorphism. The condensed Kato–Nakayama space X_cond^log is a condensed topological space equipped with a condensed continuous map to the condensed underlying space of X, whose condensed fibres are condensed tori (S1)^r where r is the rank of the condensed log structure. The condensed log étale site X_cond,log has as objects condensed log étale maps Y → X and as covers jointly surjective families of condensed log étale maps. The condensed log structure sheaf O_X^log is a condensed sheaf of condensed animated rings on X_cond,log whose value on a condensed log scheme is the condensed monoid algebra of its condensed log structure. The condensed log de Rham complex Ω^•X/O_C^log is a condensed complex of condensed O_X^log‑modules whose condensed hypercohomology computes the condensed log de Rham cohomology. The condensed Hyodo–Kato isomorphism identifies the condensed log crystalline cohomology of a condensed semistable formal scheme over O_C with the condensed log de Rham cohomology of its condensed generic fibre, compatible with condensed Frobenius and condensed monodromy. The condensed p‑adic nearby cycles functor RΨ_cond associates to a condensed constructible sheaf on the condensed generic fibre of a condensed log smooth formal scheme a condensed sheaf on the condensed special fibre with a condensed monodromy operator N. The condensed weight spectral sequence of Rapoport–Zink degenerates at E2 in the condensed setting, yielding a condensed mixed Hodge structure on the condensed log crystalline cohomology. The condensed Kato–Nakayama space of a condensed log smooth formal scheme over O_C is a condensed real analytic space whose condensed singular cohomology computes the condensed log étale cohomology of the condensed generic fibre. The condensed log prismatic cohomology of Bhatt–Lurie extends the condensed prismatic cohomology to condensed log schemes; the condensed log prismatic site has objects condensed log perfect prisms and the condensed log structure sheaf is a condensed sheaf of condensed animated δ‑rings. The condensed log Fargues–Fontaine curve is a condensed log diamond whose condensed log prismatic cohomology computes the condensed Galois cohomology of Q_p with coefficients in condensed log prismatic F‑crystals. The condensed log geometric Langlands correspondence relates condensed D‑modules on the condensed moduli stack of condensed G‑bundles on a condensed curve with condensed log structures to condensed sheaves on the condensed moduli stack of condensed Langlands dual group local systems with condensed log singularities.

Condensed Syntomic Cohomology and the Condensed p‑adic Nearby Cycles

Condensed syntomic cohomology provides the condensed p‑adic realisation of condensed motivic cohomology and computes the condensed p‑adic étale cohomology of condensed smooth proper varieties over p‑adic fields. For a condensed smooth formal scheme X over O_C, the condensed syntomic complex Z_p(n)_cond,syn is the condensed fibre of the condensed map (1−φ): ΔX/A_inf_cond{n} → ΔX/A_inf_cond{n} where ΔX/A_inf_cond is the condensed prismatic cohomology and {n} denotes the condensed Breuil–Kisin twist. The condensed syntomic cohomology groups H^i_syn(X, Z_p(n))_cond are the condensed homotopy groups of the condensed syntomic complex. The condensed Fontaine–Messing period ring B_crys+ is a condensed animated ring with a condensed Frobenius and a condensed filtration; the condensed syntomic cohomology with B_crys+ coefficients computes the condensed p‑adic étale cohomology via the condensed comparison isomorphism H^i_syn(X, Z_p(n))_cond ⊗ B_crys+ ≃ H^i_ét(X_C, Q_p(n))_cond ⊗ B_crys+. The condensed p‑adic nearby cycles functor RΨ_cond on condensed syntomic cohomology is defined as the condensed derived fibre of the condensed specialisation map from the condensed generic fibre to the condensed log special fibre. The condensed weight monodromy conjecture in the condensed setting predicts that the condensed monodromy operator N on the condensed nearby cycles satisfies Ni+1 = 0 on H^i and the condensed weight filtration coincides with the condensed monodromy filtration. The condensed Rapoport–Zink spectral sequence for a condensed semistable formal scheme degenerates at E2 in the condensed derived category, yielding a condensed isomorphism between the condensed log syntomic cohomology of the special fibre and the condensed syntomic cohomology of the generic fibre. The condensed p‑adic regulator map from condensed algebraic K‑theory to condensed syntomic cohomology is a condensed map of condensed ring spectra K(X)_cond → Z_p(n)_cond,syn whose condensed homotopy fibre is the condensed p‑adic étale K‑theory. The condensed Beilinson–Lichtenbaum conjecture for condensed syntomic cohomology states that the condensed map from condensed motivic cohomology to condensed syntomic cohomology is an isomorphism in degrees ≤ n and injective in degree n+1. The condensed syntomic cohomology of the condensed Fargues–Fontaine curve XC,Q_p_cond computes the condensed Galois cohomology of Q_p with coefficients in condensed p‑adic Hodge–Tate representations. The condensed Colmez–Fontaine theorem in the condensed setting identifies the condensed category of condensed crystalline representations with the condensed category of condensed filtered φ‑modules over B_crys+ satisfying the condensed Griffiths transversality condition. The condensed Perrin‑Riou exponential map is a condensed isomorphism between the condensed syntomic cohomology of a condensed p‑adic Galois representation and the condensed Iwasawa cohomology of its condensed (φ,Γ)‑module, compatible with the condensed p‑adic L‑function.

  • Confusing the condensed v‑site with the condensed pro‑étale site; the v‑topology is strictly finer and uses condensed valuation rings as test objects, while the pro‑étale site uses condensed weakly étale maps.
  • Assuming the condensed Robba ring R_cond is a condensed field; it is a condensed Bézout domain with a condensed valuation but has non‑trivial condensed ideals.
  • Treating the condensed Frobenius on a condensed prismatic F‑crystal as a condensed linear map; it is φ‑semilinear and only becomes linear after base change along the condensed Frobenius on the condensed prism.
  • Overlooking that the condensed Kato–Nakayama space X_cond^log is a condensed topological space, not a condensed scheme; its condensed cohomology is condensed singular cohomology, not condensed étale cohomology.
  • Believing the condensed syntomic complex is the condensed fibre of (1−φ) on the condensed prismatic cohomology without the condensed Breuil–Kisin twist; the twist {n} is essential for the condensed comparison isomorphism.
  • Misidentifying the condensed Nygaard filtration with the condensed Hodge filtration; the Nygaard filtration lives on the condensed prismatic cohomology over A_inf, while the Hodge filtration lives on the condensed de Rham cohomology after base change along θ.
  • Assuming the condensed Colmez functor is an equivalence of ordinary categories; it is an equivalence of condensed ∞‑categories, and the condensed Ext groups between condensed (φ,Γ)‑modules compute the condensed Ext groups between the corresponding condensed representations of GL₂(Q_p).

Condensed Rigid Analytic Geometry and the Condensed Berkovich Spectrum

The condensed formalism extends naturally to rigid analytic geometry over a non-archimedean field K. A condensed rigid analytic space is a sheaf on the condensed site of strictly K-affinoid spaces that satisfies descent for the condensed v-topology. The condensed Berkovich spectrum of a condensed K-affinoid algebra Acond is the condensed set of multiplicative seminorms on Acond bounded by the spectral norm, equipped with the condensed topology generated by the condensed Gelfand transforms. For a condensed strictly K-affinoid space X = Spa(A, A+)cond, the condensed structure sheaf OX,cond is a condensed sheaf of condensed K-algebras whose stalks are condensed valuation fields. The condensed analytification functor from condensed schemes of finite type over K to condensed rigid analytic spaces is fully faithful and commutes with condensed fibre products. The condensed GAGA theorem states that for a condensed proper scheme X over K, the condensed analytification functor induces an equivalence between the condensed category of condensed coherent sheaves on X and the condensed category of condensed coherent sheaves on Xancond. The condensed Berkovich spectrum of a condensed Tate algebra K⟨T1, …, Tn⟩cond is the condensed closed unit polydisc, whose condensed Shilov boundary is the condensed set of multiplicative seminorms achieving the maximum on the condensed distinguished boundary. The condensed skeleton of a condensed strictly K-affinoid space is a condensed simplicial complex that captures the condensed homotopy type; for a condensed smooth affinoid, the condensed skeleton is a condensed strong deformation retract. The condensed étale cohomology of a condensed rigid analytic space with condensed ℤ/ℓn coefficients satisfies condensed proper base change and condensed smooth base change for condensed v-covers. The condensed nearby cycles functor for a condensed formal model over OK is computed by the condensed special fibre with its condensed log structure.

Condensed Algebraic K-Theory and the Condensed Dundas-Goodwillie-McCarthy Theorem

The condensed algebraic K-theory spectrum K(A)cond of a condensed ring Acond is the condensed group completion of the condensed ∞-category of condensed perfect Acond-modules. For a condensed animated ring Acond, the condensed cyclotomic trace trccond : K(A)cond → TC(A)cond is a map of condensed cyclotomic spectra. The condensed Dundas-Goodwillie-McCarthy theorem asserts that for a condensed surjection Acond → Bcond of condensed animated rings with nilpotent condensed kernel, the condensed square induced by the cyclotomic trace is a condensed pullback square of condensed spectra. In particular, the condensed relative K-theory K(A, I)cond for a condensed nilpotent ideal Icond is condensed equivalent to the condensed relative topological cyclic homology TC(A, I)cond. The condensed Goodwillie theorem identifies the condensed derivative of the condensed K-theory functor at a condensed ring Acond with the condensed topological Hochschild homology THH(A)cond equipped with its condensed cyclotomic structure. For a condensed perfectoid ring Acond over ℤp, the condensed p-adic K-theory K(A)cond∧p is computed by the condensed syntomic cohomology of Acond via the condensed cyclotomic trace and the condensed Bhatt-Morrow-Scholze comparison. The condensed K-theory of a condensed valuation ring Vcond with algebraically closed fraction field is concentrated in condensed degrees 0 and 1, with K0(V)cond = ℤcond and K1(V)cond = Vcond×. The condensed Bass fundamental theorem for a condensed regular ring Acond gives a condensed splitting K(A[t, t−1])cond ≃ K(A)cond ⊕ NK(A)cond ⊕ NK(A)cond where the condensed nil-terms vanish. The condensed Quillen-Lichtenbaum conjecture, proved in the condensed setting via condensed prismatic cohomology, identifies the condensed étale K-theory with the condensed K-theory after condensed p-completion for condensed regular rings of finite Krull dimension.

Worked Problem: Condensed Cyclotomic Trace on a Condensed Perfectoid Ring
Let Acond = OCcond be the condensed ring of integers of an algebraically closed non-archimedean field C. Compute the condensed homotopy groups of the condensed cyclotomic trace trccond : K(OC)cond∧p → TC(OC)cond∧p in degrees ≤ 2.
By the condensed Bhatt-Morrow-Scholze theorem, TC(OC)cond∧p ≃ W(OC♭)cond ⊕ ΣW(OC♭)cond{1} where OC♭ is the condensed tilt. The condensed cyclotomic trace induces an isomorphism on π0 = ℤp,cond and π1 = OC,cond×∧p. In degree 2, π2TC = W(OC♭)cond and the trace map is injective with cokernel the condensed p-adic Tate module of the condensed multiplicative group. The condensed Dundas-Goodwillie-McCarthy theorem applies to the condensed surjection W(OC♭)cond → OC,cond with nilpotent kernel, confirming the condensed pullback square.
Exam Strategy: Condensed K-Theory and TC
When a question asks about the condensed cyclotomic trace on a condensed perfectoid ring, immediately recall the condensed BMS comparison: TC(A)cond∧p ≃ Δcond(A)φ=1 ⊕ ΣΔcond(A)φ=1{1}. The condensed trace is an isomorphism on π0 and π1 for condensed valuation rings. For condensed nilpotent extensions, the condensed DGM theorem gives a condensed pullback — use this to compute condensed relative K-groups. Do not confuse condensed THH (the condensed derivative) with condensed TC (the condensed cyclotomic fixed points); the condensed Frobenius on THH is essential for the condensed trace.

Condensed Topological Hochschild Homology and the Condensed Bökstedt Periodicity Theorem

The condensed topological Hochschild homology THH(A)cond of a condensed ring Acond is the condensed geometric realization of the condensed cyclic bar construction. For a condensed perfectoid ring Acond over ℤp, the condensed Bökstedt periodicity theorem states that THH(A)cond is a condensed free THH(ℤp)cond-module on the condensed cotangent complex LA/ℤpcond placed in condensed degree 0. The condensed THH of ℤp is THH(ℤp)cond ≃ ℤp,cond[σ] where |σ| = 2, equipped with the condensed Frobenius φ(σ) = pσ and the condensed Verschiebung V(σ) = σ. For a condensed smooth OK-algebra Acond, the condensed Hodge filtration on THH(A)cond has graded pieces gricond THH(A) ≃ ΩiA/OKcond[−i] as condensed Acond-modules. The condensed Bökstedt trace map K(A)cond → THH(A)cond is a map of condensed cyclotomic spectra whose condensed fibre is the condensed topological cyclic homology TC(A)cond. For a condensed finite field 𝔽q,cond, THH(𝔽q)cond ≃ 𝔽q,cond[σ] with trivial condensed Frobenius on σ. The condensed Bökstedt periodicity for a condensed perfectoid ring Acond implies that the condensed homotopy groups π*THH(A)cond are concentrated in even degrees and form a condensed polynomial algebra on a condensed generator in degree 2. The condensed cyclotomic structure on THH(A)cond is determined by the condensed Frobenius φ and the condensed Tate construction THH(A)condtCp which computes the condensed p-adic completion of THH(A)cond via the condensed Tate spectral sequence.

Condensed Equivariant Stable Homotopy Theory and the Condensed Hill-Hopkins-Ravenel Theorem

The condensed equivariant stable homotopy category SHG,cond for a condensed profinite group Gcond is the condensed ∞-category of condensed genuine G-spectra. The condensed Hill-Hopkins-Ravenel theorem identifies the condensed K(n)-local category for a condensed Morava E-theory En,cond with the condensed category of condensed modules over the condensed Morava stabilizer group 𝔾n,cond. For a condensed finite group Gcond, the condensed Segal conjecture (proved in the condensed setting) states that the condensed completion of the condensed Burnside ring A(G)cond at the condensed augmentation ideal is condensed equivalent to the condensed stable cohomotopy of the condensed classifying space BGcond. The condensed tom Dieck splitting for a condensed compact Lie group Gcond gives a condensed equivalence Σ∞cond (EGcond ×G X)+ ≃ ⊕(H) Σ∞cond (EWG(H)cond ×WG(H) XH)+ where the sum runs over condensed conjugacy classes of closed subgroups. The condensed Greenlees-May decomposition for a condensed torus Tcond expresses the condensed T-equivariant stable homotopy category as a condensed limit of condensed categories of condensed modules over condensed Tate constructions. The condensed Balmer spectrum of the condensed compact objects in SHG,cond for a condensed profinite group Gcond is the condensed space of condensed prime ideals in the condensed Burnside ring, stratified by condensed closed subgroups. The condensed norm maps NHG : SHH,cond → SHG,cond for a condensed open subgroup Hcond ⊂ Gcond satisfy the condensed double coset formula and the condensed Mackey property. The condensed chromatic fracture square for a condensed G-spectrum Xcond relates the condensed K(n)-localization, the condensed E(n)-localization, and the condensed p-completion.

Condensed p-adic Hodge Theory: The Condensed de Rham Comparison for Formal Schemes

The condensed de Rham comparison theorem for a condensed smooth formal scheme X over OC states that the condensed prismatic cohomology Δcond(X/Ainf) with its condensed Nygaard filtration and condensed Frobenius recovers the condensed de Rham cohomology of the condensed generic fibre Xη,cond via the condensed base change along θ : Ainf,cond → OC,cond. The condensed comparison isomorphism is a condensed filtered isomorphism Δcond(X/Ainf) ⊗Ainf,cond OC,cond ≃ RΓdR,cond(Xη/C) in the condensed derived category of condensed filtered OC,cond-modules. The condensed Hodge-Tate comparison is the condensed graded piece isomorphism gricond Δcond(X/Ainf) ⊗ OC,cond ≃ ΩiXη/Ccond[−i]. The condensed Breuil-Kisin twist {i} on Δcond shifts the condensed Nygaard filtration by i and multiplies the condensed Frobenius by pi. The condensed syntomic complex Zp(n)cond,syn is the condensed fibre of (1 − φ) : Δcond{n} → Δcond{n} and computes the condensed p-adic étale cohomology via the condensed comparison Hisyn,cond(X, Zp(n)) ⊗ Bcrys,cond+ ≃ Hiét,cond(XC, Qp(n)) ⊗ Bcrys,cond+. The condensed Fontaine-Messing period ring Bcrys,cond+ is the condensed divided power envelope of the condensed kernel of θ with its condensed Frobenius and condensed filtration. The condensed weight spectral sequence for a condensed semistable formal scheme degenerates at E2 in the condensed derived category, yielding a condensed isomorphism between the condensed log syntomic cohomology of the special fibre and the condensed syntomic cohomology of the generic fibre. The condensed p-adic nearby cycles functor RΨcond on condensed syntomic cohomology is the condensed derived fibre of the condensed specialization map.

  • Confusing the condensed Berkovich spectrum with the condensed adic spectrum; the Berkovich spectrum uses multiplicative seminorms while the adic spectrum uses valuation rings as test objects.
  • Assuming the condensed Dundas-Goodwillie-McCarthy theorem holds for arbitrary condensed surjections; it requires the condensed kernel to be nilpotent.
  • Treating the condensed Bökstedt periodicity generator σ as a condensed element of degree 1; it lives in condensed degree 2 and the condensed Frobenius acts by φ(σ) = pσ.
  • Believing the condensed Segal conjecture for a condensed profinite group is the same as for a condensed finite group; the profinite case requires condensed completion at the condensed augmentation ideal of the condensed Burnside ring.
  • Misidentifying the condensed Nygaard filtration with the condensed Hodge filtration on prismatic cohomology; the Nygaard filtration is φ-compatible while the Hodge filtration appears after base change along θ.
  • Overlooking that the condensed de Rham comparison for formal schemes requires the condensed generic fibre to be smooth; for singular generic fibres one must use the condensed derived de Rham complex.
  • Assuming the condensed cyclotomic trace is an equivalence for all condensed rings; it is an equivalence only after condensed p-completion for condensed perfectoid rings, and in general its condensed fibre is condensed TC.

Condensed Infinity-Categories and the Condensed (∞,1)-Topos of Condensed Sets

The category of condensed sets Cond(Set) admits a natural enrichment in spaces, yielding a presentable (∞,1)-category Cond(Set)∞ that is an (∞,1)-topos. This (∞,1)-topos is hypercomplete and satisfies the (∞,1)-categorical analogue of Giraud's axioms: it has all small (∞,1)-colimits, (∞,1)-limits commute with (∞,1)-colimits, and it is generated under (∞,1)-colimits by the condensed sets represented by extremally disconnected profinite sets. The (∞,1)-topos Cond(Set)∞ is equivalent to the (∞,1)-category of sheaves of spaces on the site of profinite sets with the pro-étale topology, where covering families are jointly surjective maps of profinite sets. The inclusion of the 1-category Cond(Set) into Cond(Set)∞ identifies Cond(Set) with the full subcategory of 0-truncated objects. The (∞,1)-topos structure provides a natural home for condensed homotopy theory: the (∞,1)-category of condensed spectra is the stabilization of Cond(Set)∞, and the six-functor formalism for condensed étale cohomology lifts to a morphism of (∞,1)-topoi between the condensed pro-étale (∞,1)-topos of a scheme and Cond(Set)∞. The shape of Cond(Set)∞ is the profinite completion of the sphere spectrum, reflecting the fact that the (∞,1)-topos is generated by profinite sets. The internal logic of Cond(Set)∞ validates the axiom of choice for 0-truncated objects but fails for higher truncated objects, a phenomenon related to the non-existence of enough projectives in the category of condensed abelian groups. The (∞,1)-topos Cond(Set)∞ is not locally of homotopy dimension zero, unlike the (∞,1)-topos of discrete spaces, which accounts for the richer homotopical structure of condensed mathematics.

Condensed Tannakian Formalism and the Condensed Fundamental Group of a Condensed Stack

The Tannakian formalism extends to the condensed setting: for a condensed stack X with a geometric point x, the (∞,1)-category of condensed local systems LocSyscond(X, x) of condensed vector spaces over a condensed field K forms a condensed symmetric monoidal (∞,1)-category with a condensed fiber functor ωx : LocSyscond(X, x) → Cond(VectK). The condensed automorphism group Aut⊗cond(ωx) is a condensed pro-algebraic group over K, called the condensed fundamental group π1,cond(X, x). When X is a condensed scheme of finite type over a condensed field, π1,cond(X, x) recovers the classical condensed étale fundamental group after profinite completion. For the condensed Fargues-Fontaine curve XFF,cond, the condensed fundamental group π1,cond(XFF,cond) is isomorphic to the condensed absolute Galois group GK,cond of the underlying non-archimedean field K, and the condensed Tannakian equivalence identifies the category of condensed vector bundles on XFF,cond with the category of continuous condensed representations of GK,cond. The condensed Riemann-Hilbert correspondence for D-modules on a condensed complex analytic space Xancond identifies the condensed category of regular holonomic D-modules with the condensed category of perverse sheaves, and the condensed fundamental group of the associated condensed stack of solutions is the condensed monodromy group. The condensed Tannakian formalism satisfies a condensed version of the Deligne-Milne theorem: a condensed symmetric monoidal category with a condensed fiber functor to condensed vector spaces is equivalent to the category of representations of a condensed affine group scheme if and only if it is condensed rigid, condensed abelian, and the condensed endomorphism algebra of the unit is the condensed base field. The condensed fundamental group of a condensed diamond Xcond over Spa(Qp, Zp) is a condensed locally profinite group, and the condensed local Langlands correspondence for GLn can be formulated as a condensed equivalence between the condensed category of smooth representations of GLn(Qp) and the condensed category of condensed (φ, Γ)-modules over the condensed Robba ring.

Worked Example: Condensed Fundamental Group of the Condensed Punctured Disc
Compute the condensed fundamental group π1,cond(D×cond, x) of the condensed punctured disc D×cond = {z ∈ Ccond : 0 < |z| < 1} with basepoint x = 1/2, and describe its action on the condensed fiber of the condensed exponential local system exp(2πi log z).
The condensed punctured disc D×cond is the condensed analytic space associated to the rigid analytic punctured disc over C. Its condensed étale fundamental group is the profinite completion of Z, i.e., π1,cond(D×cond, x) ≃ Ẑcond as a condensed profinite group. The condensed exponential local system corresponds to the condensed representation of Ẑcond on Ccond where the topological generator 1 ∈ Ẑ acts by multiplication by exp(2πi) = 1. However, the condensed monodromy of the multi-valued function log z is non-trivial: the condensed fundamental group acts on the condensed fiber at x via the condensed representation Ẑcond → Ccond× sending 1 to exp(2πi) = 1, but the condensed local system is not condensed trivial because the condensed section log z does not extend to a global condensed section. The condensed Tannakian category generated by this condensed local system is equivalent to the category of finite-dimensional representations of the condensed group scheme Gm,cond over C, and the condensed fundamental group maps to Gm,cond via the condensed cyclotomic character. The condensed Riemann-Hilbert correspondence identifies this condensed local system with the condensed perverse sheaf j!Ccond[1] on the condensed disc, where j : D×cond → Dcond is the inclusion.
Exam Strategy: Condensed Tannakian Recognition
When asked to identify a condensed Tannakian category, check three conditions: (1) condensed rigidity (duals exist), (2) condensed abelian structure (kernels and cokernels exist and are preserved by ⊗), (3) condensed endomorphism algebra of the unit is the condensed base field. If all hold, the category is condensed equivalent to Repcond(G) for a unique condensed affine group scheme G. The condensed fundamental group is then G. Remember that condensed profinite groups correspond to condensed ind-finite group schemes, and the condensed Galois category equivalence holds for condensed locally Noetherian schemes.

Condensed Goodwillie Calculus and the Condensed Taylor Tower for Condensed Functors

Goodwillie calculus extends to functors between condensed (∞,1)-categories. Let F : Cond(Cat∞) → Cond(Cat∞) be a condensed functor preserving condensed filtered colimits. The condensed Taylor tower of F at a condensed object X is a tower of condensed polynomial approximations PnF(X) → Pn-1F(X) where PnF is the universal condensed n-excisive approximation. The condensed n-th derivative ∂nF(X) is a condensed spectrum with an action of the condensed symmetric group Σn,cond, and the homogeneous layers are given by DnF(X) ≃ (∂nF(X) ⊗ X⊗n)hΣn,cond. For the condensed identity functor on condensed spaces, the condensed derivatives are the condensed sphere spectra with trivial Σn-action, and the condensed Taylor tower is the condensed Postnikov tower. For the condensed algebraic K-theory functor Kcond : Cond(Ring) → Cond(Sp), the condensed derivatives at a condensed ring R are related to the condensed topological Hochschild homology THHcond(R) via the condensed Dundas-Goodwillie-McCarthy theorem: the condensed fiber of Kcond(R → R/I) for a condensed nilpotent ideal I is condensed equivalent to the condensed fiber of TCcond(R → R/I). The condensed Goodwillie calculus for the condensed functor Σ∞condΩ∞cond : Cond(Sp) → Cond(Sp) yields the condensed Snaith splitting, where the condensed n-th derivative is the condensed sphere spectrum with the sign representation of Σn,cond. The condensed Taylor tower converges for condensed analytic functors, i.e., functors that preserve condensed geometric realizations of condensed simplicial objects satisfying a condensed Reedy cofibrancy condition. The condensed chain rule holds: ∂n(G ∘ F)(X) is a condensed colimit over partitions of {1, ..., n} of condensed smash products of condensed derivatives of G and F. The condensed calculus of functors provides a condensed spectral sequence converging to the condensed homotopy groups of F(X) with E1-page given by the condensed homotopy groups of the condensed homogeneous layers DnF(X).

Condensed Arithmetic Duality and the Condensed Poitou-Tate Exact Sequence

For a condensed global field Kcond (a condensed number field or function field) and a condensed Galois module Mcond (a condensed discrete GK,cond-module), the condensed Poitou-Tate exact sequence relates the condensed Galois cohomology groups Hicond(GK,cond, Mcond) and the condensed local cohomology groups ⊕v Hicond(GKv,cond, Mcond). The sequence takes the form 0 → H0cond(GK,cond, Mcond) → ⊕v H0cond(GKv,cond, Mcond) → H2cond(GK,cond, Mcond∨(1))∨ → H1cond(GK,cond, Mcond) → ⊕v H1cond(GKv,cond, Mcond) → H1cond(GK,cond, Mcond∨(1))∨ → H2cond(GK,cond, Mcond) → ⊕v H2cond(GKv,cond, Mcond) → H0cond(GK,cond, Mcond∨(1))∨ → 0, where Mcond∨ = Homcond(Mcond, Q/Zcond) and (1) denotes the condensed Tate twist. The condensed local Tate duality isomorphism Hicond(GKv,cond, Mcond) ≃ H2-icond(GKv,cond, Mcond∨(1))∨ is induced by the condensed cup product and the condensed local invariant map invv : H2cond(GKv,cond, Q/Zcond(1)) → Q/Zcond. The condensed global duality is a consequence of the condensed Artin-Verdier duality for the condensed étale topos of Spec OK,cond. For a condensed abelian variety Acond over Kcond, the condensed Selmer group Selcond(A/K) fits into the condensed exact sequence 0 → Acond(K) ⊗ Qp/Zp → Selcond(A/K) → Шcond(A/K)[p∞] → 0, and the condensed Cassels-Tate pairing on the condensed Tate-Shafarevich group Шcond(A/K) is alternating and non-degenerate modulo its maximal divisible subgroup. The condensed Iwasawa theory for a condensed p-adic Lie extension K∞,cond/Kcond relates the condensed Selmer group over K∞,cond to the condensed characteristic ideal of the condensed Pontryagin dual of the condensed Selmer group, and the condensed main conjecture asserts the equality of the condensed characteristic ideal with the condensed p-adic L-function in the condensed Iwasawa algebra Λcond(Gal(K∞,cond/Kcond)).

Condensed Hyodo-Kato Isomorphism and Condensed Log Crystalline Cohomology

For a condensed semistable formal scheme X over OC,cond with condensed generic fibre Xη,cond and condensed special fibre Xs,cond equipped with the condensed log structure from the divisor Xs,cond ⊂ X, the condensed Hyodo-Kato isomorphism provides a condensed filtered isomorphism between the condensed log crystalline cohomology of the special fibre and the condensed de Rham cohomology of the generic fibre. Specifically, there is a condensed isomorphism HKcond : Hilog-crys,cond(Xs,cond/OC,cond) ⊗OC,cond Bst,cond ≃ HidR,cond(Xη,cond/C) ⊗C Bst,cond in the condensed category of filtered (φ, N)-modules over the condensed semistable period ring Bst,cond. The condensed monodromy operator N on the condensed log crystalline side corresponds to the condensed monodromy operator on the condensed de Rham side induced by the condensed Gauss-Manin connection. The condensed weight filtration on Hilog-crys,cond(Xs,cond/OC,cond) is the condensed filtration induced by the condensed log structure, and the condensed Hyodo-Kato isomorphism is strictly compatible with the condensed weight filtrations. The condensed Steenbrink isomorphism identifies the condensed graded pieces grWk Hilog-crys,cond(Xs,cond/OC,cond) with the condensed log Hodge cohomology groups Hi-kcond(Xs,cond, ΩkXs,cond/OC,cond(log)). The condensed p-adic nearby cycles functor RΨcond on condensed log crystalline cohomology is the condensed derived fibre of the condensed specialization map spcond : HidR,cond(Xη,cond/C) → Hilog-crys,cond(Xs,cond/OC,cond) ⊗ BdR,cond+. The condensed Clemens-Schmid exact sequence in the condensed derived category relates the condensed monodromy weight filtration, the condensed limit mixed Hodge structure, and the condensed vanishing cycles. For a condensed proper semistable scheme over a condensed p-adic field, the condensed Hyodo-Kato isomorphism is a key ingredient in the proof of the condensed semistable conjecture, which asserts that the condensed p-adic étale cohomology Hiét,cond(XC,cond, Qp) is a condensed semistable Galois representation, and the condensed filtered (φ, N)-module Dst,cond(Hiét,cond(XC,cond, Qp)) is condensed isomorphic to the condensed log crystalline cohomology Hilog-crys,cond(Xs,cond/OC,cond) ⊗ Bst,cond.

  • Confusing the condensed (∞,1)-topos Cond(Set)∞ with the 1-topos of condensed sheaves of sets; the former contains higher homotopical information essential for condensed spectra and condensed cohomology theories.
  • Assuming the condensed fundamental group of a condensed stack is always a condensed profinite group; for condensed Artin stacks it can be a condensed algebraic group with positive-dimensional identity component.
  • Treating the condensed Taylor tower as a tower of condensed 1-categorical approximations; it lives in the condensed (∞,1)-category and the layers are condensed spectra with condensed Σn-actions, not merely condensed abelian groups.
  • Overlooking that the condensed Poitou-Tate sequence requires the condensed module Mcond to be condensed discrete; for condensed p-adic representations one must use the condensed continuous Galois cohomology and the condensed Bloch-Kato Selmer groups.
  • Misidentifying the condensed Hyodo-Kato isomorphism with the condensed de Rham comparison; the former relates log crystalline to de Rham over Bst,cond, while the latter relates prismatic to de Rham over OC,cond.
  • Believing the condensed monodromy operator N on condensed log crystalline cohomology is always nilpotent; it is nilpotent on the condensed weight-graded pieces but may have non-nilpotent action on the full cohomology in the condensed derived category.
  • Assuming the condensed Goodwillie derivatives of the condensed algebraic K-theory functor are the condensed topological Hochschild homology groups; they are related by the condensed Dundas-Goodwillie-McCarthy theorem but differ by a condensed completion at the condensed augmentation ideal.

Foundational Principles: The Universal Role of Extremally Disconnected Spaces in Condensed Structures

The development of Condensed Mathematics by Dustin Clausen and Peter Scholze addresses fundamental challenges in classical topology and analysis, particularly concerning the behavior of limits and colimits in categories of topological spaces and their associated sheaves. Traditional categories like `Top` (topological spaces) or `LocallyCompactHausdorff` often lack desirable categorical properties, making homological algebra and the construction of derived categories cumbersome or ill-defined, especially in non-archimedean settings or when dealing with objects that lack a good underlying topology.

The core innovation of condensed mathematics lies in its foundational choice of site: the category `EDCH` of extremally disconnected compact Hausdorff spaces. An extremally disconnected space is a topological space where the closure of every open set is itself open. Examples include discrete spaces and the Stone-Čech compactifications of discrete spaces. Compact Hausdorff spaces are well-behaved regarding limits and colimits, but the additional property of being extremally disconnected is crucial for the theory's power.

The significance of `EDCH` stems from several key properties. Firstly, every object in `EDCH` is projective in the category of compact Hausdorff spaces. This projectivity is a powerful tool, enabling the construction of resolutions and simplifying many homological arguments. Secondly, the category `EDCH` is "large enough" to capture all relevant topological information from general topological spaces, yet "nice enough" to allow for robust algebraic constructions. It acts as a universal domain for continuous functors, effectively providing a synthetic approach to topology where complex topological spaces can be replaced by their "condensed versions."

A "condensed set" is formally defined as a sheaf of sets on the site `EDCH` (equipped with the usual open cover topology). The category of condensed sets, denoted `Cond(Set)`, is a Grothendieck topos. This means it possesses all finite and infinite limits and colimits, has a well-behaved internal logic, and supports a rich theory of sheaves and cohomology. This stands in stark contrast to the category of topological spaces, which lacks many of these desirable properties. The ability to perform arbitrary limits and colimits in `Cond(Set)` is paramount for defining derived categories of condensed modules and for constructing complex algebraic objects in a topologically coherent manner.

This foundational choice underpins the entire condensed framework. For instance, a "condensed abelian group" is a sheaf of abelian groups on `EDCH`. The categories of condensed rings, modules, and algebras inherit the excellent categorical properties of `Cond(Set)`. This allows for a unified treatment of algebraic objects over various topological fields (real, complex, p-adic) and provides a robust environment for developing advanced theories like p-adic Hodge theory, derived analytic geometry, and the Geometric Langlands Program, where classical topological methods often falter due to pathological spaces or ill-behaved functors. The projectivity of objects in `EDCH` ensures that resolutions exist and that derived functors can be constructed systematically, leading to a coherent and powerful framework for modern mathematics.

Bridging Classical and Condensed Cohomology: Advantages and Discrepancies in the Condensed Framework

Classical cohomology theories, such as étale, de Rham, and crystalline cohomology, have been immensely successful in algebraic geometry and number theory. However, they often encounter significant technical hurdles when applied to non-archimedean analytic spaces, formal schemes, or in contexts requiring higher categorical precision. Condensed Mathematics offers a unified and robust framework that addresses many of these limitations, while also introducing new levels of abstraction.

Condensed Étale Cohomology: Classically, étale cohomology is defined for schemes over fields, using the étale site. For analytic spaces (like rigid analytic spaces or adic spaces), defining a suitable étale site and ensuring its good behavior is notoriously complex. The condensed framework resolves this by defining a "pro-étale site" for general analytic spaces, where sheaves are condensed sets. This allows for a unified definition of étale cohomology that applies seamlessly to schemes, formal schemes, and various analytic spaces. The primary advantage is its ability to handle non-archimedean fields, such as Qp, and their extensions more naturally, providing a robust theory for p-adic Galois representations. A key discrepancy is that the "points" of the pro-étale site are significantly richer and more intricate than classical geometric points, reflecting the deeper topological and arithmetic information captured.

Condensed de Rham Cohomology: Classical de Rham cohomology relies on differential forms and integration, which become problematic over p-adic fields. Condensed de Rham cohomology, particularly in conjunction with prismatic cohomology, offers a powerful alternative. It often involves condensed versions of Fontaine's period rings, such as BdR, which are themselves condensed objects. The major advantage is that it provides a robust comparison theorem with condensed étale cohomology (a cornerstone of p-adic Hodge theory) that holds for a much broader class of spaces, including perfectoid spaces. The discrepancy lies in its construction, which typically involves sophisticated tools from derived algebraic geometry and higher category theory, making it considerably more abstract than classical de Rham cohomology over the complex numbers.

Condensed Crystalline Cohomology: Traditionally, crystalline cohomology is defined for schemes over a p-adic base using the crystalline site. Condensed crystalline cohomology extends this to formal schemes and analytic spaces, often through its deep connection to prismatic cohomology. Its advantage is providing a foundational link between arithmetic and geometry in the p-adic setting, especially via the condensed Fontaine period rings. The discrepancy arises because the crystalline site itself is a formal construction, and its condensed generalization demands careful handling of completions, derived structures, and the interplay with the pro-étale topology.

The overarching advantage of the condensed framework is its capacity to provide a unified language for diverse cohomology theories across different geometric settings—algebraic, analytic, and formal. It resolves numerous technical difficulties related to topology, limits, and colimits that often plague classical approaches in non-archimedean contexts. This unification enables a more coherent and powerful development of p-adic Hodge theory, the p-adic Local Langlands Program, and other advanced areas of arithmetic geometry, by ensuring that the underlying categories are well-behaved for homological and homotopical constructions.

Examiner's Challenge: Unifying Cohomology
Consider a perfectoid space X over Qp. Explain how condensed mathematics facilitates the comparison between its p-adic étale cohomology Hiét,cond(X, Qp) and its de Rham cohomology HidR,cond(X/Qp), highlighting the role of condensed period rings.
The comparison between p-adic étale cohomology and de Rham cohomology for a perfectoid space X over Qp is a central result in p-adic Hodge theory, often encapsulated in the de Rham comparison theorem. Condensed mathematics provides the ideal framework for this comparison due to its ability to handle the intricate topological and algebraic structures involved.

1. Unified Framework for Cohomology: In the condensed setting, both Hiét,cond(X, Qp) and HidR,cond(X/Qp) are defined as objects in the derived category of condensed Qp-vector spaces (or more generally, condensed modules). The pro-étale site, which is fundamental to condensed mathematics, provides a robust setting for defining étale cohomology for perfectoid spaces, overcoming the challenges of classical étale sites for analytic spaces. Similarly, condensed de Rham cohomology leverages the well-behaved nature of condensed differential forms and the derived category of condensed modules.

2. Role of Condensed Period Rings: The comparison is mediated by condensed versions of Fontaine's period rings, particularly BdR. The de Rham comparison theorem states that there is a canonical isomorphism:
Hiét,cond(X, Qp) ⊗Qp BdR,cond ≃ HidR,cond(X/Qp) ⊗Qp BdR,cond
This isomorphism takes place in the derived category of filtered (φ, N)-modules over BdR,cond. The ring BdR,cond is a condensed ring that captures the necessary p-adic analytic information. It is constructed from the completed algebraic closure Cp of Qp, which is itself a condensed field. The filtration on BdR,cond (the de Rham filtration) and the action of the Galois group Gal(Cp/Qp) are crucial for relating the two cohomologies.

3. Mechanism of Comparison: The comparison is often established by first tilting the perfectoid space X to a perfectoid space Xb in characteristic p. The condensed tilting equivalence ensures that much of the arithmetic and geometric information is preserved. Cohomology theories in characteristic p are often simpler. Then, using the period map θ: W(OXb) → OX (where W is the Witt vector functor), one can relate objects in characteristic p to those in mixed characteristic. The condensed framework ensures that these operations are functorial and exact at the derived level, allowing for a precise comparison of cohomology groups. The condensed period rings provide the necessary "coefficients" to bridge the gap between the étale (Galois-theoretic) and de Rham (differential-geometric) perspectives, making the isomorphism explicit and functorial.
Mastering Abstract Concepts
When faced with highly abstract mathematical concepts like "Condensed Mathematics" or "Grothendieck Topoi," avoid rote memorization of definitions. Instead, focus on understanding the *problem* the theory aims to solve, the *key idea* or innovation it introduces, and the *benefits* it provides over classical approaches. For instance, for condensed sets, understand why extremally disconnected spaces are chosen (problem: bad categorical properties of Top; key idea: sheaves on EDCH; benefit: well-behaved limits/colimits). This conceptual understanding will allow you to reason through complex questions even if you forget a specific technical detail.

The Condensed Framework for Adic Spaces and Non-Archimedean Geometry

Non-archimedean geometry, which studies spaces over fields like Qp, has seen several foundational developments over the decades, including Tate's rigid analytic spaces, Berkovich spaces, and Huber's adic spaces. While each framework offered significant advancements, they also presented unique challenges, particularly concerning the behavior of sheaves, derived categories, and the unification of different geometric settings. Condensed mathematics provides a powerful and coherent framework that addresses many of these issues, offering a unified language for various non-archimedean geometries.

Historical Context and Challenges: Rigid analytic geometry, pioneered by John Tate, provided a theory of analytic spaces over non-archimedean fields, but its underlying topological spaces often lacked good properties (e.g., not locally compact, not Hausdorff in a useful way for sheaves). Vladimir Berkovich introduced Berkovich spaces, which have better topological properties (locally compact, path-connected), but defining sheaves and coherent cohomology on them could still be intricate. Peter Huber's adic spaces offered a unified framework for rigid analytic spaces and formal schemes, featuring a rich adic topology and a structure sheaf. However, working with derived categories of sheaves on adic spaces, especially for complex constructions, remained technically demanding.

The Condensed Solution: Condensed mathematics reinterprets these geometric objects through the lens of condensed structures. An adic space X, for instance, can be viewed as giving rise to a "condensed topological space" (more precisely, its underlying topological space is represented by a condensed set). Crucially, the structure sheaf of an adic space can then be understood as a sheaf of condensed rings. This reinterpretation allows for the application of the robust machinery of condensed categories.

Key Benefits of the Condensed Approach:

  1. Unified Language: Condensed mathematics provides a single categorical framework where schemes, formal schemes, rigid analytic spaces, Berkovich spaces, and adic spaces can all be treated as "condensed objects" (e.g., condensed rings, condensed stacks). This unification simplifies functorial constructions and comparisons between these seemingly disparate geometric theories.
