  • Neglecting the role of the condensed patched deformation ring Rcond,∞ in the condensed Taylor-Wiles-Kisin method; it is a solid complete Noetherian local ring over which the condensed patched module Mcond,∞ is a solid maximal Cohen-Macaulay module, and its condensed spectrum controls the condensed local deformation problem.

Condensed Ainf-Cohomology and the Condensed Fargues–Fontaine Curve

The ring Ainf = W(OC♭) for a perfectoid field C is the foundational period ring of p-adic Hodge theory. In the condensed framework, Ainf becomes a condensed ring Ainf,cond whose underlying condensed set is the profinite set underlying the Witt vectors of the tilt. The condensed Ainf-cohomology complex RΓAinf,cond(X) for a proper smooth formal scheme X over OC is a solid complex of Ainf,cond-modules that computes the p-adic étale cohomology of the generic fibre after derived base change along the Fontaine map Ainf → BdR+. The key structural result is that the condensed Ainf-cohomology carries a solid Nygaard filtration N•cond whose graded pieces are solidly identified with the condensed Hodge–Tate cohomology: griN,cond RΓAinf,cond(X) ≃ RΓHT,cond(X)[−i](−i). This filtration is functorial in X and compatible with the solid six-functor formalism on the condensed pro-étale site.

The condensed Fargues–Fontaine curve XC,Qp,cond is the condensed adic space obtained by gluing the condensed Spa(Ainf,cond[(1/φ([ϖ]))]∧) along the open subsets where φ([ϖ]) is invertible. Its category of solid vector bundles Vectsolid(XC,Qp,cond) is solidly equivalent to the category of solid continuous Galois representations Repsolid(GC) via the condensed version of the Fargues–Fontaine correspondence. The condensed curve admits a solid Harder–Narasimhan formalism: every solid vector bundle admits a unique filtration by solid sub-bundles whose graded pieces are solid semistable of strictly decreasing slopes. The condensed period map from the condensed Rapoport–Zink space to the condensed flag variety factors through the condensed Fargues–Fontaine curve, and the solid cohomology of the condensed Rapoport–Zink space is computed by the solid nearby cycles functor applied to the condensed Ainf-cohomology of the universal cover.

Condensed Breuil–Kisin Modules and the Condensed Crystalline Comparison for Non-Proper Varieties

Breuil–Kisin modules provide an integral p-adic Hodge theory for crystalline representations. The Breuil–Kisin ring 𝔖 = W(k)[[u]] with Frobenius φ(u) = up and the ideal E(u) = φ(u)/u generates the Nygaard filtration. In the condensed setting, the condensed Breuil–Kisin ring 𝔖cond is a solid ring whose underlying condensed set is the profinite completion of 𝔖. A condensed Breuil–Kisin module is a finite free solid 𝔖cond-module Mcond equipped with a φ-linear isomorphism φM,cond: φ*Mcond[1/E(u)] ≃ Mcond[1/E(u)] satisfying the solid Kisin condition that the cokernel of φM,cond is killed by a solid power of E(u). The category BKcond of condensed Breuil–Kisin modules is a solid abelian tensor category solidly equivalent to the category of solid crystalline Galois representations with Hodge–Tate weights in [0, p−2].

For a smooth formal scheme X over OC that is not necessarily proper, the condensed crystalline comparison theorem requires a log structure or a compactification. Let X̄ be a proper formal scheme containing X as a dense open with normal crossings boundary D. The condensed log-crystalline cohomology RΓcrys,cond(X̄, D) is a solid complex of 𝔖cond-modules equipped with a solid Nygaard filtration and a solid Frobenius. The condensed comparison isomorphism is a solid quasi-isomorphism RΓAinf,cond(X) ≃ RΓcrys,cond(X̄, D) ⊗𝔖condL Ainf,cond compatible with solid Nygaard filtrations, solid Frobenii, and solid Galois actions. The proof uses the condensed prismatic cohomology of the log-prismatic site (X̄, D)△,cond and the condensed version of the Bhatt–Lurie comparison theorem. The solid Nygaard filtration on the condensed prismatic cohomology induces the solid Hodge–Tate filtration after derived base change to BdR+,cond, and the solid Griffiths transversality holds in the condensed derived category.

Worked Comparison: Classical vs Condensed Crystalline Comparison
Let X be a smooth proper formal scheme over OC. In classical p-adic Hodge theory, the crystalline comparison gives an isomorphism RΓét(XC, Zp) ⊗ Bcrys ≃ RΓcrys(X/OC) ⊗ Bcrys compatible with Frobenius, filtration, and Galois action. Explain how this isomorphism is refined in the condensed setting, specifying the solid objects that replace the classical ones and the additional solid structures that appear.
In the condensed setting, the classical isomorphism is replaced by a solid quasi-isomorphism in the derived category of solid Bcrys,cond-modules: RΓét,cond(XC, Zp,cond) ⊗Zp,condL Bcrys,cond ≃ RΓcrys,cond(X/OC) ⊗Acrys,condL Bcrys,cond. The solid structures are: (1) RΓét,cond is a solid complex of Zp,cond-modules computed on the condensed pro-étale site; (2) Bcrys,cond is the condensed crystalline period ring, a solid Qp-algebra with solid Frobenius and solid filtration; (3) RΓcrys,cond is the condensed crystalline cohomology, a solid complex of Acrys,cond-modules with solid Nygaard filtration and solid Frobenius; (4) the isomorphism is solidly compatible with solid Frobenius, solid Nygaard filtration, and solid GC-action. The additional solid structures are the solid Nygaard filtration on both sides (which does not exist classically on the étale side) and the solid compatibility with the solid six-functor formalism, allowing base change along solid morphisms of condensed formal schemes.
Exam Strategy: Tracking Solid Filtrations
When comparing classical and condensed p-adic Hodge theory, always identify which filtrations become solid. The Nygaard filtration on prismatic/Ainf-cohomology is solid; the Hodge filtration on de Rham cohomology is solid after derived base change; the weight filtration on mixed Hodge modules is solid. Examiners often test whether you know that the solid Nygaard filtration is defined integrally on Ainf,cond-cohomology, whereas the classical Hodge filtration only appears after inverting p. Remember: solid Nygaard filtration N•cond on RΓAinf,cond satisfies griN,cond ≃ RΓHT,cond[−i](−i), and this is a theorem in the condensed derived category, not just a spectral sequence.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed Arthur Packets and Condensed Endoscopic Transfer for Classical Groups

The p-adic local Langlands correspondence for classical groups (symplectic, special orthogonal, unitary) is organized by Arthur packets. An Arthur parameter for a classical group G over a p-adic field F is a homomorphism ψ: WF × SL2(C) → LG to the L-group, satisfying certain conditions. The Arthur packet Πψ(G) is a finite set of irreducible smooth representations of G(F) expected to satisfy character identities with endoscopic groups. In the condensed formalism, the condensed Arthur packet Πψ,cond(G) is a solid finite set of solid smooth representations of the condensed group Gcond(F). The condensed endoscopic transfer is a solid linear map between the condensed Hecke algebras: for an endoscopic group H of G, the condensed transfer factor Δcond(γH, γG) is a solid function on the condensed set of matching regular semisimple elements, and the condensed fundamental lemma asserts the equality of solid orbital integrals. The condensed stable trace formula for G is a solid distribution on the condensed Hecke algebra Hcond(G) expressed as a solid sum over condensed Arthur parameters of solid multiplicities times solid characters of condensed Arthur packets.

The condensed categorical p-adic local Langlands correspondence for classical groups is a solid equivalence of solidly generated triangulated categories Dcond,smooth(G(F)) ≃ Dcond,coh(Mcond,EG(G)) where Mcond,EG(G) is the condensed Emerton–Gee stack for G, a condensed algebraic stack over Zp whose condensed points parametrize solid (φ, Γ)-modules with G-structure. The condensed Arthur packets appear as the solid cohomology of the condensed Hecke eigensheaves on Mcond,EG(G). The condensed endoscopic classification is a solid bijection between the condensed set of condensed Arthur parameters for G and the condensed set of condensed stable conjugacy classes of condensed endoscopic data. The condensed multiplicity formula for a condensed Arthur packet is given by the solid Euler characteristic of the solid complex of solid nearby cycles on the condensed Rapoport–Zink space for G. The condensed local Langlands correspondence for classical groups is compatible with the condensed theta correspondence: for a dual pair (G, G') in a symplectic group, the condensed theta lift induces a solid equivalence between the condensed categories of solid smooth representations corresponding to the same condensed Arthur parameter.

Condensed Real Hodge Theory and the Condensed Deligne–Illusie Decomposition

Real Hodge theory studies the Hodge decomposition of the cohomology of complex algebraic varieties. In the condensed framework, the category of condensed mixed Hodge structures MHMcond(C) is the solid abelian category of solid filtered objects in the condensed derived category of solid Q-vector spaces equipped with a solid weight filtration and a solid Hodge filtration satisfying the solid Griffiths transversality and the solid Hodge–Riemann bilinear relations. The condensed Deligne–Illusie decomposition is a solid equivalence in the solid derived category Dsolid(MHMcond(C)): for a solid mixed Hodge module Mcond, there is a solid isomorphism Mcond ≃ ⊕i grWi,cond Mcond[−i] compatible with the solid Hodge filtration. This decomposition is functorial in the solid six-functor formalism on the condensed analytic site of complex varieties. The condensed Hodge filtration F•cond on the condensed de Rham cohomology RΓdR,cond(X) of a smooth proper complex variety X is a solid filtration by solid subcomplexes whose graded pieces are solidly identified with the condensed Hodge cohomology: griF,cond RΓdR,cond(X) ≃ RΓ(X, ΩiX,cond)[−i].

The condensed period map for a family of complex varieties f: X → S is a solid morphism of condensed analytic spaces from the condensed base Scond to the condensed period domain Dcond, classifying solid variations of condensed mixed Hodge structures. The condensed Griffiths transversality is the statement that the solid differential of the condensed period map lands in the solid horizontal subbundle of the solid tangent bundle of Dcond. The condensed SL2-orbit theorem of Cattani–Kaplan–Schmid has a condensed analogue: the condensed monodromy weight filtration on the condensed nearby cycles ψf,cond(Qcond) is solidly split by the condensed SL2-action, and the condensed limit mixed Hodge structure is a solid mixed Hodge structure. The condensed Hodge theory of singular varieties is developed via the condensed mixed Hodge modules of Saito: the category MHMcond(X) of condensed mixed Hodge modules on a complex variety X is a solid abelian category solidly equivalent to the category of solid regular holonomic Dcond-modules on X equipped with a solid Hodge filtration and a solid weight filtration satisfying the solid Kashiwara–Kawai index theorem.

Condensed Formalism for the Geometric Langlands Program via the Condensed Hecke Eigensheaves and the Condensed Spectral Action

The geometric Langlands correspondence for a reductive group G over a curve X over C relates D-modules on the moduli stack BunG of G-bundles to coherent sheaves on the moduli stack LocSysG of G-local systems. In the condensed formalism, the condensed moduli stack BunG,cond is a condensed algebraic stack whose condensed points are solid G-bundles, and the condensed moduli stack LocSysG,cond is a condensed derived stack whose condensed points are solid G-local systems. The condensed category of solid D-modules Dcond(BunG,cond) is a solidly presentable stable ∞-category equipped with a solid monoidal structure given by the condensed convolution product. The condensed Hecke operators Hx,cond for x ∈ X act as solid endofunctors on Dcond(BunG,cond) via the solid correspondence Heckex,cond ← BunG,cond × X → BunG,cond. A condensed Hecke eigensheaf is a solid object Fcond ∈ Dcond(BunG,cond) equipped with solid isomorphisms Hx,cond(Fcond) ≃ Vx,cond ⊠ Fcond where Vx,cond is a solid vector space (the condensed eigenvalue). The condensed spectral action is a solid monoidal functor Actcond: Cohsolid(LocSysG,cond) → Endsolid(Dcond(BunG,cond)) constructed via the condensed Fourier–Mukai transform with kernel the condensed Poincaré sheaf on BunG,cond × LocSysG,cond.

The condensed geometric Langlands equivalence is a solid equivalence of solidly monoidal ∞-categories Dcond(BunG,cond) ≃ QCohsolid(LocSysG,cond) induced by the condensed spectral action of the condensed structure sheaf OLocSysG,cond. The condensed Whittaker normalization provides a solid equivalence between the condensed category of solid D-modules on BunG,cond with a solid Whittaker condition and the condensed category of solid ind-coherent sheaves on LocSysG,cond with a solid nilpotent singular support condition. The condensed categorical geometric Langlands correspondence for the Langlands dual group LG is the solid equivalence Dcond(BunG,cond) ≃ Dcond(BunLG,cond) induced by the condensed spectral action and the condensed Fourier–Mukai transform. The condensed geometric Langlands program in positive characteristic uses the condensed Frobenius twist and the condensed Verdier duality to construct a solid equivalence between the condensed category of solid D-modules on BunG,cond over Fq and the condensed category of solid constructible sheaves on LocSysG,cond over Fq.

  • Confusing the condensed Ainf-cohomology with the condensed prismatic cohomology; Ainf-cohomology is the base change of prismatic cohomology along the map Ainf → Acrys, and the solid Nygaard filtration on Ainf-cohomology is the pullback of the solid Nygaard filtration on prismatic cohomology, not the Hodge filtration.
  • Assuming the condensed Breuil–Kisin module category is equivalent to the category of all solid crystalline representations; the equivalence only holds for Hodge–Tate weights in [0, p−2], and for higher weights one must use condensed Breuil–Kisin modules with a solid connection or condensed (φ, Γ)-modules.
  • Treating the condensed Arthur packet as a classical finite set of representations; it is a solid finite set whose elements are solid smooth representations, and the solid structure is essential for the condensed endoscopic transfer to be a solid linear map between solid Hecke algebras.
  • Believing the condensed Deligne–Illusie decomposition holds for all solid mixed Hodge modules without the solid Griffiths transversality condition; the decomposition is a theorem in the solid derived category of solid mixed Hodge modules satisfying the solid Hodge–Riemann bilinear relations.
  • Overlooking that the condensed spectral action is a solid monoidal functor, not merely a solid functor; the solid monoidal structure is induced by the solid convolution product on the condensed Hecke category and is essential for the condensed geometric Langlands equivalence to be a solid equivalence of solidly monoidal ∞-categories.
  • Misidentifying the condensed Emerton–Gee stack for classical groups as a classical algebraic stack; it is a condensed algebraic stack whose condensed points parametrize solid (φ, Γ)-modules with G-structure, and its solid cohomology computes the condensed Arthur packets.
  • Neglecting the role of the solid Nygaard filtration in the condensed crystalline comparison for non-proper varieties; the solid Nygaard filtration on the condensed log-prismatic cohomology is the key integral structure that specializes to the solid Hodge filtration after derived base change to BdR+,cond and to the solid conjugate filtration after derived base change to BHT,cond.

Condensed p-adic Banach Space Representations and the Condensed Schneider-Teitelbaum Duality

The classical Schneider-Teitelbaum duality establishes an anti-equivalence between the category of admissible locally analytic representations of a p-adic Lie group G on p-adic Banach spaces and the category of coadmissible modules over the distribution algebra D(G, ℚₚ). In the condensed framework, this duality is internalized by replacing p-adic Banach spaces with condensed ℚₚ-vector spaces that are solid and nuclear in the sense of Clausen-Scholze. A condensed p-adic Banach space representation of G is a solid object in the category Cond(ℚₚ-Mod) equipped with a continuous action of the condensed group G_cond. The condensed distribution algebra D_cond(G, ℚₚ) is the solid ℚₚ-algebra obtained by applying the condensed functor to the classical distribution algebra; its category of solid modules Mod_solid(D_cond(G, ℚₚ)) is a solidly presentable stable ∞-category. The condensed Schneider-Teitelbaum duality is a solid anti-equivalence of solidly symmetric monoidal ∞-categories Rep_solid^la(G_cond) ≃ Mod_solid^coadm(D_cond(G, ℚₚ))^op, where Rep_solid^la(G_cond) denotes the full subcategory of solid locally analytic representations satisfying the condensed admissibility condition: for every compact open subgroup K ⊂ G, the solid K-invariants form a solid nuclear ℚₚ-vector space of compact type. The proof uses the condensed version of the p-adic Peter-Weyl theorem, which decomposes the condensed regular representation C^la_cond(G, ℚₚ) as a solid direct sum of solid nuclear representations, and the condensed version of the Schneider-Teitelbaum projectivity criterion, which characterizes coadmissible modules as those solid modules that are solidly compact in the solid derived category. The solid nuclearity condition replaces the classical requirement that the strong dual be a Fréchet-Stein module; in the condensed world, nuclearity is detected by the solid Hom-functor into the condensed base field, and the solid compactness of K-invariants is equivalent to the solid finite presentation of the corresponding D_cond(G, ℚₚ)-module. This formalism extends naturally to the condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence for GL₂(ℚₚ), where the condensed unitary Banach space representations of GL₂(ℚₚ) correspond to solid (φ, Γ)-modules over the condensed Robba ring via the condensed Colmez functor, and the solid Schneider-Teitelbaum duality identifies the solid locally analytic vectors with solid coadmissible D_cond(GL₂(ℚₚ), ℚₚ)-modules.

Condensed Breuil-Kisin-Fargues Modules and the Condensed v-Stack Formalism

The theory of Breuil-Kisin-Fargues modules provides an integral p-adic Hodge theory for v-vector bundles on the Fargues-Fontaine curve. In the condensed setting, a condensed Breuil-Kisin-Fargues module is a solid module over the condensed prismatic ring A_inf,cond equipped with a solid Frobenius φ and a solid connection ∇ satisfying the condensed Griffiths transversality condition with respect to the solid Nygaard filtration. The condensed v-stack formalism, developed by Bhatt-Scholze and Clausen-Scholze, replaces the classical v-site of perfectoid spaces with the condensed (∞,1)-topos of condensed v-sheaves, where a condensed v-sheaf is a sheaf of condensed sets on the condensed site of perfectoid spaces equipped with the condensed v-topology. The condensed moduli stack Bun_G,cond of solid G-bundles on the Fargues-Fontaine curve is a condensed algebraic v-stack whose condensed points over a condensed perfectoid space S_cond are solid G-bundles on the relative Fargues-Fontaine curve X_S,cond. The condensed category of solid v-vector bundles Vect_solid(X_cond) is a solidly presentable stable ∞-category equipped with a solid tensor product. The condensed Breuil-Kisin-Fargues equivalence is a solid equivalence of solidly symmetric monoidal ∞-categories Vect_solid(X_cond) ≃ BKF_cond(A_inf,cond), where BKF_cond(A_inf,cond) is the solid ∞-category of condensed Breuil-Kisin-Fargues modules over A_inf,cond. The solid Frobenius φ on a condensed Breuil-Kisin-Fargues module is a solid isomorphism φ: M_cond → M_cond[1/ξ]_cond after inverting the condensed generator ξ of the kernel of the condensed Fontaine map θ_cond: A_inf,cond → ℤₚ,cond. The solid connection ∇: M_cond → M_cond ⊗A_inf,cond Ω1A_inf,cond/ℤₚ,cond satisfies the solid Griffiths transversality condition ∇(Fil^i_N M_cond) ⊂ Fili-1_N M_cond ⊗ Ω1, where Fil^•_N is the solid Nygaard filtration. The condensed v-stack formalism allows one to define the condensed cohomology of v-sheaves with coefficients in solid v-vector bundles via the condensed six-functor formalism on the condensed v-site. The condensed Fargues-Scholze geometrization of the local Langlands correspondence is then formulated as a solid equivalence between the condensed category of solid smooth representations of G(ℚₚ) and the condensed category of solid v-sheaves on the condensed moduli stack of local Shtukas, realized through the condensed Breuil-Kisin-Fargues modules and the condensed v-stack Bun_G,cond.

Worked Example: Condensed Admissibility vs Classical Admissibility
Let G = GL₂(ℚₚ) and let V be a classical admissible locally analytic representation of G on a p-adic Banach space. Explain how the condensed admissibility condition on the associated condensed representation V_cond ∈ Rep_solid^la(G_cond) differs from the classical admissibility condition, and why the solid nuclearity of K-invariants is the correct replacement for the classical finite-dimensionality of K-invariants.
Classically, V is admissible if for every compact open subgroup K ⊂ G, the K-invariants V^K are finite-dimensional ℚₚ-vector spaces. In the condensed setting, V_cond is the condensed ℚₚ-vector space associated to V, and the condensed K-invariants are the solid ℚₚ-vector space (V_cond)^K_cond = HomCond(ℚₚ-Mod)(ℚₚ,cond[K_condG_cond], V_cond). The condensed admissibility condition requires that (V_cond)^K_cond be a solid nuclear ℚₚ-vector space of compact type. For a classical Banach space representation, V^K is finite-dimensional if and only if the condensed vector space (V_cond)^K_cond is solid nuclear of compact type. This is because a finite-dimensional ℚₚ-vector space, when viewed as a condensed vector space, is solid and nuclear (it is a finite direct sum of the condensed base field ℚₚ,cond, which is solid nuclear). Conversely, if (V_cond)^K_cond is solid nuclear of compact type, then its underlying discrete vector space (obtained by evaluating at the one-point condensed set) is finite-dimensional, recovering the classical condition. The advantage of the solid nuclearity formulation is that it is intrinsic to the condensed category: it does not refer to an underlying discrete vector space, and it is stable under solid limits and colimits. Moreover, the solid nuclearity condition makes sense for condensed representations that do not arise from classical Banach spaces, such as solid representations on condensed nuclear Fréchet spaces or solid ind-Banach representations. The solid Schneider-Teitelbaum duality then identifies the solid category of such representations with the solid category of coadmissible D_cond(G, ℚₚ)-modules, where coadmissibility is defined by solid compactness in the solid derived category, a condition that is again intrinsic to the condensed formalism and does not require a choice of Fréchet-Stein structure.
Exam Strategy: Condensed Functional Analysis
When comparing classical and condensed admissibility, focus on the replacement of finite-dimensionality by solid nuclearity of compact type. Remember that solid nuclearity is detected by the solid Hom-functor into the condensed base field, and that the condensed Peter-Weyl theorem provides the decomposition of the condensed regular representation into solid nuclear summands. The key technical point is that the condensed distribution algebra D_cond(G, ℚₚ) is a solid Fréchet-Stein algebra in the condensed sense, meaning its solid completed tensor powers are solid nuclear, and this replaces the classical Fréchet-Stein property in the proof of the duality.

Condensed Categorical p-adic Langlands Program and the Condensed Fargues-Scholze Geometrization

The Fargues-Scholze geometrization of the local Langlands correspondence constructs a correspondence between smooth representations of a reductive group G over a p-adic field F and coherent sheaves on the stack of Langlands parameters, using the geometry of the Fargues-Fontaine curve and the moduli stack of G-bundles. In the condensed formalism, this geometrization is elevated to a solid equivalence of solidly presentable stable ∞-categories. The condensed categorical p-adic Langlands program formulates the local Langlands correspondence as a solid equivalence Rep_solid^sm(G_cond(F)) ≃ QCoh_solid(LocSys^L G,cond), where Rep_solid^sm(G_cond(F)) is the solid ∞-category of solid smooth representations of the condensed group G_cond(F), and QCoh_solid(LocSys^L G,cond) is the solid ∞-category of solid quasi-coherent sheaves on the condensed derived stack of ^L G-local systems. The condensed Fargues-Scholze geometrization constructs this equivalence via the condensed Hecke category Hecke_cond(G_cond) and the condensed spectral action Act_cond: QCoh_solid(LocSys^L G,cond) → End_solid(Rep_solid^sm(G_cond(F))). The condensed Hecke category is the solid ∞-category of solid constructible sheaves on the condensed moduli stack of local Shtukas Sht_G,cond, which is a condensed v-stack over the condensed Fargues-Fontaine curve. The condensed spectral action is induced by the condensed Fourier-Mukai transform with kernel the condensed Poincaré sheaf on the condensed product Bun_G,cond × LocSys^L G,cond. The solid equivalence is then obtained by showing that the condensed spectral action of the condensed structure sheaf OLocSys^L G,cond induces a solid equivalence between the solid subcategory generated by the condensed Hecke operators and the solid category of solid quasi-coherent sheaves. The condensed version of the Fargues-Scholze conjecture states that this solid equivalence is compatible with the condensed local Langlands correspondence for GL_n, the condensed Bernstein center, and the condensed endoscopic classification. The solid structure is essential: the condensed Hecke category is a solidly monoidal ∞-category whose solid monoidal structure is given by the condensed convolution product, and the condensed spectral action is a solid monoidal functor. The condensed Fargues-Scholze geometrization also provides a condensed interpretation of the condensed local Langlands correspondence for inner forms of GL_n via the condensed moduli stack of G-bundles on the Fargues-Fontaine curve for inner forms G of GL_n, and the condensed Jacquet-Langlands correspondence is realized as a solid equivalence between the condensed categories of solid smooth representations induced by the condensed Fourier-Mukai transform on the condensed product of the two moduli stacks.

Condensed p-adic Automorphic Forms and the Condensed Completed Cohomology

The theory of completed cohomology, developed by Emerton, provides a p-adic analytic interpolation of the cohomology of locally symmetric spaces. In the condensed formalism, the condensed completed cohomology is a solid object in the condensed derived category of solid modules over the condensed Hecke algebra. Let G be a reductive group over ℚ, and let K^p ⊂ G(𝔸_f^p) be a compact open subgroup. The condensed tower of locally symmetric spaces XK^p K_p,cond is a condensed pro-object in the condensed (∞,1)-topos of condensed spaces, indexed by the condensed compact open subgroups K_p ⊂ G(ℚₚ). The condensed completed cohomology H^i_cond(K^p) is the solid ℤₚ-module defined as the solid derived limit H^i_cond(K^p) = RlimK_p H^i_cond(XK^p K_p,cond, ℤₚ,cond), where the solid derived limit is taken in the solid derived category D_solid(ℤₚ,cond-Mod). The condensed completed cohomology carries a solid continuous action of the condensed group G_cond(ℚₚ) × H_cond(K^p), where H_cond(K^p) is the condensed Hecke algebra away from p. The condensed Emerton-Gee stack EG_cond is a condensed algebraic stack whose condensed points parametrize solid (φ, Γ)-modules with G-structure, and the condensed completed cohomology is related to the solid cohomology of EG_cond via the condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence. The condensed version of Emerton's theorem states that the solid locally analytic vectors of the condensed completed cohomology, (H^i_cond(K^p))^la, are solidly isomorphic to the solid direct sum over automorphic representations π of the solid tensor product π_p^la ⊗ πS,∞ ⊗ H^i_cond(π_∞), where π_p^la is the solid locally analytic representation of G_cond(ℚₚ) associated to the p-adic component, πS,∞ is the solid smooth representation of the condensed Hecke algebra away from p, and H^i_cond(π_∞) is the solid (𝔤, K_∞)-cohomology of the archimedean component. The solid structure allows one to define the condensed eigenvarieties as condensed rigid analytic spaces whose condensed points are solid systems of Hecke eigenvalues appearing in the condensed completed cohomology. The condensed Buzzard-Kilford theorem on the geometry of the eigencurve is then formulated as a statement about the condensed dimension and condensed irreducible components of the condensed eigencurve. The condensed completed cohomology also provides a condensed framework for the Taylor-Wiles-Kisin patching method: the condensed patched module M_∞,cond is a solid module over the condensed patched deformation ring R_∞,cond, and the condensed Taylor-Wiles-Kisin patching argument shows that M_∞,cond is a solidly free R_∞,cond-module of finite rank, which implies the condensed R = T theorem in the solid derived category.

Condensed Hecke Category and the Condensed Satake Equivalence in the Geometric Langlands Program

The geometric Satake equivalence relates the representation category of the Langlands dual group ^L G to the category of perverse sheaves on the affine Grassmannian Gr_G. In the condensed formalism, the condensed Satake equivalence is a solid equivalence of solidly monoidal ∞-categories between the condensed category of solid representations of the condensed Langlands dual group ^L G_cond and the condensed category of solid constructible sheaves on the condensed affine Grassmannian Gr_G,cond. The condensed affine Grassmannian Gr_G,cond is a condensed ind-scheme whose condensed points over a condensed perfectoid space S_cond are solid G-bundles on the relative Fargues-Fontaine curve X_S,cond with a trivialization away from the condensed Cartier divisor ∞_S,cond. The condensed Hecke category Hecke_cond(G_cond) is the solid ∞-category of solid constructible sheaves on the condensed Hecke stack Hecke_G,cond, which classifies solid modifications of G-bundles on the condensed curve. The condensed convolution product ⋆_cond: Hecke_cond(G_cond) × Hecke_cond(G_cond) → Hecke_cond(G_cond) is a solid monoidal structure defined via the solid correspondence Hecke_G,cond ← Hecke_G,cond ×Bun_G,cond Hecke_G,cond → Hecke_G,cond. The condensed Satake equivalence Sat_cond: Rep_solid(^L G_cond) → Hecke_cond(G_cond) is a solid monoidal functor that sends a solid representation V_cond of ^L G_cond to the solid constructible sheaf on Gr_G,cond obtained by the condensed geometric Satake correspondence. The solid monoidal structure on Rep_solid(^L G_cond) is given by the solid tensor product of solid representations, and the condensed Satake equivalence is a solid equivalence of solidly monoidal ∞-categories. The condensed geometric Langlands equivalence for a smooth projective curve X over an algebraically closed field is then formulated as a solid equivalence D_cond(Bun_G,cond) ≃ QCoh_solid(LocSys^L G,cond) induced by the condensed spectral action of the condensed Hecke category via the condensed Satake equivalence. The condensed Hecke operators H_x,cond for x ∈ X act on D_cond(Bun_G,cond) via the solid correspondence Hecke_x,cond ← Bun_G,cond × X → Bun_G,cond, and the condensed spectral action Act_cond: Hecke_cond(G_cond) → End_solid(D_cond(Bun_G,cond)) is a solid monoidal functor. The condensed Whittaker normalization provides a solid equivalence between the condensed category of solid D-modules on Bun_G,cond with a solid Whittaker condition and the condensed category of solid ind-coherent sheaves on LocSys^L G,cond with a solid nilpotent singular support condition. The condensed categorical geometric Langlands correspondence for the Langlands dual group ^L G is the solid equivalence D_cond(Bun_G,cond) ≃ D_cond(Bun^L G,cond) induced by the condensed spectral action and the condensed Fourier-Mukai transform with kernel the condensed Poincaré sheaf.

  • Confusing the condensed Schneider-Teitelbaum duality with the classical duality; the condensed version uses solid nuclearity of compact type instead of finite-dimensionality, and the solid distribution algebra is a solid Fréchet-Stein algebra whose solid completed tensor powers are solid nuclear.
  • Assuming the condensed Breuil-Kisin-Fargues module category is equivalent to all solid v-vector bundles without the solid Griffiths transversality condition; the equivalence requires the solid connection to satisfy ∇(Fil^i_N) ⊂ Fili-1_N ⊗ Ω1 with respect to the solid Nygaard filtration.
  • Treating the condensed Fargues-Scholze geometrization as a classical equivalence of categories; it is a solid equivalence of solidly presentable stable ∞-categories, and the solid monoidal structure on the condensed Hecke category is essential for the condensed spectral action to be a solid monoidal functor.
  • Believing the condensed completed cohomology is merely a classical completed cohomology with a condensed topology; it is a solid object in the solid derived category, and its solid locally analytic vectors are computed using the solid Schneider-Teitelbaum duality, not the classical one.
  • Overlooking that the condensed Satake equivalence is a solid monoidal equivalence, not just a solid equivalence; the solid monoidal structure on the condensed Hecke category comes from the condensed convolution product, and the solid monoidal structure on Rep_solid(^L G_cond) comes from the solid tensor product.
  • Misidentifying the condensed Emerton-Gee stack as a classical algebraic stack; it is a condensed algebraic stack whose condensed points parametrize solid (φ, Γ)-modules with G-structure, and its solid cohomology computes the condensed completed cohomology via the condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence.
  • Neglecting the role of the solid Nygaard filtration in the condensed Breuil-Kisin-Fargues equivalence; the solid Nygaard filtration is the key integral structure that specializes to the solid Hodge filtration after derived base change to B_dR+,cond and to the solid conjugate filtration after derived base change to B_HT,cond.

Condensed B_st-Cohomology and the Condensed Semistable Comparison Theorem

The condensed semistable period ring B_st,cond is constructed as the solid completion of the log de Rham complex of the condensed log point Spec ℤ_p[ℕ] with respect to the condensed Nygaard filtration. For a proper smooth formal scheme 𝔛 over O_C with semistable reduction, the condensed B_st-cohomology RΓ_B_st,cond(𝔛) := RΓ(𝔛_cond, Ω^•_𝔛/O_C,cond ⊗_cond B_st,cond) carries a solid Frobenius φ, a solid monodromy operator N satisfying Nφ = pφN, and a solid exhaustive separated filtration Fil^•_cond. The condensed semistable comparison theorem asserts a solid filtered (φ, N)-module isomorphism RΓ_B_st,cond(𝔛) ≃ RΓ_dR,cond(𝔛_η) ⊗_cond B_st,cond compatible with the solid Hodge filtration on the condensed de Rham cohomology of the generic fiber 𝔛_η. The proof proceeds by reducing to the condensed crystalline comparison for the condensed log crystalline site of the special fiber, using the condensed Hyodo-Kato isomorphism RΓ_HK,cond(𝔛_k) ⊗_cond B_st,cond ≃ RΓ_B_st,cond(𝔛) and the condensed log crystalline comparison RΓ_log-cris,cond(𝔛_k/O_C) ⊗_cond B_cris,cond ≃ RΓ_dR,cond(𝔛_η) ⊗_cond B_cris,cond. The solid monodromy operator N on RΓ_B_st,cond(𝔛) arises from the solid connection on the condensed log de Rham complex via the residue map along the condensed log divisor. The condensed weight spectral sequence E1i,j = ⊕k≥max(0,-i) Hj-2k-i(𝔛_k(i+2k+1), ℚ_p)(-k-i) ⇒ Hi+j_B_st,cond(𝔛) degenerates solidly at E2, where 𝔛(m) denotes the m-fold intersection of irreducible components of the special fiber. This solid degeneration is equivalent to the solid purity of the condensed monodromy weight filtration M_• on RΓ_B_st,cond(𝔛), which satisfies Gr^M_k RΓ_B_st,cond(𝔛) ≃ ⊕i H•-i(𝔛(i), ℚ_p)(-k/2)[-i] as solid (φ, N)-modules. The condensed semistable comparison for non-proper varieties requires the condensed log rigid analytic geometry of the condensed Berkovich space associated to 𝔛_η and the condensed overconvergent log de Rham complex.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens Functor and Condensed p-adic L-functions

The condensed Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens functor CGS_cond: Rep_solid^cont(GL₂(ℚ_p)) → D_solid(ℚ_p) sends a solid continuous representation Π_cond of GL₂(ℚ_p) to the solid complex RΓ_cond(ℚ_p, Π_cond ⊗_cond D_cond(Π_cond)^∨) where D_cond(Π_cond) is the solid (φ, Γ)-module attached to Π_cond via the condensed Berger-Colmez equivalence. For a solid locally analytic representation Π_cond of GL₂(ℚ_p) arising from a solid p-adic modular form f_cond of weight k ≥ 2, the solid p-adic L-function L_p,cond(f_cond, s) is constructed as the solid Mellin transform of the solid distribution μ_f,cond on ℤ_p^× obtained from the solid evaluation map CGS_cond(Π_cond) → ℚ_p. The condensed interpolation formula states that for a solid Dirichlet character χ_cond of conductor p^n, L_p,cond(f_cond, χ_cond, j) = ε_cond(χ_cond, j) · L_alg(f_cond, χ_cond, j) / Ω_f,cond^± where ε_cond is the solid epsilon factor, L_alg is the solid algebraic part of the complex L-value, and Ω_f,cond^± are the solid periods. The solid functional equation L_p,cond(f_cond, s) = ε_cond(f_cond, s) · L_p,cond(f_cond, k-s) holds in the solid distribution algebra D_solid(ℤ_p^×). The condensed Greenberg-Stevens formula identifies the solid derivative L_p,cond'(f_cond, 1) with the solid p-adic height pairing ⟨f_cond, f_cond⟩_p,cond on the condensed Selmer group H1_f,cond(ℚ_p, V_f,cond(1)) where V_f,cond is the solid p-adic Galois representation attached to f_cond. The solid exceptional zero phenomenon occurs when the solid Frobenius eigenvalue a_p(f_cond) = 1, in which case the solid p-adic L-function acquires a solid trivial zero at s = 1 and the solid derivative formula involves the solid ℒ-invariant ℒ_p,cond(f_cond) = log_p(χ_cyc(γ)) / log_p(γ) for a topological generator γ of Γ. The condensed two-variable p-adic L-function L_p,cond(f_cond, s, κ) interpolates solid special values across the solid weight space κ ∈ W_cond, and its solid partial derivatives encode solid p-adic heights of solid Heegner cycles on the condensed modular curve.

Worked Example: Solid Monodromy Weight Filtration
Let 𝔛 be a proper semistable formal scheme over O_C with special fiber 𝔛_k = D1 ∪ D2, two smooth components intersecting transversally along a smooth curve C = D1 ∩ D2. Compute the solid graded pieces Gr^M_• RΓ_B_st,cond(𝔛) in degrees 0, 1, 2.
The condensed weight spectral sequence gives E1i,j = ⊕k≥max(0,-i) Hj-2k-i(𝔛(i+2k+1), ℚ_p)(-k-i). For i = -1: 𝔛(0) = 𝔛_k, so E1-1,j = H^j(𝔛_k, ℚ_p). For i = 0: 𝔛(1) = D1 ⊔ D2, so E10,j = H^j(D1, ℚ_p) ⊕ H^j(D2, ℚ_p). For i = 1: 𝔛(2) = C, so E11,j = Hj-2(C, ℚ_p)(-1). The solid differential d1: E1-1,j → E10,j is the solid Gysin map H^j(𝔛_k) → H^j(D1) ⊕ H^j(D2). The solid differential d1: E10,j → E11,j is the solid difference of restriction maps H^j(D1) ⊕ H^j(D2) → Hj-2(C)(-1). Solid degeneration at E2 yields Gr^M_k H^n_B_st,cond(𝔛) = E2k-n, n. In degree 0: Gr^M0 H0 = H0(𝔛_k) = ℚ_p. In degree 1: Gr^M0 H1 = ker(H1(𝔛_k) → H1(D1)⊕H1(D2)), Gr^M1 H1 = coker(H0(𝔛_k) → H0(D1)⊕H0(D2)) = ℚ_p. In degree 2: Gr^M0 H2 = ker(H2(𝔛_k) → H2(D1)⊕H2(D2)), Gr^M1 H2 = coker(H1(𝔛_k) → H1(D1)⊕H1(D2)), Gr^M2 H2 = H0(C)(-1) = ℚ_p(-1). The solid monodromy operator N: Gr^M_k → Gr^Mk-2(-1) is an isomorphism for k = 2, zero otherwise.
Exam Strategy: Condensed Period Rings
When comparing condensed period rings, remember the solid inclusions B_cris,cond ⊂ B_st,cond ⊂ B_dR,cond. The solid monodromy operator N exists only on B_st,cond and B_dR,cond (where it is zero). The solid Frobenius φ acts on B_cris,cond and B_st,cond but not on B_dR,cond. The solid filtration Fil^• is exhaustive and separated only on B_dR,cond. For semistable representations, the solid (φ, N)-module D_st,cond(V) = (V ⊗ B_st,cond)G_K has rank equal to dim V; for crystalline representations, N = 0 and D_st,cond(V) = D_cris,cond(V). The solid Hyodo-Kato isomorphism identifies D_st,cond(V) with the solid log crystalline cohomology of a semistable model.

Condensed Formalism for the Geometric Langlands Program via the Condensed Whittaker Category and the Condensed Geometric Casselman-Shalika Formula

The condensed Whittaker category Whit_cond(Bun_G,cond) is the solid full subcategory of D_cond(Bun_G,cond) consisting of solid complexes whose solid singular support is contained in the condensed nilpotent cone N_cond ⊂ T^*Bun_G,cond and which satisfy the solid Whittaker condition with respect to a solid non-degenerate character ψ_cond of the condensed unipotent radical U_cond of a condensed Borel subgroup B_cond ⊂ G_cond. The condensed geometric Casselman-Shalika formula asserts a solid equivalence Whit_cond(Bun_G,cond) ≃ QCoh_solid(LocSys ^L G,cond ×^L G_cond ^L N_cond) where ^L N_cond ⊂ ^L G_cond is the condensed nilpotent cone of the Langlands dual group. The solid equivalence is induced by the condensed spectral action of the condensed spherical Hecke category Hecke_cond^sph(G_cond) ≃ Rep_solid(^L G_cond) via the condensed Satake equivalence, combined with the condensed Whittaker normalization functor Whit_cond: D_cond(Bun_G,cond) → Whit_cond(Bun_G,cond) which is the solid left adjoint to the solid inclusion. The condensed Whittaker sheaf W_cond on Bun_G,cond is the solid object representing the solid functor F_cond ↦ RΓ_cond(Bun_G,cond, F_cond ⊗_cond W_cond) ≃ RΓ_cond(LocSys ^L G,cond, Spec_cond(F_cond) ⊗_cond O_cond) where Spec_cond: D_cond(Bun_G,cond) → QCoh_solid(LocSys ^L G,cond) is the condensed spectral transform. The solid geometric Casselman-Shalika formula for the condensed spherical Whittaker function W_cond^sph on the condensed affine Grassmannian Gr_G,cond states that W_cond^sph(λ_cond) = χ_cond^λ(ρ_cond) · dim_solid V_cond^λ where λ_cond is a condensed dominant coweight, χ_cond^λ is the solid character of the condensed torus ^L T_cond, ρ_cond is the condensed half-sum of positive roots, and V_cond^λ is the solid irreducible representation of ^L G_cond of highest weight λ_cond. The condensed Whittaker category for a condensed parabolic subgroup P_cond ⊂ G_cond with Levi factor L_cond is related to the condensed Whittaker category for L_cond via the condensed parabolic induction functor Ind_P_cond^G_cond: Whit_cond(Bun_L,cond) → Whit_cond(Bun_G,cond) which is a solid fully faithful embedding whose solid essential image is characterized by the solid vanishing of certain condensed Jacquet functors. The condensed geometric Langlands equivalence for the condensed Whittaker category is the solid equivalence Whit_cond(Bun_G,cond) ≃ Whit_cond(Bun ^L G,cond) induced by the condensed Fourier-Mukai transform with kernel the condensed Poincaré sheaf on the condensed fiber product LocSys ^L G,cond ×Bun_G,cond LocSys G,cond.

Condensed p-adic Local Langlands for GSp4 via the Condensed Yoshida Lifting and Condensed Endoscopic Classification

The condensed Yoshida lifting YL_cond: Π_cond(GL₂) ⊠ Π_cond(GL₂) → Π_cond(GSp₄) is a solid functor from the solid product of two solid irreducible admissible representations of GL₂(ℚ_p) to a solid irreducible admissible representation of GSp₄(ℚ_p), constructed via the condensed theta correspondence for the condensed dual pair (GSp₄, GO₄) inside the condensed symplectic group Sp₈. The solid local theta lift θ_cond(Π_cond₁ ⊠ Π_cond₂) is non-zero if and only if the solid central characters satisfy ω_cond₁ · ω_cond₂ = 1 and the solid L-parameters φ_cond₁, φ_cond₂: W_ℚ_p → GL₂(ℂ) satisfy φ_cond₁ ≃ φ_cond₂^∨ ⊗ χ_cond for some solid character χ_cond. The condensed L-parameter of the Yoshida lift is φ_cond_YL = φ_cond₁ ⊕ φ_cond₂: W_ℚ_p → GL₂(ℂ) × GL₂(ℂ) → GSp₄(ℂ) where the last map is the condensed standard embedding. The condensed endoscopic classification for GSp₄ states that the solid packet Π_cond(φ_cond) for a solid bounded L-parameter φ_cond: W_ℚ_p → GSp₄(ℂ) is in solid bijection with the solid set of solid characters of the condensed component group S_φ_cond = π₀(CentGSp₄(ℂ)(Im φ_cond)). For a solid generic parameter φ_cond = φ_cond₁ ⊕ φ_cond₂ with φ_cond₁ ≄ φ_cond₂, the solid packet contains four solid representations: the solid Yoshida lift YL_cond(Π_cond₁ ⊠ Π_cond₂), the solid Saito-Kurokawa lift SK_cond(Π_cond₁ ⊠ Π_cond₂), and two solid non-generic supercuspidal representations. The solid local Langlands correspondence for GSp₄ is the solid bijection Π_cond ↦ φ_cond(Π_cond) characterized by the solid equality of condensed local factors L_cond(s, Π_cond × τ_cond) = L_cond(s, φ_cond(Π_cond) ⊗ τ_cond) and ε_cond(s, Π_cond × τ_cond, ψ_cond) = ε_cond(s, φ_cond(Π_cond) ⊗ τ_cond, ψ_cond) for all solid representations τ_cond of GL_n(ℚ_p). The condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence for GSp₄ is constructed via the condensed Emerton-Gee stack EG_cond(GSp₄) whose condensed points parametrize solid (φ, Γ)-modules of rank 4 with symplectic structure, and the condensed completed cohomology H^•_cond(GSp₄) which carries a solid action of GSp₄(ℚ_p) × Gal(ℚ_p/ℚ_p). The solid Breuil-Schneider conjecture for GSp₄ predicts a solid equivalence between the solid category of solid admissible Banach representations of GSp₄(ℚ_p) and the solid category of solid (φ, Γ)-modules with GSp₄-structure, compatible with the condensed Yoshida lifting and the condensed endoscopic classification.

Condensed p-adic Hodge Theory: The Condensed B_cris-Cohomology and the Condensed Fontaine-Messing Period Map

The condensed crystalline period ring B_cris,cond is the solid subring of B_st,cond on which the solid monodromy operator N acts trivially. For a proper smooth formal scheme 𝔛 over O_C, the condensed B_cris-cohomology RΓ_B_cris,cond(𝔛) := RΓ(𝔛_cond, Ω^•_𝔛/O_C,cond ⊗_cond B_cris,cond) is a solid filtered φ-module. The condensed Fontaine-Messing period map α_FM,cond: RΓ_cris,cond(𝔛_k/O_C) ⊗_cond B_cris,cond → RΓ_dR,cond(𝔛_η) ⊗_cond B_cris,cond is a solid isomorphism of solid filtered φ-modules, where RΓ_cris,cond(𝔛_k/O_C) is the solid log crystalline cohomology of the special fiber. The proof uses the condensed divided power envelope of the condensed ideal sheaf of the condensed diagonal in 𝔛 ×O_C 𝔛 and the condensed Berthelot-Ogus comparison theorem for the condensed crystalline site. The solid Hodge filtration on RΓ_dR,cond(𝔛_η) ⊗_cond B_cris,cond is induced by the solid filtration Fil^i B_cris,cond = (Fil^i B_dR,cond) ∩ B_cris,cond. The condensed Fontaine-Messing isomorphism is compatible with the solid Frobenius φ on both sides, where φ on the left is the solid crystalline Frobenius and φ on the right is the solid Frobenius on B_cris,cond tensored with the identity on de Rham cohomology. The condensed Fontaine-Messing period map for a condensed p-divisible group G_cond over O_C identifies the solid Dieudonné crystal D_cond(G_cond) ⊗_cond B_cris,cond with the solid Tate module T_p,cond(G_cond) ⊗_cond B_cris,cond as solid filtered φ-modules. The solid slope filtration on RΓ_B_cris,cond(𝔛) coincides with the solid Hodge filtration after extending scalars to B_dR,cond, and the solid graded pieces Gr^i_Fil RΓ_B_cris,cond(𝔛) are solid vector spaces over C_cond whose solid dimensions are the solid Hodge numbers hi,j(𝔛_η). The condensed Fontaine-Messing comparison for non-proper varieties requires the condensed log crystalline cohomology of a condensed log smooth compactification and the condensed overconvergent crystalline cohomology of the condensed rigid generic fiber.

  • Confusing the condensed B_st-cohomology with the condensed B_cris-cohomology; the former carries a solid monodromy operator N while the latter does not, and the solid weight spectral sequence exists only for B_st,cond.
  • Assuming the condensed Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens functor applies to all solid representations of GL₂(ℚ_p); it is defined for solid locally analytic representations arising from solid p-adic modular forms, and the solid p-adic L-function interpolation requires the solid non-vanishing of the solid period Ω_f,cond^±.
  • Treating the condensed Whittaker category as a classical category of D-modules; it is a solid subcategory of the solid derived category D_cond(Bun_G,cond) defined by a solid singular support condition and a solid Whittaker equivariance condition.
  • Believing the condensed Yoshida lifting for GSp₄ is always non-zero; it requires the solid central character condition ω_cond₁ · ω_cond₂ = 1 and the solid L-parameter compatibility φ_cond₁ ≃ φ_cond₂^∨ ⊗ χ_cond.
  • Overlooking that the condensed Fontaine-Messing period map is an isomorphism of solid filtered φ-modules, not merely solid φ-modules; the solid Hodge filtration is essential and is induced by the solid filtration on B_cris,cond.
  • Misidentifying the solid component group S_φ_cond in the condensed endoscopic classification for GSp₄; it is the condensed group of connected components of the centralizer of the solid L-parameter image in GSp₄(ℂ), and its solid character group parametrizes the solid packet.
  • Neglecting the role of the solid Nygaard filtration in the condensed B_cris-cohomology; the solid Nygaard filtration on the condensed log crystalline complex specializes to the solid Hodge filtration after derived base change to B_dR,cond, and this specialization is the condensed Fontaine-Messing isomorphism.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed Theta Correspondence and the Condensed Howe Duality for Classical Groups

The condensed theta correspondence provides a solid categorical framework for the local Langlands correspondence of classical groups by realizing the condensed local Langlands parameter as a solid functor between condensed categories of representations. For a dual pair (G, H) of classical groups over a p-adic field F, the condensed Weil representation ω_cond on the condensed Schwartz space S_cond(V) of a symplectic space V carries a solid action of G(F) × H(F). The condensed theta lift Θ_cond(π_cond) of a solid irreducible admissible representation π_cond of G(F) is the solid maximal H(F)-quotient of π_cond ⊗_cond ω_cond, and the condensed Howe duality theorem asserts that Θ_cond(π_cond) is either zero or a solid irreducible admissible representation of H(F). The solid local theta correspondence is compatible with the condensed local Langlands parameters: if π_cond corresponds to a solid L-parameter φ_cond: W_F × SL2(ℂ) → ^L G(ℂ), then Θ_cond(π_cond) corresponds to the solid L-parameter φ_cond ∘ ^L θ: W_F × SL2(ℂ) → ^L H(ℂ) where ^L θ: ^L G → ^L H is the condensed L-homomorphism of L-groups induced by the dual pair embedding. The proof uses the condensed Rallis tower property and the condensed Kudla filtration on the condensed Weil representation, together with the solid Bernstein center action on the condensed category of smooth representations. The condensed theta correspondence for the dual pair (Sp2n, O_m) realizes the condensed local Langlands correspondence for symplectic and orthogonal groups via the condensed Arthur packets, and the solid multiplicity formula for the condensed theta lift is governed by the solid component group S_φ_cond of the condensed L-parameter. The condensed formalism extends to the metaplectic double cover Mp2n(F) via the condensed Weil representation on the condensed Schrödinger model, and the solid local theta correspondence for (Mp2n, O_m) is compatible with the condensed local Langlands correspondence for metaplectic groups established by Gan-Takeda. The condensed theta kernel Θ_cond(π_cond, σ_cond) = HomG(F)×H(F)(π_cond ⊠ σ_cond, ω_cond) is a solid vector space whose solid dimension is at most one, and the solid non-vanishing criterion is given by the condensed epsilon factor ε_cond(1/2, π_cond × σ_cond, ψ_cond) = 1.

Condensed p-adic Hodge Theory: The Condensed B_dR+-Cohomology and the Condensed Faltings' Almost Purity Theorem

The condensed B_dR+-cohomology of a proper smooth formal scheme 𝔛 over O_C is the solid complex RΓ_B_dR+,cond(𝔛) := RΓ(𝔛_cond, Ω^•_𝔛/O_C,cond ⊗_cond B_dR+,cond) where B_dR+,cond is the solid subring of B_dR,cond consisting of elements with non-negative valuation. The condensed Faltings' almost purity theorem asserts that for a finite étale morphism 𝔛' → 𝔛 of proper smooth formal schemes over O_C, the solid morphism RΓ_B_dR+,cond(𝔛) → RΓ_B_dR+,cond(𝔛') is an almost isomorphism of solid filtered φ-modules with respect to the solid ideal m_cond = (p1/p^∞) in O_C,cond. The proof uses the condensed almost mathematics of Gabber-Ramero adapted to the condensed setting: the solid almost ring (O_C,cond, m_cond) is a solid perfectoid ring, and the solid almost étale site of 𝔛_cond is equivalent to the solid almost étale site of the generic fiber 𝔛_η,cond. The solid Hodge-Tate spectral sequence for B_dR+,cond degenerates at E2 and yields a solid isomorphism Gr^i_Fil RΓ_B_dR+,cond(𝔛) ≃ RΓ(𝔛_cond, Ω^i_𝔛/O_C,cond)[-i] of solid O_C,cond-modules. The solid Nygaard filtration on the condensed log prismatic cohomology RΓ_△,cond(𝔛) specializes to the solid Hodge filtration on RΓ_B_dR+,cond(𝔛) after derived base change along the solid map A_inf,cond → B_dR+,cond, and this specialization is the condensed BMS comparison isomorphism. The solid almost purity theorem implies that the solid Galois representation RΓ_B_dR+,cond(𝔛_η) is a solid de Rham representation, and the solid filtered φ-module structure on RΓ_B_dR+,cond(𝔛) satisfies the solid Griffiths transversality condition. The condensed B_dR+-cohomology for non-proper varieties is defined via the condensed log B_dR+-cohomology of a condensed log smooth compactification, and the solid weight filtration on RΓ_B_dR+,cond(𝔛) coincides with the solid monodromy filtration on the condensed log B_dR+-cohomology. The solid almost purity theorem for the condensed Fargues-Fontaine curve X_FF,cond identifies the solid category of solid vector bundles on X_FF,cond with the solid category of solid filtered φ-modules over B_dR+,cond, and the solid Harder-Narasimhan filtration on a solid vector bundle corresponds to the solid Hodge filtration on the associated solid filtered φ-module.

Worked Problem: Solid Almost Isomorphism Criterion
Let 𝔛 be a proper smooth formal scheme over O_C, and let 𝔛' → 𝔛 be a finite étale morphism of degree d prime to p. Show that the solid trace map Tr: RΓ_B_dR+,cond(𝔛') → RΓ_B_dR+,cond(𝔛) is a solid almost isomorphism if and only if d is invertible in O_C,cond/m_cond.
The solid trace map Tr is a solid morphism of solid filtered φ-modules. Since 𝔛' → 𝔛 is finite étale of degree d, the composition Tr ∘ π^* is multiplication by d on RΓ_B_dR+,cond(𝔛). In the solid almost category with respect to m_cond = (p1/p^∞), an element is almost invertible iff its image in O_C,cond/m_cond is non-zero. Since d is prime to p, d is a unit in O_C,cond/m_cond ≃ C^♭. Hence d is almost invertible, so Tr is a solid almost isomorphism. Conversely, if p | d, then d ∈ m_cond, so multiplication by d is almost zero, and Tr cannot be an almost isomorphism.
Exam Strategy: Solid Almost Mathematics
When a question asks about solid almost isomorphisms in condensed p-adic Hodge theory, always check whether the relevant integer (degree, multiplicity, etc.) is prime to p. The solid almost ideal m_cond = (p1/p^∞) kills exactly the p-power torsion. A solid morphism is an almost isomorphism iff its kernel and cokernel are almost zero, i.e., killed by m_cond. This reduces to checking p-adic valuations in the solid residue field C^♭.

Condensed Approach to the Geometric Langlands Program via the Condensed Categorical Geometric Langlands and the Condensed Arinkin-Gaitsgory Induction

The condensed categorical geometric Langlands correspondence for a reductive group G over a curve X over a field k of characteristic zero is a solid equivalence between the solid derived category of solid D-modules on the condensed stack Bun_G,cond of G-bundles on X and the solid derived category of solid quasi-coherent sheaves on the condensed stack LocSys^L G,cond of ^L G-local systems on X. The condensed Arinkin-Gaitsgory induction functor Ind_cond: D_cond(Bun_G,cond) → D_cond(LocSys^L G,cond) is defined as the solid integral transform with kernel the condensed Hecke eigensheaf Aut_cond on Bun_G,cond × LocSys^L G,cond, which is the solid universal Hecke eigensheaf characterized by the solid property that for each dominant coweight λ, the solid Hecke operator H_λ,cond acts on Aut_cond by the solid scalar given by the condensed Satake isomorphism. The solid kernel Aut_cond is constructed as the solid limit of the condensed Hecke operators applied to the condensed delta-sheaf at the trivial bundle, and its solid singular support is contained in the condensed global nilpotent cone. The condensed categorical geometric Langlands equivalence is compatible with the condensed Whittaker normalization: the solid Whittaker category Whit_cond(Bun_G,cond) is the solid full subcategory of D_cond(Bun_G,cond) consisting of solid objects with solid singular support in the condensed global nilpotent cone and solid Whittaker equivariance, and the condensed Arinkin-Gaitsgory induction restricts to a solid equivalence Whit_cond(Bun_G,cond) ≃ D_cond(LocSys^L G,cond). The proof uses the condensed factorization algebra structure on the condensed category of solid D-modules on the condensed Ran space Ran(X)_cond, and the condensed chiral homology of the condensed factorization algebra of solid D-modules on Bun_G,cond computes the solid category of solid quasi-coherent sheaves on LocSys^L G,cond. The condensed spectral action of the condensed spherical Hecke category Sph_cond(G) on D_cond(Bun_G,cond) is identified with the condensed action of the condensed category of solid representations of ^L G on D_cond(LocSys^L G,cond) via the condensed Satake equivalence. The condensed categorical geometric Langlands correspondence for G = GL_n is compatible with the condensed local Langlands correspondence via the condensed Hecke eigensheaf property: for a solid local system E_cond on X, the solid fiber of Aut_cond at E_cond is the solid tensor product of the solid local Langlands correspondences at each point of X.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed Paskunas Functor and the Condensed Breuil-Schneider Conjecture for GL_n(Q_p)

The condensed Paskunas functor P_cond: Rep_cond^sm(GL_n(Q_p)) → Mod_cond^solid(Λ_cond) from the condensed category of smooth representations of GL_n(Q_p) to the condensed category of solid modules over the condensed Iwasawa algebra Λ_cond = O_E[[K_cond]] (where K_cond = GL_n(Z_p) and E is a finite extension of Q_p) is defined by P_cond(π_cond) = Hom_cond^sm(π_cond, C_c^∞,cond(GL_n(Q_p), O_E)) where C_c^∞,cond is the condensed space of locally constant compactly supported functions. The solid Breuil-Schneider conjecture for GL_n predicts a solid equivalence between the solid category of solid admissible Banach representations of GL_n(Q_p) and the solid category of solid (φ, Γ)-modules of rank n over the condensed Robba ring R_cond, compatible with the condensed Paskunas functor and the condensed Colmez-Montreal functor for n=2. The solid Paskunas functor is exact on the solid subcategory of solid admissible representations and commutes with the solid Bernstein center action. The solid Breuil-Schneider conjecture for GL_n is equivalent to the solid statement that the condensed Paskunas functor induces a solid equivalence between the solid derived category of solid admissible Banach representations and the solid derived category of solid (φ, Γ)-modules, and this equivalence is compatible with the solid local Langlands correspondence via the condensed Emerton-Gee stack EG_cond(GL_n) whose condensed points parametrize solid (φ, Γ)-modules of rank n. The solid proof for n=2 uses the condensed Colmez functor and the condensed Berger-Colmez theory of trianguline representations. For n>2, the solid Breuil-Schneider conjecture is related to the solid categorical p-adic Langlands program of Fargues-Scholze, where the solid completed cohomology H^•_cond(GL_n) carries a solid action of GL_n(Q_p) × Gal(Q_p/Q_p) and the solid Paskunas functor identifies the solid GL_n(Q_p)-action with the solid Galois action via the solid local Langlands correspondence. The solid Breuil-Schneider conjecture for GL_n implies the solid compatibility of the condensed local Langlands correspondence with the condensed parabolic induction and the condensed Zelevinsky classification: the solid Paskunas functor intertwines the solid parabolic induction on the representation side with the solid tensor product of (φ, Γ)-modules on the Galois side.

Condensed Prismatic Cohomology and the Condensed BMS Comparison Theorem for Formal Schemes

The condensed prismatic cohomology of a formal scheme 𝔛 over O_C is the solid complex RΓ_△,cond(𝔛) := RΓ((𝔛_cond)_△, O_△,cond) where (𝔛_cond)_△ is the condensed prismatic site of 𝔛_cond and O_△,cond is the condensed structure sheaf. The condensed BMS comparison theorem of Bhatt-Morrow-Scholze asserts that for a proper smooth formal scheme 𝔛 over O_C, there is a solid isomorphism of solid filtered φ-modules RΓ_△,cond(𝔛) ⊗_A_inf,cond^L B_dR+,cond ≃ RΓ_B_dR+,cond(𝔛) where A_inf,cond = W(O_C^♭)_cond is the condensed Fontaine period ring. The solid Nygaard filtration on RΓ_△,cond(𝔛) is the solid filtration Fil^i_Nyg RΓ_△,cond(𝔛) = φ-1(p^i) RΓ_△,cond(𝔛), and the solid BMS comparison isomorphism identifies the solid associated graded Gr^i_Nyg RΓ_△,cond(𝔛) with the solid Hodge cohomology RΓ(𝔛_cond, Ω^i_𝔛/O_C,cond)[-i] after derived base change to B_dR+,cond. The solid prismatic cohomology carries a solid Frobenius φ and a solid action of the condensed Galois group Gal(C/Q_p)_cond, and the solid BMS comparison is compatible with these structures. The condensed prismatic site (𝔛_cond)_△ is the solid category of condensed prisms (A_cond, I_cond) with a solid map 𝔛_cond → Spf(A_cond/I_cond), and the condensed structure sheaf O_△,cond is the solid sheaf sending (A_cond, I_cond) to A_cond. The solid Hodge-Tate comparison theorem identifies RΓ_△,cond(𝔛) ⊗_A_inf,cond^L O_C,cond with the solid Hodge-Tate cohomology ⊕_i RΓ(𝔛_cond, Ω^i_𝔛/O_C,cond)[-i] as solid O_C,cond-modules with solid Galois action. The solid étale comparison theorem identifies RΓ_△,cond(𝔛) ⊗_A_inf,cond^L Z_p,cond with the solid p-adic étale cohomology RΓ_ét(𝔛_η,cond, Z_p,cond) as solid Z_p,cond-modules with solid Galois action. The solid BMS comparison for non-proper varieties uses the condensed log prismatic cohomology of a condensed log smooth compactification, and the solid weight filtration on RΓ_△,cond(𝔛) coincides with the solid monodromy filtration on the condensed log prismatic cohomology. The solid prismatic F-crystals on 𝔛_cond are solid crystals on the condensed prismatic site with a solid Frobenius structure, and the solid category of solid prismatic F-crystals is solid equivalent to the solid category of solid filtered φ-modules over B_dR+,cond via the solid BMS comparison.

  • Confusing the condensed B_dR+-cohomology with the condensed B_cris-cohomology; the former carries a solid Hodge filtration while the latter carries a solid Frobenius and a solid Nygaard filtration, and the solid BMS comparison relates prismatic cohomology to B_dR+, not B_cris.
  • Assuming the condensed theta correspondence for (Sp2n, O_m) is always non-zero; it requires the solid epsilon factor condition ε_cond(1/2, π_cond × σ_cond, ψ_cond) = 1 and the solid local Langlands parameter compatibility.
  • Treating the condensed Arinkin-Gaitsgory induction as a classical integral transform; it is a solid functor between solid derived categories of solid D-modules and solid quasi-coherent sheaves, and its kernel is the solid universal Hecke eigensheaf Aut_cond, not a classical sheaf.
  • Believing the condensed Paskunas functor is exact on all solid smooth representations; it is exact only on the solid subcategory of solid admissible representations, and its solid derived functor on the solid derived category requires solid compact generation.
  • Overlooking that the condensed BMS comparison isomorphism is an isomorphism of solid filtered φ-modules, not merely solid φ-modules; the solid Hodge filtration on B_dR+ is essential and is induced by the solid Nygaard filtration on prismatic cohomology.
  • Misidentifying the solid component group S_φ_cond in the condensed theta correspondence for classical groups; it is the condensed group of connected components of the centralizer of the solid L-parameter image in the dual group, and its solid character group parametrizes the solid Arthur packet.
  • Neglecting the role of the solid almost ideal m_cond = (p1/p^∞) in the condensed Faltings' almost purity theorem; the solid almost isomorphism is with respect to this ideal, and an integer is almost invertible iff it is prime to p.

The Universal Property of Condensed Objects and the Categorical Foundation of Pro-Systems

Condensed mathematics fundamentally redefines how we approach topological spaces and their associated algebraic structures by introducing a universal framework for handling limits and colimits. At its core, a condensed set is not merely a set equipped with a topology, but rather a functor from the category of extremally disconnected compact Hausdorff spaces (often denoted EDCH) to the category of sets, satisfying a certain exactness condition. More precisely, a condensed set X is a presheaf on EDCH that is a sheaf for the pro-étale topology on EDCH. This perspective provides a powerful universal property: the category of condensed sets, denoted Cond(Set), is the universal completion of the category of sets under arbitrary limits and colimits that are "topologically sensible."

This construction elegantly generalizes the classical notion of pro-objects. A pro-object in a category (e.g., a pro-finite group, which is an inverse limit of finite groups) is a formal inverse system. While useful, pro-objects can be cumbersome, especially when dealing with non-discrete topological spaces or when trying to define derived functors. Condensed sets offer a more unified and flexible approach. For any topological space T, there is a canonical associated condensed set Tcond. This association is functorial and allows for a seamless translation of topological concepts into the condensed framework. The universal property manifests in the fact that continuous maps from a topological space T to a condensed set X are naturally identified with maps from Tcond to X in Cond(Set). This means that Cond(Set) acts as a "universal recipient" for topological information.

The significance of EDCH spaces lies in their exceptional topological properties: every open cover of an EDCH space has a finite subcover of clopen sets. This property makes them ideal test objects for defining sheaves in a way that respects topological limits. The category Cond(Set) is a Grothendieck (∞,1)-topos, meaning it possesses all the desirable categorical properties for doing homotopy theory and higher algebra. This higher categorical structure is crucial for defining derived categories of condensed modules, condensed spectra, and other advanced constructions. For instance, the category of condensed abelian groups, Cond(Ab), is an abelian category with enough projectives and injectives, allowing for standard homological algebra. The universal property ensures that any construction performed in Cond(Ab) (e.g., taking cohomology) has a direct and well-behaved interpretation in terms of classical topological abelian groups, provided they are sufficiently "nice."

Furthermore, the condensed framework provides a natural home for solid modules and liquid modules, which are specific types of condensed modules over condensed rings. Solid modules are defined over condensed rings that are "solid" (e.g., complete topological rings), and they exhibit properties analogous to modules over discrete rings, but in a topologically enriched context. The universal property of condensed sets ensures that the category of solid modules is well-behaved and allows for the development of a robust homological algebra, including derived categories and six-functor formalisms. This foundational aspect is what allows condensed mathematics to bridge disparate areas like algebraic geometry, p-adic analysis, and stable homotopy theory under a single coherent umbrella, by providing a common language for objects that are inherently topological and often involve inverse limits.

Condensed E∞-Algebras and their Role in Advanced Homotopy Theory

In modern stable homotopy theory, E∞-algebras play a foundational role as the correct algebraic objects for encoding commutative ring structures in a higher categorical setting. An E∞-algebra is, informally, a commutative algebra object in a symmetric monoidal (∞,1)-category, where commutativity holds up to all higher homotopies. These structures are essential for defining advanced invariants like topological Hochschild homology (THH), topological cyclic homology (TC), and algebraic K-theory spectra. The condensed framework provides a natural and robust environment for constructing and studying condensed E∞-algebras, thereby extending these powerful tools to topological and p-adic settings.

A condensed E∞-algebra is an E∞-algebra object in the symmetric monoidal (∞,1)-category of condensed spectra, Cond(Sp). The category Cond(Sp) is constructed by taking spectra object-wise in Cond(Set) or, more precisely, by forming the stable (∞,1)-category of condensed abelian groups. This allows for a notion of "algebraic geometry over the sphere spectrum" within the condensed world. For a condensed ring R, one can form its associated E∞-ring spectrum HR, and then study condensed modules over HR. This approach is particularly fruitful for arithmetic applications, as it allows for the computation of invariants that are sensitive to both algebraic and topological information.

The construction of condensed THH and TC is a prime example of the utility of condensed E∞-algebras. For a condensed E∞-algebra A, its condensed THH, denoted THHcond(A), is itself a condensed spectrum that captures the "algebraic K-theory of A up to homotopy." By applying the cyclotomic trace map, one can then define condensed TC, TCcond(A), which is a crucial invariant in p-adic Hodge theory and algebraic K-theory. These condensed invariants are typically constructed using specific models for E∞-algebras, such as simplicial commutative algebras or operadic algebras, adapted to the condensed setting. The solidity property of modules often plays a role here, ensuring that the underlying algebraic structures are well-behaved with respect to limits and colimits.

The power of condensed E∞-algebras extends to the study of chromatic homotopy theory in a condensed context. Chromatic homotopy theory aims to understand stable homotopy theory by localizing at various "chromatic primes" or Morava K-theories. Condensed E∞-algebras provide the necessary algebraic machinery to define condensed versions of Morava E-theories and other chromatic invariants. This allows for a deeper understanding of the structure of condensed spectra and their connections to arithmetic geometry, particularly through the lens of the Fargues-Fontaine curve and p-adic period rings. The ability to work with E∞-algebras in a condensed setting ensures that the powerful tools of higher algebra and stable homotopy theory can be brought to bear on problems involving topological rings and spaces, yielding new insights into their fundamental properties and relationships.

Examiner's Challenge: Condensed vs. Classical Derived Categories
Consider a compact Hausdorff space X. Let D(Ab(X)) be the classical derived category of sheaves of abelian groups on X, and D(Cond(Ab)) be the derived category of condensed abelian groups. Explain why, despite X having an associated condensed set Xcond, the derived category of sheaves on Xcond in Cond(Ab) is not generally equivalent to D(Ab(X)), and what the key difference in their construction or interpretation implies for homological computations.
The core difference lies in the underlying categories and the notion of "sheaf." In classical algebraic topology, D(Ab(X)) is the derived category of sheaves of abelian groups on the topological space X, where sheaves are defined with respect to the usual open cover topology on X. This construction relies on the existence of enough injectives in Ab(X). In contrast, the derived category of condensed abelian groups, D(Cond(Ab)), is built upon the category of condensed abelian groups, Cond(Ab), which is itself a category of sheaves on the site EDCH (extremally disconnected compact Hausdorff spaces) with the pro-étale topology. When we consider sheaves on Xcond (the condensed set associated with X), we are working within the (∞,1)-topos of condensed sets. The derived category of sheaves on Xcond would be the derived category of modules over the structure sheaf OXcond in Cond(Ab). The key discrepancy arises because the site EDCH and its pro-étale topology are fundamentally different from the open set topology on X. The category Cond(Ab) is a much "larger" and more flexible category designed to handle arbitrary limits and colimits in a topologically coherent way. While there is a functor from classical sheaves on X to condensed sheaves on Xcond, this functor is generally not an equivalence of categories, let alone derived categories. The condensed framework captures "pro-homotopical" information that classical sheaves on a general topological space might miss. For instance, a classical sheaf on X might not be "solid" in the condensed sense, meaning it might not behave well with respect to the continuous limits inherent in the condensed definition. Homological computations in D(Cond(Ab)) benefit from the fact that Cond(Ab) is a Grothendieck abelian category with very good homological properties, allowing for a more robust and often simpler treatment of derived functors and spectral sequences, particularly for non-archimedean fields or highly pathological topological spaces where classical sheaf theory struggles. The condensed derived category is designed to capture the "derived topological information" of X in a way that is compatible with p-adic analysis and higher algebra, which is a broader scope than classical sheaf cohomology.
Mastering Abstract Definitions
When encountering highly abstract definitions like "condensed set as a functor from EDCH," focus on the *purpose* and *consequences* rather than memorizing the precise categorical details. Understand that EDCH spaces are "test objects" that simplify topological limits, and the functorial definition ensures universality. For exams, be ready to explain *why* such a definition is useful (e.g., handles non-discrete spaces, generalizes pro-objects, provides a robust derived category) and contrast it with classical approaches, rather than merely stating the definition.

The Condensed Adic Formalism for Perfectoid Spaces and Valuation Theory

The theory of perfectoid spaces, introduced by Scholze, revolutionized p-adic geometry by providing a framework where "tilting" becomes an equivalence, connecting spaces over a p-adic field K with spaces over its tilt K♭ (a field of characteristic p). The condensed formalism offers a natural and powerful setting for developing the adic theory of perfectoid spaces, extending their utility and providing a robust foundation for their geometric and analytic properties beyond the initial tilting equivalence.

A perfectoid space X is intrinsically an adic space, meaning it is locally modeled on spectra of complete topological rings equipped with a suitable topology. In the condensed framework, this adic structure is naturally accommodated. A condensed perfectoid space is a condensed adic space Xcond where the underlying condensed ring OXcond satisfies the perfectoid condition (e.g., p is topologically nilpotent, and the Frobenius map on OXcond/p is an isomorphism). This allows for a more flexible and general definition of perfectoid spaces, including those that are not necessarily affinoid or rigid analytic in the classical sense.

The connection to valuation theory is paramount. Points of an adic space are given by equivalence classes of valuations. In the condensed setting, the structure of points on a perfectoid space Xcond can be understood through condensed valuation rings and their associated condensed fields. The tilting equivalence, X → X♭, translates the valuation theory of X into the valuation theory of its tilt X♭ in characteristic p. This means that the set of valuations on X is in canonical bijection with the set of valuations on X♭, preserving their structure. The condensed framework makes this bijection precise and functorial, allowing for the comparison of geometric properties (e.g., connectedness, dimension, singularities) across characteristics.

Furthermore, the condensed adic formalism provides a robust setting for defining and studying sheaves on perfectoid spaces. For instance, the structure sheaf OXcond is a condensed ring, and condensed modules over it form the basis for cohomology theories. This is crucial for constructing period rings like Ainf, Bcris, BdR, etc., in a condensed context. These period rings are themselves condensed rings, and their properties (e.g., their solid structures, their Frobenius and Galois actions) are essential for p-adic Hodge theory. The condensed approach allows for a unified treatment of these period rings, showing how they arise naturally from the tilting equivalence and the adic structure of perfectoid spaces.

The geometric implications are profound. The condensed adic formalism allows for the construction of condensed diamonds, which are a generalization of perfectoid spaces that serve as a universal home for objects in p-adic geometry. Diamonds are sheaves on the category of perfectoid spaces with the pro-étale topology, and they inherit the rich structure of condensed sets. This enables the study of moduli spaces of p-adic objects (e.g., p-divisible groups, local Shtukas) in a highly flexible and powerful way, leading to breakthroughs in the p-adic Langlands program and arithmetic geometry. The ability to define and manipulate these complex geometric objects in a condensed setting, respecting their adic and valuation-theoretic properties, is a cornerstone of modern p-adic analysis.

Condensed Étale Cohomology with Coefficients in Condensed Spectra and its Arithmetic Significance

The classical theory of étale cohomology is a cornerstone of algebraic geometry, providing powerful invariants for schemes and algebraic spaces. When combined with the machinery of stable homotopy theory, specifically through the use of spectra as coefficients, it leads to even richer invariants such as algebraic K-theory and motivic cohomology.
