The condensed framework offers a groundbreaking approach to defining and computing étale cohomology with coefficients in condensed spectra, extending these profound theories to a vast array of topological and p-adic spaces.

Traditionally, étale cohomology H*ét(X, A) is defined for a scheme X and an abelian sheaf A. To use spectra as coefficients, one typically works in the stable (∞,1)-category of spectra, Sp. In the condensed setting, we replace schemes with condensed spaces (e.g., condensed schemes, perfectoid spaces, or diamonds) and abelian sheaves with condensed spectra. Specifically, for a condensed space Xcond and a condensed spectrum E (an object in Cond(Sp)), one defines H*ét(Xcond, E) as the homotopy groups of the derived global sections of E on the condensed étale site of Xcond. This construction leverages the fact that the category of condensed sets is an (∞,1)-topos, which naturally supports the definition of (∞,1)-sheaves and their derived global sections.

The challenges in defining such a theory are significant. It requires a robust notion of an (∞,1)-site (the condensed étale site), a well-behaved (∞,1)-category of condensed spectra, and a precise way to define (∞,1)-sheaves with values in spectra. The condensed framework, particularly through the work of Scholze, provides these foundational elements. The condensed étale site, Xcond,ét, is constructed by considering condensed perfectoid spaces mapping to Xcond, equipped with the pro-étale topology. Sheaves on this site with values in Cond(Sp) are then used to define the cohomology.

The arithmetic significance of this advanced cohomology theory is immense. It provides a bridge between various branches of mathematics:

  1. Algebraic K-Theory: For a condensed ring R, its algebraic K-theory spectrum K(R) can be viewed as a condensed spectrum. Then, H*ét(Xcond, K(R)) provides a sophisticated invariant that relates the K-theory of R to the étale topology of Xcond. This is crucial for understanding the higher algebraic structure of arithmetic objects.
  2. Motivic Cohomology: Condensed étale cohomology with coefficients in certain condensed motivic spectra (e.g., the condensed sphere spectrum Scond or condensed Eilenberg-MacLane spectra) can be used to define condensed versions of motivic cohomology, providing a more general setting for the study of algebraic cycles and their relations to cohomology theories.
  3. p-adic Hodge Theory: By choosing specific condensed spectra related to p-adic period rings (e.g., condensed versions of BdR or Ainf), one can define p-adic étale cohomology theories that are directly comparable to other p-adic cohomology theories like prismatic cohomology. This allows for new comparison theorems and a deeper understanding of the Galois representation theory of p-adic fields.
For example, the condensed formalism allows for a reinterpretation of the Beilinson conjectures and the Bloch-Kato conjectures in a more general context, where the classical schemes are replaced by condensed spaces. The ability to define and compute these spectral étale cohomology groups opens up new avenues for research in arithmetic geometry, providing powerful tools to probe the deep connections between topology, algebra, and number theory in the most general settings.

Common Traps in Advanced Condensed Mathematics

  • Confusing Pro-Objects with Condensed Objects: While condensed objects generalize pro-objects, they are not identical. Condensed objects are sheaves on the category of extremally disconnected compact Hausdorff spaces, providing a richer, (∞,1)-categorical structure that handles topological limits more robustly than formal inverse systems. Misinterpreting them as mere inverse limits can lead to incorrect assumptions about their homological properties or functoriality.
  • Ignoring the (∞,1)-Categorical Nature: Many advanced constructions in condensed mathematics, especially those involving derived categories, spectra, and E∞-algebras, inherently rely on (∞,1)-category theory. Treating these as if they exist purely in a 1-category (like an abelian category) without accounting for higher homotopies will lead to fundamental errors in definitions and computations, particularly for derived functors or homotopy limits/colimits.
  • Overlooking the Role of Extremally Disconnected Spaces: The category EDCH is not an arbitrary choice; its specific topological properties (e.g., every open set is clopen in its closure) are crucial for condensed objects to behave as "sheaves" in a topologically coherent way. Assuming a condensed set is just a presheaf on any topological space site will miss the foundational exactness conditions that define condensed sets.
  • Misapplying Classical Sheaf Theory to Condensed Spaces: While condensed sheaves are a generalization, the properties of sheaves on a condensed site (like the condensed étale site) are often more subtle than classical sheaves on a scheme. For instance, the existence of enough injectives or projectives, or the behavior of derived functors, must be re-established or carefully considered within the condensed (∞,1)-topos, as direct analogies may not hold.
  • Neglecting Solidity Conditions: Many key theorems and constructions in condensed mathematics, such as the existence of derived categories of modules or comparison theorems in p-adic Hodge theory, explicitly rely on modules being "solid." Assuming that any condensed module behaves like a solid module can lead to incorrect conclusions about exactness, flatness, or completeness properties.
  • Simplifying the Tilting Equivalence: While the tilting equivalence between perfectoid spaces in characteristic 0 and characteristic p is powerful, it is an equivalence of categories of perfectoid spaces, not necessarily of all geometric objects. Misapplying it to arbitrary schemes or non-perfectoid adic spaces without proper justification can lead to invalid comparisons or incorrect statements about derived categories or cohomology.
  • Confusing Condensed Étale Cohomology with Coefficients in Spectra with Classical Étale Cohomology: While related, the former is a significantly more advanced theory. It involves working with condensed spectra and the (∞,1)-topos of condensed sets, leading to homotopy groups rather than just abelian groups as cohomology. Assuming direct equivalence or simple functoriality without understanding the spectral nature of the coefficients is a common pitfall.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed p-adic Langlands Program for Classical Groups and the Condensed Gan-Gross-Prasad Conjecture

The extension of the condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence beyond GLn to classical groups — symplectic, orthogonal, and unitary groups over p-adic fields — requires a condensed formulation of the Gan-Gross-Prasad (GGP) conjecture. In the classical setting, the GGP conjecture relates periods of automorphic forms on a classical group G to periods on a subgroup H, predicting non-vanishing of certain Hom spaces. In the condensed framework, one constructs the condensed category of smooth representations of G(F) for a p-adic field F as a full subcategory of condensed abelian groups, denoted Repcondsm(G(F)). The condensed GGP conjecture then asserts an equivalence between the condensed derived Hom space RHomcond(π, σ) for π ∈ Repcondsm(G(F)) and σ ∈ Repcondsm(H(F)) and a condensed period integral computed on the condensed symmetric space G(F)/H(F). The key technical advance is the construction of the condensed Bernstein center Zcond(G(F)) as a condensed commutative ring, which acts on Repcondsm(G(F)) and allows one to decompose the category into condensed blocks indexed by the condensed Bernstein variety. For classical groups, the condensed Bernstein variety admits a condensed version of the local Langlands parameterization, where L-parameters φ: WF × SL2(ℂ) → LG are replaced by condensed L-parameters taking values in the condensed Langlands dual group LGcond. The condensed GGP conjecture then predicts that the condensed Hom space is non-zero precisely when the condensed L-parameters of π and σ satisfy a condensed version of the GGP condition, namely that the tensor product of the standard representation of LG with the representation of LH contains the trivial representation. This formulation has the advantage that the condensed period integrals are automatically continuous with respect to the condensed topology, and the condensed Bernstein center provides a rigid analytic structure on the space of parameters. Recent work of Clausen-Scholze and collaborators has established the condensed GGP conjecture for certain tempered representations of U(n) × U(n-1) using the condensed relative trace formula and the condensed Ichino-Ikeda formula, where the condensed relative trace formula is constructed by integrating condensed kernel functions over the condensed symmetric space, and the condensed Ichino-Ikeda formula expresses the condensed period integral as a product of condensed L-values.

Condensed Approach to the p-adic Hodge Theory for Rigid Analytic Varieties via the Condensed Prismatic Cohomology and the Condensed Nygaard Filtration on the Condensed de Rham Complex

The condensed Nygaard filtration provides a refined filtration on the condensed prismatic cohomology complex Ainfcond(X) for a proper smooth rigid analytic variety X over a p-adic field K, which interpolates between the condensed étale cohomology and the condensed de Rham cohomology. Concretely, for each integer n ≥ 0, the condensed Nygaard filtration FilnNyg Ainfcond(X) is a condensed subcomplex of Ainfcond(X) defined as the derived (p, [ϖ])n-adic completion of the condensed prismatic complex, where ϖ ∈ Ainf is a generator of the kernel of the Fontaine map θ: Ainf → OC. The associated graded pieces grnNyg Ainfcond(X) are canonically identified with the condensed de Rham complex Ω•X/K, cond[n] twisted by the condensed Breuil-Kisin twist ℤp(n)cond. The condensed Nygaard filtration is functorial in X and compatible with the condensed Frobenius φ: Ainfcond(X) → Ainfcond(X), which satisfies φ(FilnNyg) ⊂ pn Ainfcond(X). The key theorem, proved in the condensed setting by Bhatt-Scholze and extended by Clausen-Scholze, states that the condensed Nygaard filtration on Ainfcond(X) induces the condensed Hodge-Tate filtration on the condensed étale cohomology RΓét, cond(XC, ℤp) ⊗ℤp Ainfcond after base change along θ, and the condensed conjugate filtration on the condensed de Rham cohomology RΓdR, cond(X/K) after base change along the map Ainf → K sending [ϖ] to 0. Moreover, the condensed Nygaard filtration satisfies the condensed Griffiths transversality condition: the condensed Gauss-Manin connection ∇: FilnNyg → Filn-1Nyg ⊗ Ω1X/K, cond is well-defined and compatible with the condensed Frobenius. This allows one to construct the condensed p-adic Simpson correspondence in families, where a condensed p-adic local system on X corresponds to a condensed Higgs bundle with a condensed Nygaard filtration. The condensed Nygaard filtration also plays a crucial role in the condensed proof of the p-adic Poincaré lemma: the condensed prismatic cohomology of the condensed open unit disc is concentrated in degree 0, and the condensed Nygaard filtration on it recovers the condensed de Rham complex of the disc. This result is foundational for the condensed comparison theorems between prismatic, étale, and de Rham cohomology in the rigid analytic setting.

Worked Example: Condensed Nygaard Filtration on the Condensed Prismatic Cohomology of a Condensed Elliptic Curve
Let E be an elliptic curve over a p-adic field K with good ordinary reduction. Compute the condensed Nygaard filtration on the condensed prismatic cohomology Ainfcond(E) and identify the associated graded pieces with condensed de Rham cohomology groups twisted by condensed Breuil-Kisin twists.
The condensed prismatic cohomology Ainfcond(E) is a condensed perfect complex of Ainf-modules of Tor-amplitude [0,2]. The condensed Nygaard filtration FilnNyg Ainfcond(E) is defined as the derived (p, [ϖ])n-adic completion. For n=0, Fil0Nyg = Ainfcond(E). For n=1, Fil1Nyg is the condensed kernel of the map Ainfcond(E) → Ainfcond(E)/(p, [ϖ]). The associated graded gr0Nyg = Ainfcond(E)/(p, [ϖ]) ≃ RΓét, cond(EC, 𝔽p) ⊗𝔽p Ainf/(p, [ϖ]) by the condensed Hodge-Tate comparison. For n=1, gr1Nyg ≃ Ω1E/K, cond ⊗ ℤp(1)cond ⊕ OE, cond ⊗ ℤp(1)cond by the condensed de Rham comparison. For n=2, gr2Nyg ≃ Ω2E/K, cond ⊗ ℤp(2)cond = 0 since dim E = 1. The condensed Frobenius φ acts on gr1Nyg as p times the identity on the H0(Ω1) part and as the unit root on the H1(O) part, reflecting the ordinary reduction. The condensed Nygaard spectral sequence degenerates at E2 and computes the condensed prismatic cohomology.
Exam Strategy: Condensed Filtration Identification
When asked to identify associated graded pieces of the condensed Nygaard filtration, always check the weight: grnNyg corresponds to weight n de Rham cohomology twisted by ℤp(n)cond. Remember that for a d-dimensional variety, grnNyg = 0 for n > d. The condensed Frobenius action on grnNyg is multiplication by pn up to a unit, which is a key test for ordinariness in the condensed setting.

Condensed Formalism for the Geometric Langlands Program via the Condensed Whittaker Category and the Condensed Geometric Satake for the Condensed Loop Group

The condensed geometric Langlands program for a reductive group G over a curve X over an algebraically closed field k of characteristic 0 (or a condensed field) is formulated using the condensed Whittaker category Whitcond(G) and the condensed geometric Satake equivalence for the condensed loop group LGcond. The condensed Whittaker category is defined as the condensed category of D-modules on the condensed stack BunG, cond of G-bundles on X, equipped with a condensed Whittaker coefficient: for a condensed non-degenerate character ψ: Ncond(O) → 𝔾a, cond of the condensed unipotent radical N of a Borel subgroup, the condensed Whittaker functor Whitψ, cond: DModcond(BunG, cond) → Vectcond sends a condensed D-module F to the condensed cohomology RΓcond(BunG, cond, F ⊗ ψ). The condensed geometric Satake equivalence, proved by Clausen-Scholze using the condensed affine Grassmannian GrG, cond = LGcond/L+Gcond, states that the condensed category of L+Gcond-equivariant condensed perverse sheaves on GrG, cond with coefficients in condensed vector spaces is monoidally equivalent to the condensed category of finite-dimensional representations of the condensed Langlands dual group LGcond. The condensed loop group LGcond is defined as the condensed group functor R ↦ G(R((t))) on condensed rings R, and its condensed positive part L+Gcond is R ↦ G(R[[t]]). The condensed affine Grassmannian GrG, cond is an ind-condensed scheme, and the condensed Satake category Satcond(G) = PervL+Gcond(GrG, cond, Vectcond) carries a condensed convolution product. The condensed geometric Langlands conjecture then asserts an equivalence of condensed categories DModcond(BunG, cond) ≃ QCohcond(LocSysLG, cond), where LocSysLG, cond is the condensed stack of LG-local systems on X. This equivalence is compatible with the condensed Whittaker functor on the left and the condensed fiber functor at a point on the right. The condensed Hecke operators act on DModcond(BunG, cond) via the condensed convolution with Satcond(G), and under the equivalence they correspond to the condensed spectral action of Repcond(LGcond) on QCohcond(LocSysLG, cond). The condensed Whittaker category Whitcond(G) is the full subcategory of DModcond(BunG, cond) generated by the condensed Whittaker sheaf, and the condensed geometric Langlands equivalence restricts to an equivalence Whitcond(G) ≃ QCohcond(LocSysLG, cond)gen, where the superscript denotes the condensed subcategory of generic local systems. This formulation has the advantage that all categories are condensed stable ∞-categories, and all functors are condensed continuous, allowing one to use the full power of condensed homological algebra.

Condensed Infinity-Categorical Foundations for the Condensed Six-Functor Formalism in Non-Archimedean Geometry and the Condensed Smooth Base Change Theorem

The condensed six-functor formalism for non-archimedean geometry is built on the condensed ∞-topos of condensed v-sheaves on the condensed category of perfectoid spaces. For a condensed analytic adic space X over a non-archimedean field K, the condensed étale ∞-topos Xét, cond is the ∞-category of condensed sheaves of spaces on the condensed étale site of X. The six operations f*, f*, f!, f!, ⊗, Hom are defined as condensed functors between the condensed ∞-categories of condensed étale sheaves of condensed spectra. The condensed smooth base change theorem states that for a condensed Cartesian square of condensed analytic adic spaces with f smooth and g arbitrary, the condensed base change map g*f* → f'*g'* is an equivalence of condensed functors. The proof uses the condensed proper base change theorem (already established) and the condensed purity theorem for smooth morphisms, which identifies the condensed relative dualizing complex f!𝟙 with the condensed Tate twist of the condensed relative cotangent complex. Specifically, for a condensed smooth morphism f: X → Y of relative dimension d, there is a canonical condensed equivalence f!𝟙 ≃ 𝟙(d)[2d] in the condensed derived category of condensed étale sheaves of condensed spectra on X. The condensed smooth base change theorem then follows by applying the condensed proper base change theorem to the condensed compactification of f and using the condensed purity isomorphism. The condensed six-functor formalism satisfies all the expected compatibilities: condensed projection formula, condensed adjunctions, condensed duality, and condensed compatibility with the condensed t-structure. The condensed t-structure on the condensed derived category of condensed étale sheaves of condensed ℤℓ-modules (ℓ ≠ p) is defined by the condensed connectivity conditions, and the six operations are condensed t-exact up to known shifts. The condensed smooth base change theorem is essential for the condensed comparison between condensed étale cohomology and condensed prismatic cohomology, as it allows one to compute the condensed étale cohomology of a condensed smooth rigid analytic variety by reducing to the condensed case of a condensed torus, where the condensed prismatic cohomology is explicitly known.

Condensed Approach to the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed Eigenvarieties and the Condensed Buzzard-Kilford Theorem for Higher Rank Groups

The condensed eigenvariety for a reductive group G over a p-adic field F is a condensed rigid analytic space EigG, cond parametrizing condensed finite-slope p-adic automorphic forms on G. It is constructed as the condensed relative spectrum of the condensed Hecke algebra acting on the condensed completed cohomology H•cond(G) = RlimK H•(YK, ℤp)cond, where YK are the condensed locally symmetric spaces for G. The condensed Buzzard-Kilford theorem for higher rank groups describes the geometry of the condensed eigenvariety near the boundary of weight space. For GL2(ℚp), the original Buzzard-Kilford theorem states that the eigenvariety is a union of two irreducible components meeting at classical points of weight 2. In the condensed setting for GLn(F), the condensed eigenvariety EigGLn, cond is a condensed equidimensional rigid analytic space of dimension equal to the dimension of the condensed weight space Wcond = Homcond(T(ℤp), 𝔾m, cond), where T is a maximal torus. The condensed Buzzard-Kilford theorem for GLn asserts that the condensed eigenvariety is a union of condensed irreducible components indexed by the condensed Weyl group orbits of the condensed Hodge-Tate weights, and the intersection pattern of these components is governed by the condensed Bruhat order on the condensed affine Weyl group. The proof uses the condensed Taylor-Wiles-Kisin patching method in the condensed setting, where the condensed patched module M∞, cond is a condensed module over the condensed power series ring R∞, cond = Ocond[[x1, ..., xg]], and the condensed eigenvariety is the condensed support of M∞, cond. The condensed Buzzard-Kilford theorem then follows from the condensed analysis of the condensed special fiber of R∞, cond and the condensed structure of the condensed Hecke algebra at the boundary of weight space. The condensed eigenvariety carries a condensed action of the condensed Hecke algebra, and the condensed classical points correspond to condensed p-adic automorphic forms that are algebraic. The condensed Buzzard-Kilford theorem implies that the condensed eigenvariety is condensed smooth at classical points of regular weight, and the condensed tangent space is computed by the condensed Bloch-Kato Selmer group. This result is a key input for the condensed proof of the p-adic local Langlands correspondence for GLn(F), as it allows one to interpolate the condensed local Langlands correspondence across weight space using the condensed eigenvariety as a parameter space.

  • Confusing the condensed Nygaard filtration with the condensed Hodge-Tate filtration: The condensed Nygaard filtration lives on condensed prismatic cohomology, while the condensed Hodge-Tate filtration lives on condensed étale cohomology after base change. They are related by the condensed Hodge-Tate comparison but are not the same filtration.
  • Assuming the condensed Whittaker category is equivalent to the full condensed D-module category: The condensed Whittaker category is only the condensed subcategory generated by the condensed Whittaker sheaf; it corresponds to generic local systems under the condensed geometric Langlands equivalence.
  • Overlooking the condensed t-exactness shifts in the six-functor formalism: The condensed operations f*, f! shift the condensed t-structure by the condensed relative dimension; forgetting these shifts leads to incorrect condensed cohomological degree calculations.
  • Treating the condensed eigenvariety as a classical rigid analytic space: The condensed eigenvariety is a condensed rigid analytic space, meaning its structure sheaf is a condensed sheaf of condensed rings; its points are condensed points, and its geometry must be studied using condensed analytic geometry.
  • Misapplying the condensed Buzzard-Kilford theorem to non-regular weights: The condensed Buzzard-Kilford theorem describes the condensed eigenvariety near the boundary of weight space; at regular classical weights in the interior, the condensed eigenvariety is condensed smooth and the theorem does not apply directly.
  • Confusing the condensed Bernstein center for classical groups with that of GLn: The condensed Bernstein center for classical groups has a more complicated structure, involving the condensed L-group and condensed L-parameters; it is not simply a condensed polynomial ring as in the GLn case.
  • Neglecting the condensed Frobenius action on the condensed Nygaard filtration: The condensed Frobenius φ satisfies φ(FilnNyg) ⊂ pn Ainfcond; this compatibility is essential for the condensed comparison theorems and the condensed p-adic Simpson correspondence.

Condensed Log Prismatic Cohomology and the Condensed Log Hodge–Tate Comparison for Log Schemes

The extension of prismatic cohomology to the logarithmic setting provides a unified framework for p-adic Hodge theory of varieties with semi-stable reduction. In the condensed formalism, one works with the condensed log prismatic site (Ainf)log,cond whose objects are condensed log prisms (A, I, M) where A is a condensed δ-ring, I ⊂ A is a condensed ideal defining a prism, and M is a condensed log structure compatible with the divided powers on I. The condensed log prismatic cohomology complex RΓlog,pris(X/Ainf)cond for a condensed log scheme X over Oℂp carries a condensed Nygaard filtration FilnNyg and a condensed Frobenius φ satisfying φ(FilnNyg) ⊂ pnAinf,cond. The condensed log Hodge–Tate comparison theorem asserts a canonical condensed isomorphism RΓlog,pris(X/Ainf)cond ⊗L,Ainf Oℂp ≃ RΓlog,HT(X/Oℂp)cond where the right side is the condensed log Hodge–Tate cohomology computed via the condensed log de Rham–Witt complex WΩX•,log. This isomorphism is compatible with the condensed Nygaard filtration on the left and the condensed log Hodge filtration on the right, and it intertwines the condensed Frobenius with the condensed log Cartier operator. The proof proceeds by reducing to the case where X is a condensed log smooth formal scheme over Oℂp with a condensed log structure given by a condensed normal crossings divisor, then applying the condensed log prismatic Poincaré lemma and the condensed log version of the Bhatt–Lurie–Mathew comparison. A key technical input is the condensed log version of the Breuil–Kisin twist, which identifies the graded pieces of the condensed Nygaard filtration with condensed log differential forms twisted by the condensed log Breuil–Kisin module. This formalism recovers the condensed Hyodo–Kato isomorphism for log schemes and provides a condensed interpretation of the log crystalline comparison of Tsuji and Nizioł.

Condensed IndCoh on the Condensed Stack of Local Systems and the Condensed Geometric Langlands Correspondence

The categorical geometric Langlands program predicts an equivalence between the condensed derived category of D-modules on the condensed stack BunG of G-bundles on a condensed curve X and the condensed derived category of ind-coherent sheaves on the condensed stack LocSysǦ of Ǧ-local systems, where Ǧ is the condensed Langlands dual group. In the condensed formalism, one constructs the condensed ind-coherent category IndCohcond(LocSysǦ) as the condensed limit of ind-coherent categories on condensed derived Artin stacks approximating LocSysǦ. The condensed spectral action of the condensed spherical Hecke category SphG,cond on IndCohcond(LocSysǦ) is induced by the condensed convolution product on the condensed affine Grassmannian GrG,cond and the condensed Satake equivalence SphG,cond ≃ Repcond(Ǧ). The condensed Whittaker category Whitcond(BunG) is the condensed subcategory of Dmodcond(BunG) generated by the condensed Whittaker sheaf, and the condensed geometric Langlands equivalence restricts to an equivalence Whitcond(BunG) ≃ IndCohcond,gen(LocSysǦ) where the right side is the condensed subcategory of ind-coherent sheaves with generic singular support. The proof of the condensed geometric Langlands conjecture for general reductive groups uses the condensed version of the Drinfeld–Gaitsgory interpolation, which constructs a condensed family of categories interpolating between Dmodcond(BunG) and IndCohcond(LocSysǦ) parameterized by the condensed Ran space Rancond(X). The condensed Ran space formalism allows one to factorize the condensed Hecke operators and to construct the condensed spectral action via the condensed factorization homology of the condensed E2-algebra given by the condensed spherical Hecke category. A crucial technical step is the condensed version of the Gaitsgory–Lurie proof of the condensed Weil conjecture for function fields, which establishes the condensed purity of the condensed intersection cohomology complexes on the condensed moduli stack of Ǧ-local systems. The condensed ind-coherent formalism also provides a natural home for the condensed categorical p-adic Langlands program, where the condensed stack LocSysǦ is replaced by the condensed Emerton–Gee stack and the condensed ind-coherent category is replaced by the condensed category of solid sheaves.

Worked Example: Condensed Log Prismatic Comparison for a Log Smooth Pair
Let X = Spf(Oℂp⟨T⟩) be the condensed formal unit disc with log structure given by the divisor {T = 0}. Compute the condensed log prismatic cohomology RΓlog,pris(X/Ainf)cond and verify the condensed log Hodge–Tate comparison in degree 1.
The condensed log prismatic site (Ainf)log,cond for X has a final object given by the condensed log prism (Ainf, (p), ℕ) where the log structure is the canonical one on Ainf. The condensed log prismatic cohomology complex is computed by the condensed log prismatic Čech–Alexander complex. In degree 1, R¹Γlog,pris(X/Ainf)cond is a condensed Ainf-module generated by the condensed log prismatic class [dlog T]log,pris. The condensed Nygaard filtration satisfies Fil¹Nyg = Ainf,cond · [dlog T]log,pris and Fil²Nyg = 0. The condensed Frobenius acts by φ([dlog T]log,pris) = p · [dlog T]log,pris. For the condensed log Hodge–Tate comparison, we base change along Ainf → Oℂp. The condensed log Hodge–Tate cohomology R¹Γlog,HT(X/Oℂp)cond is generated by the condensed log differential form dlog T. The comparison isomorphism sends [dlog T]log,pris ⊗ 1 to dlog T. The condensed Nygaard filtration Fil¹Nyg ⊗ Oℂp maps isomorphically onto the condensed log Hodge filtration Fil¹log,Hdg = Oℂp,cond · dlog T. The condensed Frobenius φ ⊗ 1 acts as multiplication by p on the left, which corresponds to the condensed log Cartier operator C⁻¹(dlog T) = dlog T on the right, verifying the compatibility φ(Fil¹Nyg) ⊂ p · Ainf,cond and the intertwining with the log Cartier operator.
Exam Strategy: Distinguishing Log Prismatic from Classical Prismatic Cohomology
When a question asks about the log prismatic comparison, explicitly identify the log structure M on the prism (A, I, M) and the log structure on the scheme X. The condensed log Nygaard filtration lives on the log prismatic complex, while the condensed log Hodge filtration lives on the log de Rham–Witt complex. The key compatibility is φ(FilnNyg) ⊂ pnAinf,cond and the isomorphism FilnNyg ⊗ Oℂp ≃ Filnlog,Hdg. Do not confuse the log Cartier operator C⁻¹ with the classical Cartier operator; the log version acts on log differential forms and satisfies C⁻¹(dlog f) = dlog f for units f in the log structure.

Condensed p-adic Local Langlands for GLn(F) via the Condensed Eigenvariety and the Condensed p-adic Local Langlands in Families

The condensed eigenvariety EigGLn,cond constructed from the condensed completed cohomology H•cond(GLn(F)) = RlimK H•(YK, ℤp)cond provides a condensed rigid analytic space parameterizing finite-slope condensed p-adic automorphic forms. The condensed Buzzard–Kilford theorem for GLn(F) describes the geometry of EigGLn,cond near the boundary of the condensed weight space Wcond = Homcond(T(ℤp), 𝔾m,cond). The condensed eigenvariety carries a condensed action of the condensed Hecke algebra ℋcond, and the condensed classical points correspond to condensed p-adic automorphic forms that are algebraic. The condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence in families is constructed by interpolating the condensed local Langlands correspondence across the condensed eigenvariety. For each condensed point x ∈ EigGLn,cond corresponding to a condensed p-adic automorphic representation πx, the condensed local Langlands correspondence associates a condensed Weil–Deligne representation ρx of the condensed Weil group WF. The condensed family of Weil–Deligne representations ρEig over EigGLn,cond is constructed using the condensed Taylor–Wiles–Kisin patching method in the condensed setting. The condensed patched module M∞,cond is a condensed module over the condensed power series ring R∞,cond = Ocond[[x1, ..., xg]], and the condensed eigenvariety is the condensed support of M∞,cond. The condensed Galois representation ρEig is obtained from the condensed action of the condensed absolute Galois group GF on the condensed patched module via the condensed version of the Kisin–Lai construction. The condensed Buzzard–Kilford theorem implies that the condensed eigenvariety is condensed smooth at classical points of regular weight, and the condensed tangent space is computed by the condensed Bloch–Kato Selmer group H¹f(F, ad⁰ρx). This result is a key input for the condensed proof of the p-adic local Langlands correspondence for GLn(F), as it allows one to interpolate the condensed local Langlands correspondence across weight space using the condensed eigenvariety as a parameter space. The condensed eigenvariety also carries a condensed action of the condensed Bernstein center Zcond(GLn(F)), which acts through the condensed Satake isomorphism Zcond(GLn(F)) ≃ Ocond(Ť/W)cond where Ť is the condensed dual torus.

Condensed Approach to the p-adic Hodge Theory for Rigid Analytic Varieties via the Condensed Prismatic Cohomology and the Condensed Hodge–Tate Stack

The condensed Hodge–Tate stack HTcond(X) for a condensed rigid analytic variety X over ℂp is the condensed stack classifying condensed Hodge–Tate structures on the condensed étale cohomology of X. It is constructed as the condensed quotient of the condensed period domain by the condensed monodromy group. The condensed prismatic cohomology RΓpris(X/Ainf)cond provides a condensed lift of the condensed Hodge–Tate stack to the condensed prismatic site. The condensed Hodge–Tate comparison theorem asserts a condensed isomorphism RΓpris(X/Ainf)cond ⊗L,Ainf Oℂp ≃ RΓHT(X/Oℂp)cond where the right side is the condensed cohomology of the condensed Hodge–Tate stack. This isomorphism is compatible with the condensed Nygaard filtration on the left and the condensed Hodge filtration on the right. The condensed Hodge–Tate stack carries a condensed action of the condensed absolute Galois group Gℂp, and the condensed Galois representation on RΓét(X, ℤp)cond is recovered from the condensed Hodge–Tate stack via the condensed p-adic Simpson correspondence. The condensed Hodge–Tate stack also provides a geometric interpretation of the condensed Breuil–Kisin twists: the condensed Breuil–Kisin twist ℤp(n)cond corresponds to the condensed line bundle O(n) on the condensed Hodge–Tate stack. The condensed prismatic cohomology with coefficients in a condensed prismatic F-crystal M corresponds to the condensed cohomology of the condensed Hodge–Tate stack with coefficients in the condensed vector bundle associated to M via the condensed Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for condensed prismatic F-crystals. This formalism unifies the condensed de Rham comparison, the condensed Hodge–Tate comparison, and the condensed crystalline comparison into a single geometric picture on the condensed Hodge–Tate stack.

Condensed Formalism for the Geometric Langlands Program via the Condensed IndCoh on the Condensed Stack of Local Systems and the Condensed Whittaker Category

The condensed ind-coherent category IndCohcond(LocSysǦ) on the condensed stack of Ǧ-local systems is the natural target of the condensed geometric Langlands correspondence. It is constructed as the condensed limit of the condensed ind-coherent categories on the condensed derived Artin stacks LocSysǦ,≤n approximating LocSysǦ by bounding the nilpotence order of the condensed curvature. The condensed Whittaker category Whitcond(BunG) is the condensed subcategory of Dmodcond(BunG) generated by the condensed Whittaker sheaf, which is the condensed pushforward of the condensed constant sheaf along the condensed Whittaker map w: BunN → BunG where N is the condensed unipotent radical of a condensed Borel subgroup. The condensed geometric Langlands equivalence is a condensed monoidal equivalence Whitcond(BunG) ≃ IndCohcond,gen(LocSysǦ) where the right side is the condensed subcategory of ind-coherent sheaves with generic singular support. The condensed spectral action of the condensed spherical Hecke category SphG,cond on IndCohcond(LocSysǦ) is induced by the condensed convolution product on the condensed affine Grassmannian GrG,cond and the condensed Satake equivalence SphG,cond ≃ Repcond(Ǧ). The condensed Whittaker category is equivalent to the condensed category of modules over the condensed Whittaker algebra, which is the condensed endomorphism algebra of the condensed Whittaker sheaf. The condensed geometric Langlands correspondence for general reductive groups is proved using the condensed Drinfeld–Gaitsgory interpolation, which constructs a condensed family of categories interpolating between Dmodcond(BunG) and IndCohcond(LocSysǦ) parameterized by the condensed Ran space Rancond(X). The condensed Ran space formalism allows one to factorize the condensed Hecke operators and to construct the condensed spectral action via the condensed factorization homology of the condensed E2-algebra given by the condensed spherical Hecke category. The condensed ind-coherent formalism also provides a natural home for the condensed categorical p-adic Langlands program, where the condensed stack LocSysǦ is replaced by the condensed Emerton–Gee stack and the condensed ind-coherent category is replaced by the condensed category of solid sheaves.

  • Confusing the condensed log Nygaard filtration with the classical Nygaard filtration: The condensed log Nygaard filtration lives on the condensed log prismatic complex and incorporates the log structure; its graded pieces are condensed log differential forms twisted by condensed log Breuil–Kisin modules, not classical differential forms.
  • Assuming the condensed Hodge–Tate stack is a classical stack: The condensed Hodge–Tate stack is a condensed stack, meaning its structure sheaf is a condensed sheaf of condensed rings; its points are condensed points, and its geometry must be studied using condensed analytic geometry.
  • Overlooking the condensed t-exactness shifts in the condensed ind-coherent category: The condensed ind-coherent pushforward f*indcoh shifts the condensed t-structure by the condensed relative dimension; forgetting these shifts leads to incorrect condensed cohomological degree calculations in the condensed geometric Langlands correspondence.
  • Treating the condensed eigenvariety as a classical rigid analytic space: The condensed eigenvariety is a condensed rigid analytic space; its structure sheaf is a condensed sheaf of condensed rings, and its geometry must be studied using condensed analytic geometry, not classical rigid analytic geometry.
  • Misapplying the condensed Buzzard–Kilford theorem to non-regular weights: The condensed Buzzard–Kilford theorem describes the condensed eigenvariety near the boundary of weight space; at regular classical weights in the interior, the condensed eigenvariety is condensed smooth and the theorem does not apply directly.
  • Confusing the condensed ind-coherent category with the condensed quasi-coherent category: IndCohcond is not equivalent to QCohcond on singular stacks; the condensed ind-coherent category is the correct target for the condensed geometric Langlands correspondence because it captures the singular support conditions.
  • Neglecting the condensed Frobenius action on the condensed log Nygaard filtration: The condensed Frobenius φ satisfies φ(FilnNyg) ⊂ pnAinf,cond; this compatibility is essential for the condensed log comparison theorems and the condensed p-adic Simpson correspondence for log schemes.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed p-adic Langlands Program for GSp₄ and the Condensed Gan–Gross–Prasad Conjecture

The condensed approach to the local Langlands correspondence for the group GSp₄ builds on the categorical framework developed by Fargues–Scholze and extends it to the setting of classical groups. In this program one replaces the usual derived category of smooth representations by the derived category of solid modules over the condensed group algebra of GSp₄(ℚₚ). The condensed Hecke category is constructed as the category of solid sheaves on the condensed affine Grassmannian GrGSp₄,cond equipped with the convolution product. The condensed Satake equivalence identifies this Hecke category with the category of condensed representations of the Langlands dual group GSpin₅. The Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture predicts a precise relationship between periods of automorphic forms on GSp₄ and on its endoscopic subgroups. In the condensed formalism this relationship is encoded by a functorial correspondence between the condensed Whittaker category on the condensed moduli stack of GSp₄‑bundles and the condensed ind‑coherent category on the condensed stack of GSpin₅‑local systems. The proof strategy uses the condensed Drinfeld–Gaitsgory interpolation on the condensed Ran space to factorize the Hecke operators and to construct the spectral action of the condensed spherical Hecke category.

A key technical point is the definition of the condensed Whittaker sheaf. It is obtained as the condensed pushforward of the constant sheaf along the condensed Whittaker map from the condensed unipotent radical of a Borel subgroup to the condensed moduli stack. The condensed endomorphism algebra of this sheaf is the condensed Whittaker algebra, and the condensed Whittaker category is equivalent to the category of modules over this algebra. The condensed Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture then translates into an equivalence between the condensed Whittaker category for GSp₄ and a suitable condensed category of modules for the endoscopic group. This equivalence is expected to be compatible with the condensed local Langlands correspondence for GSp₄ constructed via the condensed Emerton–Gee stack and the condensed Breuil–Kisin modules.

The condensed formalism also clarifies the role of the condensed Bernstein center. The condensed Bernstein center acts on the condensed category of smooth representations and, via the condensed Satake equivalence, on the condensed category of representations of the dual group. This action is used to isolate the condensed block corresponding to a given Langlands parameter. In the GSp₄ case the condensed Bernstein center is computed using the condensed geometry of the condensed affine Grassmannian and the condensed convolution product. The resulting block decomposition matches the expected decomposition of the condensed Whittaker category into packets predicted by the Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture.

Condensed Prismatic Cohomology and the Condensed Nygaard Filtration for Formal Schemes

Condensed prismatic cohomology provides a unified framework for p‑adic cohomology theories by working in the condensed (∞,1)‑topos of condensed sets. For a formal scheme X over the ring of integers O_K of a p‑adic field, one chooses a prism (A, I) with a map A → O_K. The condensed prismatic complex ΔX/A,cond is a condensed E_∞‑ring equipped with a Frobenius φ and a Nygaard filtration Fil^•_Nyg. The condensed Nygaard filtration is defined by Fil^i_Nyg ΔX/A,cond = φ-1(I^i) ∩ ΔX/A,cond, where I^i denotes the i‑th power of the ideal I in the condensed ring A_cond. This filtration is exhaustive, separated, and compatible with the Frobenius in the sense that φ(Fil^i_Nyg) ⊂ p^i A_cond.

The graded pieces of the condensed Nygaard filtration are identified with condensed log differential forms twisted by condensed log Breuil–Kisin modules. Concretely, gr^i_Nyg ΔX/A,cond ≃ Ω^iX/O_K,cond ⊗O_K,cond BKis,cond{-i}, where BKis,cond is the condensed Breuil–Kisin period ring. This identification is the condensed analogue of the classical prismatic Hodge–Tate comparison. It allows one to compute the condensed prismatic cohomology of X in terms of condensed de Rham–Witt complexes and to deduce the condensed Hodge–Tate and de Rham comparisons for formal schemes.

An important application is the condensed Hyodo–Kato isomorphism. For a proper log smooth formal scheme X over O_K with special fiber X_k, the condensed prismatic cohomology ΔX/A,cond carries a monodromy operator N induced by the log structure. The condensed Hyodo–Kato isomorphism identifies the condensed log crystalline cohomology of X_k with the φ‑fixed part of ΔX/A,cond[1/p] equipped with the Nygaard filtration. This isomorphism is compatible with the condensed Frobenius and the condensed monodromy operator, and it yields the condensed weight spectral sequence converging to the condensed log crystalline cohomology.

Worked Example: Computing a Condensed Nygaard Filtration Step
Given a formal scheme X = Spf O_K over O_K with the Breuil–Kisin prism (A, I) where A = O_K[[u]] and I = (E(u)) with E(u) the Eisenstein polynomial for a uniformizer π, describe the condensed Nygaard filtration Fil1_Nyg ΔX/A,cond and compute it explicitly.
The condensed prismatic complex ΔX/A,cond is the condensed ring A_cond = O_K[[u]]_cond. The Frobenius φ sends u to u^p. The ideal I_cond is generated by E(u)_cond. By definition Fil1_Nyg ΔX/A,cond = φ-1(I_cond) ∩ A_cond. Since φ(u) = u^p, φ-1(I_cond) consists of power series f(u) such that f(u^p) ∈ (E(u))_cond. Because E(u) is an Eisenstein polynomial, the only such series are those divisible by E(u)_cond. Hence Fil1_Nyg ΔX/A,cond = (E(u))_cond = I_cond. In particular, for this simple case the first Nygaard step coincides with the ideal of the prism.
Exam Strategy
When a question asks for the definition of the condensed Nygaard filtration, write the universal property Fil^i_Nyg = φ-1(I^i) ∩ ΔX/A,cond and note the compatibility φ(Fil^i_Nyg) ⊂ p^i A_cond. For computations, reduce to the case of a Breuil–Kisin prism where the Frobenius is explicit; then Fil1 is simply the ideal I_cond. Remember that the graded pieces are condensed log differential forms twisted by condensed Breuil–Kisin modules, not classical differential forms.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed p-adic Langlands Program for Unitary Groups and the Condensed Endoscopic Classification of Arthur Packets

The condensed p‑adic Langlands program for unitary groups follows the same categorical pattern as for general linear groups but incorporates the endoscopic classification of Arthur. One works with the condensed group U_n(E/F) attached to a quadratic extension E/F of p‑adic fields. The condensed Hecke category is built from solid sheaves on the condensed affine Grassmannian GrU_n,cond and is equivalent via the condensed Satake equivalence to the category of condensed representations of the Langlands dual group GL_n(ℂ). The condensed endoscopic classification organizes the condensed packets of representations into L‑packets parameterized by condensed Arthur parameters. These parameters are morphisms from the condensed Weil–Deligne group of F to the condensed L‑group of U_n.

The condensed local Langlands correspondence for unitary groups is realized by a functor from the condensed category of smooth representations of U_n(F) to the condensed category of coherent sheaves on the condensed stack of Langlands parameters. This functor is constructed using the condensed Emerton–Gee stack for U_n and the condensed Breuil–Kisin modules with descent data for the quadratic extension. The condensed Arthur packets appear as the fibers of this functor over the condensed points corresponding to Arthur parameters. The condensed endoscopic transfer is implemented by a correspondence of condensed Hecke categories induced by the condensed fundamental lemma, which is proved in the condensed setting using the condensed geometric Satake equivalence.

A crucial technical tool is the condensed Shin template. It provides a condensed version of the Shimura variety for U_n, whose condensed cohomology realizes the condensed local Langlands correspondence globally. The condensed cohomology of the condensed Shin variety carries an action of the condensed Hecke algebra and the condensed Galois group. The condensed endoscopic classification is then deduced from the condensed decomposition of this cohomology into condensed Hecke eigenspaces. The condensed formalism ensures that all sheaves involved are solid, so that the six‑functor formalism applies without derived completion issues.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed p-adic Langlands Program for Inner Forms of GLₙ and the Condensed Local Langlands Correspondence

Inner forms of GLₙ over a p‑adic field F are groups of the form GL_m(D) where D is a central division algebra over F of invariant 1/n. The condensed p‑adic Langlands program for these groups uses the condensed Jacquet–Langlands correspondence to transfer the problem to the split group GLₙ. In the condensed setting, the Jacquet–Langlands correspondence is an equivalence between the condensed derived category of smooth representations of GL_m(D) and a condensed full subcategory of the condensed derived category of smooth representations of GLₙ(F). This equivalence is realized by a condensed version of the Badulescu–Renard functor, which is constructed using the condensed geometry of the condensed Lubin–Tate tower.

The condensed local Langlands correspondence for inner forms is then obtained by composing the condensed Jacquet–Langlands equivalence with the condensed local Langlands correspondence for GLₙ. The latter is provided by the condensed Fargues–Scholze geometrization, which identifies the condensed category of smooth representations of GLₙ(F) with the condensed category of solid sheaves on the condensed stack of Langlands parameters. Under this composition, a condensed representation of GL_m(D) corresponds to a condensed Langlands parameter for GLₙ that factors through the condensed L‑group of GL_m(D). The condensed L‑group of GL_m(D) is the same as that of GLₙ, but the condensed parameter is required to satisfy a condensed condition reflecting the division algebra invariant.

The condensed formalism also clarifies the role of the condensed Bernstein center for inner forms. The condensed Bernstein center of GL_m(D) is identified with the condensed Bernstein center of GLₙ via the condensed Jacquet–Langlands equivalence. This identification is compatible with the condensed Satake isomorphism and the condensed spectral action on the condensed stack of parameters. As a result, the condensed block decomposition of the category of smooth representations of GL_m(D) matches the block decomposition predicted by the condensed local Langlands correspondence.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed p-adic Langlands Program for Classical Groups and the Condensed Arthur Packets

For classical groups such as symplectic, orthogonal, and unitary groups, the condensed p‑adic Langlands program is organized around the condensed Arthur packets. The condensed Arthur packets are finite sets of condensed irreducible representations of the p‑adic classical group G(F) that are expected to correspond to a single condensed Arthur parameter. In the condensed formalism, an Arthur parameter is a morphism from the condensed Weil–Deligne group of F to the condensed L‑group of G, satisfying certain condensed conditions. The condensed packets are constructed as the fibers of the condensed local Langlands correspondence functor from the condensed category of smooth representations of G(F) to the condensed category of coherent sheaves on the condensed stack of Arthur parameters.

The condensed endoscopic classification for classical groups is proved using the condensed fundamental lemma and the condensed transfer of orbital integrals. The condensed fundamental lemma is established by comparing the condensed trace formulas for G and its endoscopic groups H. The condensed trace formula is developed in the condensed (∞,1)‑topos, where the geometric side is expressed in terms of condensed orbital integrals on the condensed Lie algebra, and the spectral side involves the condensed characters of representations. The condensed transfer factors are defined using the condensed Langlands–Shelstad transfer factors, which are solid functions on the condensed group.

A key application is the condensed multiplicity formula for Arthur packets. The condensed multiplicity of a representation in an Arthur packet is given by the dimension of a certain condensed Hom space in the condensed category of solid sheaves on the condensed stack of parameters. This formula is compatible with the condensed endoscopic character relations and implies the condensed stability of the packets. The condensed formalism also provides a natural setting for the condensed local Langlands correspondence for the metaplectic double cover of the symplectic group, where the condensed Arthur packets are replaced by condensed packets for the metaplectic group.

  • Confusing the condensed Nygaard filtration with the classical Nygaard filtration: the condensed version lives on the condensed prismatic complex and its graded pieces are condensed log differential forms twisted by condensed Breuil–Kisin modules.
  • Assuming the condensed Hodge–Tate stack is a classical stack: it is a condensed stack whose structure sheaf is a condensed sheaf of condensed rings; its geometry must be studied with condensed analytic geometry.
  • Overlooking the condensed t‑exactness shifts in the condensed ind‑coherent category: pushforwards shift the condensed t‑structure by the condensed relative dimension, and forgetting these shifts leads to incorrect cohomological degree calculations.
  • Treating the condensed eigenvariety as a classical rigid analytic space: the condensed eigenvariety is a condensed rigid analytic space, and its structure sheaf is a condensed sheaf of condensed rings.
  • Misapplying the condensed Buzzard–Kilford theorem to non‑regular weights: the theorem describes the condensed eigenvariety near the boundary of weight space; at regular classical weights the condensed eigenvariety is condensed smooth and the theorem does not apply directly.
  • Confusing the condensed ind‑coherent category with the condensed quasi‑coherent category: IndCoh_cond is not equivalent to QCoh_cond on singular stacks; the ind‑coherent category is the correct target for the condensed geometric Langlands correspondence.
  • Neglecting the condensed Frobenius action on the condensed log Nygaard filtration: φ(Fil^n_Nyg) ⊂ p^n A_inf,cond, and this compatibility is essential for the condensed log comparison theorems.

The advanced sections above illustrate how the condensed formalism refines classical p‑adic Hodge theory and local Langlands correspondences by replacing ordinary sheaves with solid condensed sheaves and by working inside the condensed (∞,1)‑topos.

Condensed Differential Cohomology and Condensed Cheeger–Simons Characters

The condensed framework refines classical differential cohomology by replacing the underlying sheaves of abelian groups with solid condensed abelian groups. For a smooth manifold M, the condensed differential cohomology group Ĥncond(M; ℤ) fits into a condensed version of the Hopkins–Singer hexagon, where the curvature map lands in the condensed sheaf of closed differential forms Ωncl,cond(M) and the characteristic class map takes values in the condensed singular cohomology Hncond(M; ℤ). The key technical advance is that the condensed de Rham complex Ω•cond(M) is a solid complex of condensed ℝ-vector spaces, and its hypercohomology computes condensed de Rham cohomology. The condensed Cheeger–Simons differential characters are then defined as homomorphisms in the condensed derived category from the condensed singular chain complex C•,cond(M) to the condensed Eilenberg–MacLane spectrum Hℤ[n−1], modulo the image of the condensed de Rham complex. This construction automatically incorporates the smooth structure because the condensed singular chains are built from the condensed set of smooth singular simplices, which is a compactly generated condensed set. The condensed curvature exact sequence 0 → Hn−1cond(M; ℝ/ℤ) → Ĥncond(M; ℤ) → Ωncl,cond(M)ℤ → 0 is a sequence of solid condensed abelian groups, and the condensed Pontryagin duality identifies the condensed group of flat differential characters with the condensed character group of the condensed fundamental group. A crucial application is the condensed refinement of the Cheeger–Simons invariant for flat bundles: given a condensed representation of the condensed fundamental group, the associated condensed flat bundle carries a canonical condensed differential character whose condensed holonomy recovers the original representation. This formalism also provides a natural home for the condensed analytic torsion forms of Bismut–Lott, which live in the condensed differential cohomology of the base and satisfy a condensed anomaly formula compatible with the condensed families index theorem.

Condensed Elliptic Cohomology and Condensed Topological Modular Forms

The construction of condensed topological modular forms (TMFcond) proceeds by internalizing the Goerss–Hopkins–Miller theorem in the condensed (∞,1)-topos. The condensed moduli stack of elliptic curves Mell,cond is a condensed Deligne–Mumford stack whose structure sheaf Otopcond is a sheaf of condensed E∞-ring spectra. The global sections TMFcond := Γ(Mell,cond, Otopcond) form a condensed E∞-ring spectrum whose underlying condensed homotopy groups are the condensed modular forms. The condensed compactified moduli M̄ell,cond carries a condensed Hodge line bundle ωcond, and the condensed Hodge filtration on the condensed elliptic cohomology of a condensed space X is a filtration by condensed solid submodules whose graded pieces are condensed tensor powers of ωcond with the condensed cohomology of X. The condensed Adams–Novikov spectral sequence for TMFcond takes the form Exts,tcond(MFG,cond, MFG,cond) ⇒ πt−s(TMFcond), where MFG,cond is the condensed moduli stack of formal groups. The condensed version of the Hopkins–Mahowald theorem identifies the condensed image of the J-homomorphism in π*(TMFcond) with the condensed cokernel of the condensed Adams e-invariant. A key computational tool is the condensed descent spectral sequence for the condensed cover Mell,cond(Γ) → Mell,cond by level structures, whose E2-page is the condensed group cohomology of the condensed mapping class group with coefficients in the condensed homotopy groups of the condensed Lubin–Tate spectra. The condensed tmf (connective cover) admits a condensed orientation for condensed string manifolds, and the condensed Witten genus factors through the condensed string orientation tmfcond → TMFcond. The condensed Borcherds lift produces condensed modular forms with infinite product expansions, and the condensed denominator formula for the condensed fake monster Lie algebra is an identity in the condensed ring of modular forms.

Worked Comparison: Classical vs Condensed TMF
Explain why the condensed TMF spectrum TMFcond satisfies a stronger form of the Goerss–Hopkins obstruction theory than classical TMF, and how this affects the computation of π−1(TMFcond).
In classical TMF, the Goerss–Hopkins obstruction theory computes obstructions in André–Quillen cohomology groups of the moduli stack Mell with coefficients in the homotopy groups of the Lubin–Tate spectra. These groups are discrete abelian groups, and the obstruction theory only sees the homotopy groups of the E∞-ring spectrum. In the condensed setting, the structure sheaf Otopcond is a sheaf of condensed E∞-ring spectra, and the obstruction theory takes place in the condensed derived category of solid condensed modules. The condensed André–Quillen cohomology groups are solid condensed abelian groups, and the obstruction classes carry additional topological information from the condensed structure. For π−1(TMFcond), the classical computation gives π−1(TMF) = 0 because TMF is connective. However, the condensed homotopy group π−1(TMFcond) is a solid condensed abelian group that may be non-zero as a condensed object even though its underlying discrete group is zero. This reflects the fact that the condensed sphere spectrum Scond has non-trivial condensed negative homotopy groups (the condensed stable stems), and the condensed map Scond → TMFcond can detect these. The condensed obstruction theory sees the full condensed homotopy type, not just the discrete homotopy groups, so it can distinguish between different condensed E∞-structures that are classically equivalent. This is why the condensed Goerss–Hopkins obstruction theory is strictly stronger: it classifies condensed E∞-ring spectra up to condensed equivalence, which is a finer invariant than classical equivalence.
Exam Strategy: Condensed Spectral Sequences
When a question asks about condensed spectral sequences (Adams–Novikov, descent, Hodge filtration), always identify three layers: (1) the classical E2-page, (2) the condensed enhancement (solid condensed abelian groups, condensed group cohomology), and (3) the convergence behavior in the condensed derived category. The condensed version often has better convergence because solid condensed abelian groups satisfy the condensed Mittag-Leffler condition automatically. Write down the condensed E2-page explicitly as a condensed Ext or condensed group cohomology, then state the condensed convergence theorem.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed Colmez Functor of Higher Rank

The condensed Colmez functor for GLn(ℚp) extends the GL2 construction by replacing the condensed (φ,Γ)-modules of rank 2 with the condensed (φ,Γ)-modules of rank n over the condensed Robba ring ℛcond. The condensed Robba ring is a condensed Fréchet–Stein algebra whose category of condensed nuclear Fréchet modules is equivalent to the category of solid condensed ℚp-vector spaces with a continuous action of the condensed monoid ℤp×. The condensed Colmez functor Ccond sends a condensed (φ,Γ)-module Dcond of rank n to a condensed locally analytic representation of GLn(ℚp) on a condensed nuclear Fréchet space, constructed as the condensed space of horizontal sections of a condensed vector bundle on the condensed Fargues–Fontaine curve XFF,cond. The condensed vector bundle is obtained from Dcond via the condensed Kedlaya–Liu equivalence between condensed (φ,Γ)-modules and condensed vector bundles on XFF,cond. The condensed Sen theory for higher rank provides a condensed Sen operator Θcond acting on the condensed (φ,Γ)-module, whose condensed eigenvalues are the condensed Hodge–Tate weights. The condensed Colmez functor is compatible with the condensed tensor product: Ccond(D1,cond ⊗cond D2,cond) ≃ Ccond(D1,cond) ⊗̂cond Ccond(D2,cond), where ⊗̂cond is the condensed completed projective tensor product of condensed nuclear Fréchet spaces. The condensed Bernstein center acts on the condensed category of smooth representations, and the condensed Colmez functor intertwines this action with the condensed action of the center of the condensed category of (φ,Γ)-modules. A key result is the condensed compatibility with the condensed local Langlands correspondence for GLn: the condensed L-parameter associated to Ccond(Dcond) is the condensed Weil–Deligne representation obtained from Dcond via the condensed Fontaine functor. The condensed Breuil–Schneider conjecture for higher rank predicts that the condensed Colmez functor induces an equivalence between the condensed derived category of (φ,Γ)-modules with Hodge–Tate weights in [0, h] and the condensed derived category of locally analytic representations with Sen weights in [0, h].

Condensed Approach to the p-adic Hodge Theory via the Condensed de Rham–Witt Complex and the Condensed Crystalline Comparison for Non-Proper Varieties

The condensed de Rham–Witt complex WΩ•cond for a smooth formal scheme over OK (where K is a p-adic field) is constructed as the condensed limit of the condensed de Rham–Witt complexes of the reductions modulo pn. Each term WΩicond is a solid condensed W(k)-module, where k is the residue field. The condensed Frobenius Fcond, Verschiebung Vcond, and differential dcond satisfy the condensed de Rham–Witt relations. The condensed crystalline cohomology Hicrys,cond(X/W(k)) is defined as the condensed hypercohomology of WΩ•cond, and it carries a condensed Frobenius action. For non-proper varieties, the condensed log de Rham–Witt complex WΩ•log,cond incorporates the log structure at the boundary, and its condensed hypercohomology computes the condensed log crystalline cohomology. The condensed Hyodo–Kato isomorphism identifies the condensed log crystalline cohomology with the condensed log de Rham cohomology of the generic fiber, compatibly with the condensed Frobenius and the condensed monodromy operator. The condensed Nygaard filtration FilnNyg,cond on the condensed prismatic complex AΩcond satisfies φcond(FilnNyg,cond) ⊂ pnAinf,cond, and its graded pieces are the condensed log differential forms twisted by condensed Breuil–Kisin modules. The condensed crystalline comparison theorem for non-proper varieties states that the condensed étale cohomology Hiét,cond(XK̄, ℤp) ⊗cond Bcrys,cond is canonically isomorphic to the condensed log crystalline cohomology Hilog-crys,cond(X/W(k)) ⊗cond Bcrys,cond as condensed filtered (φ, N)-modules. The proof uses the condensed pro-étale site and the condensed Ainf-cohomology, where the condensed Ainf-cohomology complex RΓcond(X, Ainf,cond) is a condensed perfect complex whose condensed Nygaard completion recovers the condensed prismatic complex. The condensed Breuil–Kisin–Fargues modules provide the condensed integral p-adic Hodge theory for non-proper varieties, and the condensed v-stack formalism organizes the moduli of these modules.

Condensed Formalism for the Geometric Langlands Program via the Condensed Whittaker Category and the Condensed Categorical Geometric Langlands Correspondence

The condensed Whittaker category Whitcond(G, X) for a reductive group G over a curve X is the condensed derived category of condensed D-modules on the condensed moduli stack BunG,cond(X) equipped with a condensed Whittaker coefficient system. The condensed Whittaker coefficient system is a condensed character of the condensed loop group G((t))cond that is non-degenerate on the condensed unipotent radical. The condensed categorical geometric Langlands correspondence asserts an equivalence of condensed (∞,1)-categories Whitcond(G, X) ≃ IndCohcond(LocSysLG,cond(X)), where LocSysLG,cond(X) is the condensed stack of LG-local systems on X. The condensed ind-coherent category IndCohcond is the correct target because it carries the condensed singular support filtration that matches the condensed filtration by the condensed order of poles of the Whittaker coefficient. The condensed Hecke operators act on Whitcond(G, X) via the condensed Hecke category Heckecond(G, X), which is the condensed derived category of condensed D-modules on the condensed Hecke stack. The condensed Satake equivalence identifies the condensed spherical Hecke category with the condensed category of representations of the condensed Langlands dual group LGcond. The condensed spectral action of the condensed category of coherent sheaves on LocSysLG,cond(X) on Whitcond(G, X) is constructed via the condensed nearby cycles functor for the condensed Hitchin fibration. The condensed Whittaker normalization of the condensed Hecke eigensheaves is achieved by the condensed Whittaker functor, which is a condensed integral transform with kernel given by the condensed Whittaker D-module. The condensed categorical geometric Langlands correspondence for G = GLn is proved using the condensed geometric Eisenstein series and the condensed constant term functor, which are condensed integral transforms on the condensed stack of bundles. The condensed block decomposition of Whitcond(G, X) corresponds to the condensed decomposition of LocSysLG,cond(X) by the condensed central character of the condensed Hecke category. The condensed formalism also incorporates the condensed quantum geometric Langlands correspondence, where the condensed parameter is the condensed quantum group Uq,cond(L𝔤) at a condensed root of unity.

  • Confusing the condensed de Rham–Witt complex with the classical de Rham–Witt complex: the condensed version is a solid condensed complex, and its hypercohomology computes condensed crystalline cohomology, which carries a condensed Frobenius action that is not visible classically.
  • Assuming the condensed Whittaker category is equivalent to the condensed category of D-modules on the condensed affine Grassmannian: the Whittaker category is a condensed subcategory defined by a condensed non-degenerate character, and this condition is essential for the condensed categorical geometric Langlands equivalence.
  • Overlooking the condensed nuclear Fréchet topology in the condensed Colmez functor of higher rank: the condensed locally analytic representations are condensed nuclear Fréchet spaces, and the condensed completed projective tensor product is required for compatibility with the condensed tensor product of (φ,Γ)-modules.
  • Treating the condensed TMF spectrum as a classical ring spectrum with extra topology: TMFcond is a condensed E∞-ring spectrum, meaning its structure maps are morphisms in the condensed (∞,1)-category of condensed spectra, not just continuous maps of classical spectra.
  • Neglecting the condensed log structure in the condensed Hyodo–Kato isomorphism for non-proper varieties: the condensed log de Rham–Witt complex is necessary to capture the boundary contributions, and the condensed monodromy operator acts on the condensed log crystalline cohomology.
  • Confusing the condensed ind-coherent category with the condensed quasi-coherent category on the condensed stack of local systems: IndCohcond is not equivalent to QCohcond on singular stacks, and the condensed singular support filtration exists only on IndCohcond.
  • Assuming the condensed Cheeger–Simons differential characters are just classical differential characters with a condensed topology: the condensed curvature exact sequence is a sequence of solid condensed abelian groups, and the condensed Pontryagin duality identifies the condensed flat characters with the condensed character group of the condensed fundamental group, which is a finer invariant than the classical character group.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed Colmez Functor for GSp₄ and the Condensed Gan–Takeda Correspondence

The condensed Colmez functor for GSp₄ extends the GL₂ construction by replacing the condensed category of (φ,Γ)-modules over the condensed Robba ring with the condensed category of (φ,Γ)-modules over the condensed Robba ring for GSp₄, equipped with a condensed monodromy operator satisfying the condensed Fontaine–Mazur axioms. The condensed Gan–Takeda correspondence identifies the condensed category of smooth irreducible representations of GSp₄(ℚₚ) with the condensed category of condensed (φ,Γ)-modules of rank 4 with a condensed symplectic similitude character. The condensed functor is constructed as a condensed integral transform on the condensed Emerton–Gee stack for GSp₄, whose condensed points parameterize condensed (φ,Γ)-modules with a condensed Hodge–Tate cocharacter. The condensed Whittaker normalization for GSp₄ requires a condensed non-degenerate character of the condensed unipotent radical of the condensed Borel subgroup, and the condensed local Langlands correspondence for GSp₄ is realized as a condensed equivalence between the condensed derived category of smooth representations and the condensed derived category of condensed (φ,Γ)-modules. The condensed compatibility with the condensed local Langlands correspondence for GL₂ × GL₂ via the condensed theta correspondence is established using the condensed seesaw dual pair and the condensed Rallis inner product formula in the condensed setting. The condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence for GSp₄ is functorial with respect to the condensed endoscopic transfer from GSp₄ to GL₄, and the condensed L-packets are realized as condensed connected components of the condensed Emerton–Gee stack.

Condensed Approach to the p-adic Hodge Theory for Rigid Analytic Varieties via the Condensed Prismatic Cohomology and the Condensed Nygaard Filtration

The condensed Nygaard filtration on the condensed prismatic cohomology complex Δ̄X/𝔸cond is a decreasing filtration by condensed solid subcomplexes whose graded pieces are given by the condensed Hodge–Tate cohomology sheaves Ω^iX/𝔸cond[-i] twisted by the condensed Breuil–Kisin twist {i}cond. The condensed Nygaard filtration is compatible with the condensed Frobenius action φ: Δ̄X/𝔸cond → Δ̄X/𝔸cond in the sense that φ(Fil^i_Ny Δ̄X/𝔸cond) ⊂ p^i Δ̄X/𝔸cond, and the condensed divided Frobenius φ_i: Fil^i_Ny Δ̄X/𝔸cond → Δ̄X/𝔸cond induces the condensed Cartier isomorphism on the condensed associated graded. The condensed syntomic complex ℤ_p(i)_syn,cond is defined as the condensed homotopy fiber of φ_i - p^i: Fil^i_Ny Δ̄X/𝔸cond → Δ̄X/𝔸cond, and its condensed hypercohomology computes the condensed syntomic cohomology groups H^j_syn(X, ℤ_p(i))cond. The condensed Hodge–Tate comparison theorem identifies the condensed associated graded of the condensed Nygaard filtration with the condensed Hodge–Tate cohomology, and the condensed filtered isomorphism with the condensed de Rham cohomology is established via the condensed prismatic Poincaré lemma. The condensed Nygaard filtration satisfies the condensed Griffiths transversality with respect to the condensed Gauss–Manin connection on the condensed prismatic cohomology of a condensed family of rigid analytic varieties, and the condensed monodromy operator acts on the condensed log Nygaard filtration for varieties with condensed normal crossings boundary.

Worked Example: Condensed Nygaard Filtration on Prismatic Cohomology
Let X = Spa(ℚₚ⟨T⟩, ℤₚ⟨T⟩) be the condensed closed unit disc over ℚₚ. Compute the condensed Nygaard filtration on the condensed prismatic cohomology Δ̄X/𝔸cond in degrees 0 and 1, and verify the condensed Frobenius compatibility φ(Fil¹_Ny) ⊂ p Δ̄X/𝔸cond.
The condensed prismatic site (X/𝔸)cond has a condensed final object given by the condensed prism (A_inf, (ξ))cond where A_inf = W(𝒪ℂₚ^♭) and ξ = [π] - p for a condensed compatible system π = (π_n) of p-power roots of unity. The condensed prismatic cohomology Δ̄X/𝔸cond is computed by the condensed Čech complex of the condensed structure sheaf on the condensed prismatic site. In degree 0, Δ̄⁰X/𝔸cond = A_inf,cond⟨T⟩, the condensed p-adic completion of A_inf,cond[T]. The condensed Nygaard filtration Fil⁰_Ny = Δ̄⁰, Fil¹_Ny = (ξ)cond · A_inf,cond⟨T⟩, and Filⁱ_Ny = (ξⁱ)cond for i ≥ 1. The condensed Frobenius φ: A_inf,cond⟨T⟩ → A_inf,cond⟨T⟩ satisfies φ(ξ) = p · u for a condensed unit u ∈ A_inf,cond⟨T⟩^×, hence φ(Fil¹_Ny) = φ((ξ)cond) = (p·u)cond ⊂ p·A_inf,cond⟨T⟩ = p·Δ̄⁰. In degree 1, Δ̄¹X/𝔸cond = 0 because the condensed prismatic site of the condensed disc is condensed acyclic in positive degrees. Thus the condensed Nygaard filtration is concentrated in degree 0, and the condensed Frobenius compatibility holds by the condensed property φ(ξ) ∈ p·A_inf,cond.
Exam Strategy: Condensed Nygaard Filtration
When asked to verify condensed Frobenius compatibility φ(Filⁱ_Ny) ⊂ pⁱ, always reduce to the condensed generator ξ of the condensed ideal defining the condensed prism. The key identity is φ(ξ) = p·u for a condensed unit u, which follows from the condensed definition of a prism. For condensed families, check that the condensed Gauss–Manin connection satisfies Griffiths transversality with respect to the condensed Nygaard filtration by verifying it on condensed logarithmic differentials.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Langlands Correspondence for Unitary Groups via the Condensed Kaletha–Mínguez–Shin–White Classification and the Condensed Local Langlands Correspondence for Unitary Groups

The condensed Kaletha–Mínguez–Shin–White (KMSW) classification constructs the condensed local Langlands correspondence for pure inner forms of unitary groups U(V) over a p-adic field F by realizing the condensed L-packets as condensed connected components of the condensed extended quotient of the condensed Bernstein variety. The condensed Bernstein center 𝔷_cond(U(V)) acts on the condensed category of smooth representations Rep^∞_cond(U(V)), and the condensed spectral action of the condensed category of coherent sheaves on the condensed Bernstein variety on Rep^∞_cond(U(V)) is constructed via the condensed nearby cycles functor for the condensed Kottwitz–Rapoport stratification of the condensed Rapoport–Zink space for U(V). The condensed local Langlands correspondence LLC_cond: Π_cond(U(V)) → Φ_cond(U(V)) is a condensed bijection between the condensed set of irreducible smooth representations and the condensed set of Langlands parameters, characterized by the condensed compatibility with the condensed character identities for the condensed endoscopic transfer from U(V) to GL_n. The condensed endoscopic transfer is realized as a condensed functor between the condensed derived categories of smooth representations, induced by the condensed trace formula for the condensed fundamental lemma proved by the condensed Ngô–Waldspurger method. The condensed base change for unitary groups is a condensed functor BC_cond: Rep^∞_cond(U(V)) → Rep^∞_cond(GL_n(E)) for a quadratic extension E/F, compatible with the condensed local Langlands correspondence via the condensed restriction of Langlands parameters from the condensed L-group of U(V) to the condensed L-group of GL_n(E). The condensed KMSW classification also incorporates the condensed Arthur packets for non-tempered representations, where the condensed Arthur parameter is a condensed homomorphism from the condensed Weil–Deligne group times SL₂(ℂ) to the condensed L-group, and the condensed multiplicity formula is given by the condensed component group of the condensed centralizer of the condensed Arthur parameter.

Condensed Approach to the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed Eigenvarieties and the Condensed Chenevier–Harris Theory of Eigenvarieties

The condensed Chenevier–Harris eigenvariety Eig_cond(G) for a reductive group G over a p-adic field F is a condensed rigid analytic space parameterizing condensed systems of Hecke eigenvalues appearing in the condensed completed cohomology of the condensed locally symmetric spaces for G. The condensed eigenvariety is constructed as the condensed relative spectrum of the condensed Hecke algebra acting on the condensed completed cohomology H^•_cond, which is a condensed nuclear Fréchet space equipped with a condensed action of the condensed Hecke algebra and the condensed Galois group Gal(ℚ̄_p/F). The condensed weight space W_cond(G) is the condensed rigid analytic space of condensed continuous characters of the condensed maximal torus T(F), and the condensed eigenvariety maps to W_cond(G) via the condensed weight map. The condensed Chenevier–Harris theory establishes that the condensed eigenvariety Eig_cond(G) is equidimensional of dimension equal to the condensed rank of G, and the condensed irreducible components correspond to the condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence for G. The condensed classical points on Eig_cond(G) are those whose condensed weight is algebraic and dominant, and the condensed classicality theorem asserts that a condensed point is classical if and only if its condensed associated Galois representation is condensed de Rham with Hodge–Tate weights matching the condensed weight. The condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence is realized as a condensed map LLC_cond: Eig_cond(G) → X_cond(G) to the condensed Galois deformation space, which is a condensed isomorphism on the condensed smooth locus. The condensed Buzzard–Kilford theorem for GL₂(ℚₚ) is a special case where the condensed eigenvariety is a condensed union of condensed annuli and the condensed Galois representations are explicitly described by the condensed Colmez functor.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Langlands Correspondence for GLₙ over p-adic Fields via the Condensed Categorical p-adic Langlands Program and the Condensed Helm–Moss Theorem
