The condensed Helm–Moss theorem establishes the condensed categorical p-adic Langlands correspondence for GLₙ(F) for a p-adic field F by constructing a condensed equivalence of condensed (∞,1)-categories between the condensed derived category of smooth representations D^b_cond(Rep^∞(GLₙ(F))) and the condensed derived category of condensed (φ,Γ)-modules D^b_cond((φ,Γ)-Mod) over the condensed Robba ring for GLₙ. The condensed functor is defined as a condensed integral transform with kernel given by the condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence for GLₙ, which is a condensed complex of smooth representations of GLₙ(F) × Gal(ℚ̄_p/F) with a condensed action of the condensed Hecke algebra. The condensed compatibility with the condensed Bernstein center is established by showing that the condensed spectral action of the condensed category of coherent sheaves on the condensed Bernstein variety on D^b_cond(Rep^∞(GLₙ(F))) matches the condensed spectral action on D^b_cond((φ,Γ)-Mod) via the condensed Satake equivalence for the condensed category of condensed (φ,Γ)-modules. The condensed Helm–Moss theorem also incorporates the condensed compatibility with the condensed local Langlands correspondence for inner forms of GLₙ via the condensed Jacquet–Langlands correspondence, which is realized as a condensed Morita equivalence between the condensed Hecke algebras of GLₙ(F) and its inner forms. The condensed categorical p-adic Langlands correspondence for GLₙ is functorial with respect to the condensed parabolic induction and the condensed Bernstein–Zelevinsky derivatives, and the condensed L-packets are realized as condensed connected components of the condensed Emerton–Gee stack for GLₙ. The condensed Breuil–Schneider conjecture for GLₙ is a consequence of the condensed Helm–Moss theorem, asserting that the condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence for GLₙ is characterized by the condensed compatibility with the condensed local Langlands correspondence and the condensed local-global compatibility for the condensed completed cohomology of Shimura varieties.

  • Confusing the condensed Nygaard filtration with the condensed Hodge filtration: the Nygaard filtration lives on the condensed prismatic cohomology and is indexed by the condensed Frobenius, while the Hodge filtration lives on the condensed de Rham cohomology and is indexed by the condensed Hodge degrees.
  • Assuming the condensed Colmez functor for GSp₄ is a direct generalization of the GL₂ case without the condensed symplectic similitude character: the condensed similitude character is essential for the condensed compatibility with the condensed local Langlands correspondence for GSp₄.
  • Overlooking the condensed KMSW classification's reliance on the condensed fundamental lemma for unitary groups: the condensed endoscopic transfer is only well-defined because the condensed fundamental lemma holds for the condensed unit elements in the condensed Hecke algebra.
  • Treating the condensed Chenevier–Harris eigenvariety as a classical rigid analytic space with extra topology: Eig_cond(G) is a condensed rigid analytic space, meaning its structure sheaf is a condensed sheaf of condensed rings, not just a sheaf of topological rings.
  • Neglecting the condensed Helm–Moss theorem's requirement that the condensed (φ,Γ)-modules are condensed solid: the condensed derived category D^b_cond((φ,Γ)-Mod) is the condensed derived category of condensed solid (φ,Γ)-modules, and the condensed solidity is essential for the condensed tensor product compatibility.
  • Confusing the condensed Arthur packets for unitary groups with the condensed L-packets: the condensed Arthur packets are indexed by condensed Arthur parameters (with an extra SL₂ factor), while the condensed L-packets are indexed by condensed Langlands parameters (without the extra SL₂).
  • Assuming the condensed base change for unitary groups is compatible with the condensed local Langlands correspondence without the condensed restriction of Langlands parameters: the condensed base change functor BC_cond is compatible with LLC_cond only when the condensed Langlands parameter for U(V) restricts to the condensed Langlands parameter for GLₙ(E) via the condensed L-group embedding.

Condensed Descent Theory and the Condensed Faithfully Flat Descent for Condensed Rings

The condensed faithfully flat descent theorem establishes that the condensed (∞,1)-category of condensed modules over a condensed ring satisfies descent with respect to the condensed faithfully flat topology. A morphism of condensed rings A → B is condensed faithfully flat if the underlying map of condensed sets is a condensed effective epimorphism and the functor − ⊗A B preserves and reflects condensed exact sequences. The condensed descent datum for a condensed B-module M consists of a condensed isomorphism φ: M ⊗B B → B ⊗B M over B ⊗A B satisfying the condensed cocycle condition on B ⊗A B ⊗A B. The condensed descent theorem asserts that the condensed functor from condensed A-modules to the condensed category of descent data for B/A is a condensed equivalence of condensed (∞,1)-categories. The proof uses the condensed Barr–Beck–Lurie monadicity theorem applied to the condensed monad − ⊗A B on the condensed (∞,1)-category of condensed A-modules. The condensed solidity of B as a condensed A-module is essential: when A and B are condensed solid rings, the condensed tensor product B ⊗A B is computed in the condensed solid category, and the condensed descent category is equivalent to the condensed category of condensed solid B-modules with condensed solid descent data. This condensed descent formalism underpins the condensed étale cohomology of condensed schemes: for a condensed scheme X = Speccond A, the condensed étale topos Shvcond,ét(X) is the condensed topos of condensed sheaves on the condensed étale site, and the condensed structure sheaf OX,cond is a condensed sheaf of condensed rings satisfying condensed faithfully flat descent. The condensed Čech nerve of a condensed faithfully flat cover Speccond B → Speccond A computes the condensed derived global sections of any condensed quasi-coherent sheaf, yielding the condensed Amitsur complex which is a condensed resolution by condensed faithfully flat condensed A-algebras. In the condensed perfectoid setting, the condensed tilting equivalence identifies the condensed faithfully flat site of a condensed perfectoid algebra with that of its tilt, so condensed faithfully flat descent for condensed perfectoid rings is preserved under condensed tilting.

Condensed Duality Theory and the Condensed Spanier–Whitehead Duality in the Condensed Stable Homotopy Category

The condensed stable homotopy category SHcond is the condensed (∞,1)-category of condensed spectra, obtained by stabilizing the condensed (∞,1)-topos of condensed sets. An object X in SHcond is condensed dualizable if the condensed functor MapSHcond(X, −) is corepresented by a condensed spectrum DX, the condensed Spanier–Whitehead dual. The condensed duality pairing is a condensed map X ∧cond DX → Scond inducing condensed equivalences MapSHcond(X ∧cond Y, Z) ≃ MapSHcond(Y, DX ∧cond Z) for all condensed spectra Y, Z. The condensed sphere spectrum Scond is condensed dualizable with DScond ≃ Scond. A condensed spectrum is condensed dualizable if and only if it is a condensed retract of a condensed finite cell spectrum built from condensed spheres Scondn by iterated condensed cofibers. The condensed dual of a condensed suspension spectrum Σ∞cond X+ for a condensed set X is the condensed function spectrum Fcond(Σ∞cond X+, Scond), which is condensed equivalent to the condensed suspension spectrum of the condensed dualizing object of X when X is a condensed compact Hausdorff space. For a condensed smooth manifold M, the condensed Atiyah duality theorem identifies the condensed Spanier–Whitehead dual of Σ∞cond M+ with the condensed Thom spectrum of the condensed virtual normal bundle −TM. In the condensed p-adic setting, the condensed p-complete sphere Scond∧p is condensed dualizable, and its condensed dual is the condensed p-complete sphere itself. The condensed Anderson dual IcondZp of the condensed p-adic integers is a condensed spectrum representing condensed cohomology with condensed Zp-coefficients, and the condensed duality between condensed homology and condensed cohomology is mediated by the condensed pairing X ∧cond IcondZp → IcondZp. The condensed Brown–Comenetz dual of a condensed spectrum X is the condensed function spectrum Fcond(X, IcondQ/Z), and the condensed Pontryagin duality for condensed abelian groups extends to a condensed duality between condensed connective spectra and condensed coconnective spectra via the condensed Eilenberg–MacLane spectrum HcondZ.

Worked Example: Condensed Faithfully Flat Descent for a Condensed Perfectoid Cover
Let A = Zp⟨T1/p∞⟩ be the condensed perfectoid Tate algebra over Zp, and let B = A⟨U1/p∞⟩/(Up − T) be the condensed faithfully flat cover obtained by adjoining a p-power root of T. Describe the condensed descent datum for a condensed solid B-module M corresponding to a condensed solid A-module N, and verify the condensed cocycle condition on B ⊗A B ⊗A B.
The condensed ring map A → B is condensed faithfully flat because B is a condensed free A-module of rank p with basis 1, U, …, Up−1, and the condensed map on condensed spectra is a condensed surjection. The condensed tensor product B ⊗A B is the condensed perfectoid algebra A⟨U1/p∞, V1/p∞⟩/(Up − T, Vp − T) where U and V are two p-power roots of T. The condensed isomorphism φ: M ⊗B (B ⊗A B) → (B ⊗A B) ⊗B M is determined by the condensed action of the condensed Galois group μp∞ = lim← μpn on M, where ζ ∈ μp∞ acts by U ↦ ζU. Explicitly, φ(m ⊗ 1 ⊗ b) = 1 ⊗ m ⊗ b for m ∈ M, b ∈ B, extended condensed-linearly. The condensed cocycle condition on B ⊗A B ⊗A B = A⟨U, V, W⟩/(Up−T, Vp−T, Wp−T) requires that the two condensed isomorphisms (id ⊗ φ) ∘ (φ ⊗ id) and (φ ⊗ id) ∘ (id ⊗ φ) from M ⊗B (B ⊗A B ⊗A B) to (B ⊗A B ⊗A B) ⊗B M coincide. This holds because the condensed μp∞-action on M is a condensed group action: (ζ1ζ2)·m = ζ1·(ζ2·m). The condensed descent category is equivalent to the condensed category of condensed solid A-modules with a condensed semilinear μp∞-action, which by the condensed tilting equivalence corresponds to condensed solid A♭-modules with a condensed semilinear μp∞-action over the tilt.
Exam Strategy: Recognizing Condensed Descent Patterns
When a problem asks about condensed descent for a condensed ring map A → B, first check whether B is condensed faithfully flat over A by verifying that B is a condensed effective epimorphism and condensed flat. If B is a condensed perfectoid cover, use the condensed Galois group of the cover to encode the condensed descent datum as a condensed semilinear action. The condensed cocycle condition translates to the condensed associativity of this action. For condensed solid rings, remember that the condensed tensor product is computed in the condensed solid category, so all condensed descent data are automatically condensed solid. In condensed étale cohomology, the condensed Čech-to-derived spectral sequence for a condensed faithfully flat cover degenerates at the condensed E2-page when the cover is a condensed pro-étale cover of a condensed perfectoid space.

Condensed Power Operations and the Condensed Steenrod Algebra in Condensed Spectra

The condensed Steenrod algebra Acond at a prime p is the condensed algebra of stable condensed cohomology operations on the condensed mod-p Eilenberg–MacLane spectrum HcondFp. As a condensed ring, Acond is the condensed free associative algebra on condensed generators β (the condensed Bockstein) and Pi (the condensed reduced powers) for i ≥ 1, subject to the condensed Adem relations and the condensed relation β2 = 0. The condensed coproduct Δ: Acond → Acond ⊗cond Acond makes Acond a condensed Hopf algebra, with Δ(β) = β ⊗ 1 + 1 ⊗ β and Δ(Pi) = Σj=0i Pi−j ⊗ Pj. The condensed dual Steenrod algebra Acond* is the condensed homology of HcondFp, a condensed polynomial algebra Fp[ξ1, ξ2, …] ⊗ Λ(τ0, τ1, …) with condensed coproduct Δ(ξn) = Σi=0n ξn−ipi ⊗ ξi and Δ(τn) = τn ⊗ 1 + Σi=0n ξn−ipi ⊗ τi. The condensed power operations on a condensed E∞-ring spectrum R are condensed maps Pi: πn(R) → πn+2i(p−1)(R) for p odd, and condensed maps Sqi: πn(R) → πn+i(R) for p = 2, satisfying the condensed Cartan formula Pi(xy) = Σj=0i Pj(x)Pi−j(y) and the condensed Adem relations. For the condensed sphere spectrum Scond, the condensed Steenrod operations act trivially on π*(Scond) except for the condensed image of the condensed J-homomorphism. The condensed Adams spectral sequence for a condensed spectrum X has condensed E2-page ExtAconds,t(Hcondt(X; Fp), Fp) converging to the condensed p-completion of πt−s(X). The condensed May spectral sequence computes this condensed Ext by filtering the condensed cobar complex on Acond*. In the condensed chromatic setting, the condensed Morava stabilizer group acts on the condensed Lubin–Tate spectrum En,cond, and the condensed power operations on En,cond are determined by the condensed action of the condensed extended Morava stabilizer group on the condensed formal group law.

Condensed Chromatic Homotopy Theory and the Condensed Telescope Conjecture

The condensed chromatic filtration on the condensed stable homotopy category SHcond is the tower of condensed Bousfield localizations Ln,cond with respect to the condensed Morava K-theories K(n)cond for n ≥ 0. The condensed telescope conjecture asserts that for each n ≥ 1, the condensed localization Ln,cond is equivalent to the condensed smashing localization with respect to the condensed telescope of a condensed vn-self-map of a condensed finite type-n spectrum. A condensed vn-self-map of a condensed finite spectrum X of type n is a condensed map v: ΣdX → X such that K(m)cond*(v) is an isomorphism for m = n and zero for m ≠ n. The condensed telescope Tel(v) is the condensed homotopy colimit of the condensed sequence X → Σ−dX → Σ−2dX → …. The condensed smashing localization LTel(v),cond is the condensed localization with respect to Tel(v). The condensed telescope conjecture is known to hold for n = 1 at all primes by the condensed Miller–Ravenel theorem, and for n = 2 at p ≥ 5 by the condensed Behrens–Lawson theorem using condensed topological modular forms. The condensed counterexample at n = 2, p = 2 by the condensed Mahowald–Ravenel–Shick construction shows that the condensed telescope conjecture fails in general: there exists a condensed finite spectrum X of type 2 at p = 2 with a condensed v2-self-map v such that LTel(v),cond ≠ L2,cond. The condensed chromatic convergence theorem states that for any condensed finite spectrum X, the condensed natural map X → holimn Ln,condX is a condensed equivalence. The condensed nilpotence theorem asserts that a condensed map f: X → Y of condensed finite spectra is condensed nilpotent if and only if K(n)cond*(f) = 0 for all n. The condensed thick subcategory theorem classifies condensed thick subcategories of the condensed category of condensed finite spectra: they are precisely the condensed categories of condensed finite spectra of type ≥ n for n ≥ 0. The condensed Balmer spectrum of the condensed tensor-triangulated category of condensed finite spectra is the condensed set {0, 1, 2, …, ∞} with the condensed topology generated by {n, n+1, …, ∞}.

Condensed Realization Functors and the Condensed Betti-to-Étale Comparison for Condensed Varieties

The condensed Betti realization functor ReB,cond from the condensed (∞,1)-category of condensed smooth proper varieties over C to the condensed (∞,1)-category of condensed spectra sends a condensed variety X to the condensed suspension spectrum of the condensed underlying topological space X(C)cond. The condensed étale realization functor Reét,cond sends X to the condensed pro-étale homotopy type Xcond,proét. The condensed comparison map ReB,cond(X) → Reét,cond(X) is a condensed equivalence after condensed profinite completion, by the condensed Artin–Mazur theorem extended to the condensed setting. For a condensed smooth proper variety X over a p-adic field K, the condensed p-adic étale realization Reét,p,cond(X) takes values in the condensed (∞,1)-category of condensed p-adic spectra, and the condensed de Rham realization RedR,cond(X) takes values in the condensed (∞,1)-category of condensed filtered vector spaces over K. The condensed p-adic comparison isomorphism is a condensed equivalence Reét,p,cond(X) ⊗cond BdR,cond ≃ RedR,cond(X) ⊗cond BdR,cond in the condensed (∞,1)-category of condensed BdR,cond-modules, where BdR,cond is the condensed Fontaine period ring. The condensed Hodge–Tate comparison is the condensed graded piece: Reét,p,cond(X) ⊗cond Ccond ≃ ⊕i RedR,condi(X) ⊗cond Ccond(−i). The condensed prismatic realization Re△,cond(X) takes values in the condensed (∞,1)-category of condensed prismatic complexes, and the condensed prismatic comparison Re△,cond(X) ⊗cond Ainf,cond ≃ Reét,p,cond(X) ⊗cond Ainf,cond identifies the condensed Nygaard filtration on the left with the condensed filtration induced by the condensed Galois action on the right. The condensed syntomic realization Resyn,cond(X) is the condensed fiber of the condensed map (1 − φ): Re△,cond(X) → Re△,cond(X) where φ is the condensed Frobenius. The condensed Betti-to-étale comparison for condensed Shimura varieties is compatible with the condensed Hecke action and the condensed Galois action, realizing the condensed Langlands correspondence in condensed cohomology.

  • Confusing the condensed faithfully flat descent condition with the condensed fpqc descent condition: condensed fpqc descent requires the condensed map to be a condensed effective epimorphism in the condensed fpqc topology, which is strictly stronger than condensed faithfully flat for non-Noetherian condensed rings.
  • Assuming the condensed Spanier–Whitehead dual of a condensed suspension spectrum Σ∞cond X+ is always a condensed suspension spectrum: it is a condensed suspension spectrum only when X is a condensed compact Hausdorff space satisfying condensed Poincaré duality.
  • Treating the condensed Steenrod algebra Acond as a discrete algebra rather than a condensed algebra: the condensed topology on Acond is essential for the condensed Adams spectral sequence to converge correctly in the condensed category.
  • Overlooking the condensed solidity requirement in the condensed dual Steenrod algebra: Acond* is a condensed solid coalgebra, and its condensed comultiplication is computed in the condensed solid category, not the condensed discrete category.
  • Assuming the condensed telescope conjecture holds for all n and p: the condensed counterexample at n = 2, p = 2 is a fundamental result, and the condensed telescope conjecture is known to fail for all n ≥ 2 at p = 2.
  • Confusing the condensed Betti realization with the condensed étale realization for varieties over non-archimedean fields: the condensed Betti realization is only defined for varieties over C or R, while the condensed étale realization works for any condensed field.
  • Neglecting the condensed Frobenius action in the condensed prismatic comparison: the condensed Nygaard filtration is defined using the condensed Frobenius φ, and the condensed comparison isomorphism is φ-equivariant, which is essential for the condensed syntomic realization.

Condensed Formalism for the p-adic Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed Eigenvarieties and the Condensed Chenevier Infinite Fern

The condensed eigenvariety construction realizes the p-adic Langlands correspondence for GL₂(ℚₚ) as a condensed geometric object over the condensed weight space. Let ℰcond denote the condensed eigenvariety, a condensed rigid analytic space over the condensed weight space 𝒲cond = Homcont(ℤₚ^×, 𝔾m,cond). The condensed Chenevier infinite fern ℱcond ⊂ ℰcond is the condensed Zariski closure of the set of classical points of weight k ≥ 2. The condensed spectral variety 𝒵cond → 𝒲cond parametrizes condensed systems of Hecke eigenvalues; the condensed eigenvariety ℰcond is the condensed normalization of 𝒵cond. The condensed Buzzard-Kilford theorem describes the condensed geometry of ℰcond at the boundary of weight space: for p = 2, the condensed eigenvariety has infinitely many condensed connected components accumulating at the boundary, forming the condensed infinite fern. The condensed Coleman-Mazur-Buzzard theorem asserts that the condensed map ℰcond → 𝒲cond is finite and flat of degree 2 away from the condensed boundary. The condensed Emerton completed cohomology H̃cond¹ carries a condensed action of the condensed Hecke algebra ℍcond and the condensed group GL₂(ℚₚ). The condensed eigenvariety ℰcond is the condensed rigid analytic spectrum of the condensed Hecke algebra acting on the condensed locally analytic vectors H̃cond¹la. The condensed Chenevier infinite fern ℱcond is characterized as the condensed support of the condensed cuspidal part of H̃cond¹la. The condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence for GL₂(ℚₚ) is realized by the condensed Colmez functor ℱColmez,cond from the condensed category of condensed (φ,Γ)-modules to the condensed category of condensed locally analytic representations of GL₂(ℚₚ), which induces a condensed equivalence on the condensed subcategory of condensed trianguline (φ,Γ)-modules. The condensed eigenvariety ℰcond parametrizes the condensed trianguline (φ,Γ)-modules, and the condensed infinite fern ℱcond corresponds to the condensed irreducible trianguline (φ,Γ)-modules. The condensed geometry of ℰcond at classical points encodes the condensed local-global compatibility: the condensed completed cohomology H̃cond¹ realizes the condensed p-adic Langlands correspondence for GL₂(ℚₚ) in families over ℰcond.

Condensed Approach to the p-adic Hodge Theory for Rigid Analytic Varieties via the Condensed Relative Prismatic Cohomology and the Condensed Hodge-Tate Comparison for Families

The condensed relative prismatic cohomology provides a family version of the condensed prismatic comparison for proper smooth morphisms of rigid analytic varieties. Let f: X → S be a proper smooth morphism of condensed rigid analytic varieties over a p-adic field K. The condensed relative prismatic cohomology ℙ𝕣X/S,cond is a condensed prismatic complex over the condensed relative prism (Ainf,cond(S), (ξ)) where Ainf,cond(S) is the condensed Ainf-ring of S. The condensed relative Hodge-Tate comparison is a condensed isomorphism ℙ𝕣X/S,cond ⊗Ainf,cond(S)L Ainf,cond(S)/(ξ) ≃ ⊕i ΩX/S,condⁱ[-i] in the condensed derived category Dcond(Ainf,cond(S)/(ξ)). The condensed relative de Rham comparison is a condensed isomorphism ℙ𝕣X/S,cond ⊗Ainf,cond(S)L BdR,cond(S) ≃ Rfcond,* ΩX/S,cond^• ⊗OS,cond BdR,cond(S) compatible with the condensed Nygaard filtration on the left and the condensed Hodge filtration on the right. The condensed relative crystalline comparison identifies ℙ𝕣X/S,cond ⊗Ainf,cond(S)L Bcris,cond(S) with the condensed relative crystalline cohomology of X/S. The condensed relative syntomic cohomology RΓsyn,cond(X/S) is the condensed fiber of (1 - φ): ℙ𝕣X/S,cond → ℙ𝕣X/S,cond. The condensed relative Nygaard filtration N•cond on ℙ𝕣X/S,cond satisfies Nicond = φ-1(ξi) and the condensed Hodge-Tate comparison is strictly compatible with the condensed Nygaard filtration. The condensed relative prismatic cohomology satisfies condensed base change: for any condensed morphism S' → S of condensed rigid analytic varieties, the condensed natural map ℙ𝕣X/S,cond ⊗Ainf,cond(S)L Ainf,cond(S') → ℙ𝕣X×SS'/S',cond is a condensed isomorphism. The condensed relative prismatic cohomology is a condensed perfect complex over Ainf,cond(S) and its condensed formation commutes with condensed arbitrary base change. The condensed relative Hodge-Tate comparison for families implies the condensed variation of Hodge structure on the condensed relative de Rham cohomology is induced by the condensed Nygaard filtration on the condensed relative prismatic cohomology.

Worked Example: Condensed Relative Hodge-Tate Comparison for a Family of Elliptic Curves
Let π: E → S be a family of elliptic curves over a condensed rigid analytic space S over ℚₚ. Describe the condensed relative prismatic cohomology ℙ𝕣E/S,cond and the condensed Hodge-Tate comparison isomorphism in degree 1.
The condensed relative prismatic cohomology ℙ𝕣E/S,cond is a condensed perfect complex over Ainf,cond(S) concentrated in degrees [0,2]. In degree 1, ℙ𝕣E/S,cond¹ is a condensed projective Ainf,cond(S)-module of rank 2 equipped with a condensed Frobenius φ and a condensed Nygaard filtration N•cond. The condensed Hodge-Tate comparison gives a condensed isomorphism ℙ𝕣E/S,cond¹ ⊗Ainf,cond(S) Ainf,cond(S)/(ξ) ≃ H⁰(E, ΩE/S,cond¹) ⊕ H¹(E, OE,cond)[-1]. The condensed Nygaard filtration satisfies N⁰cond = ℙ𝕣E/S,cond¹, N¹cond = ker(φ - ξ), N²cond = 0. The condensed Hodge filtration on the right corresponds to the condensed filtration induced by N•cond on the left. The condensed Frobenius φ acts on ℙ𝕣E/S,cond¹ with characteristic polynomial X² - ap,condX + p where ap,cond is the condensed trace of Frobenius. The condensed relative de Rham cohomology R¹πcond,* ΩE/S,cond^• carries the condensed Gauss-Manin connection, which is recovered from the condensed prismatic cohomology via the condensed de Rham comparison ℙ𝕣E/S,cond¹ ⊗Ainf,cond(S) BdR,cond(S) ≃ R¹πcond,* ΩE/S,cond^• ⊗OS,cond BdR,cond(S).
Exam Strategy: Family Versions of p-adic Comparisons
When a question asks about families, always check whether the comparison isomorphisms are compatible with base change. The condensed relative prismatic cohomology is the unique theory satisfying condensed base change and the condensed Hodge-Tate comparison. Memorize the three key properties: (1) condensed perfectness over Ainf,cond(S), (2) condensed base change compatibility, (3) condensed Nygaard filtration inducing the condensed Hodge filtration. The condensed Nygaard filtration Nicond = φ-1(ξi) is the defining formula — write it down explicitly.

Condensed Formalism for the Geometric Langlands Program via the Condensed Categorical Geometric Langlands and the Condensed Arinkin-Gaitsgory Induction

The condensed categorical geometric Langlands correspondence for a reductive group G over a condensed curve X over an algebraically closed field k of characteristic 0 is an equivalence of condensed (∞,1)-categories between the condensed category of condensed D-modules on the condensed moduli stack BunG,cond(X) and the condensed category of condensed quasi-coherent sheaves on the condensed moduli stack LocSysǦ,cond(X) of Ǧ-local systems, where Ǧ is the condensed Langlands dual group. The condensed Arinkin-Gaitsgory induction functor Indcond: Dcond(BunG,cond) → Dcond(BunG,cond) is defined by the condensed convolution with the condensed Hecke eigensheaf. The condensed Hecke category HeckeG,cond(X) is the condensed category of condensed D-modules on the condensed Hecke stack HeckeG,cond(X) = Mapscond(X, GG((t))/G[[t]]). The condensed Satake equivalence identifies the condensed spherical Hecke category with the condensed category of condensed representations of the condensed Langlands dual group Ǧcond. The condensed spectral action of the condensed category QCohcond(LocSysǦ,cond(X)) on Dcond(BunG,cond(X)) is constructed via the condensed Arinkin-Gaitsgory induction. The condensed categorical geometric Langlands equivalence is the condensed functor Lcond: Dcond(BunG,cond(X)) → QCohcond(LocSysǦ,cond(X)) induced by the condensed spectral action of the condensed unit object. The condensed Whittaker category Whitcond(BunG,cond(X)) is the condensed category of condensed D-modules on BunG,cond(X) with condensed Whittaker coefficients. The condensed categorical geometric Langlands equivalence restricts to a condensed equivalence between the condensed Whittaker category and the condensed category of condensed quasi-coherent sheaves on the condensed moduli stack of Ǧ-local systems with a condensed Whittaker reduction. The condensed Arinkin-Gaitsgory induction satisfies the condensed projection formula and the condensed base change property with respect to condensed correspondences of condensed curves. The condensed categorical geometric Langlands correspondence for GLn is constructed via the condensed geometric Eisenstein series and the condensed constant term functors. The condensed Hecke eigensheaf property is encoded in the condensed spectral action: for any condensed local system E ∈ LocSysǦ,cond(X), the condensed skyscraper sheaf δE,cond acts on Dcond(BunG,cond(X)) by the condensed Hecke eigensheaf functor with eigenvalue E.

Condensed p-adic Hodge Theory: The Condensed Fontaine-Laffaille Theory and the Condensed Crystalline Comparison for Semi-Stable Reduction

The condensed Fontaine-Laffaille theory provides a condensed classification of condensed crystalline Galois representations with Hodge-Tate weights in [0, p-2] via condensed Fontaine-Laffaille modules. Let K be a p-adic field with perfect residue field k. The condensed category FLcond(K) of condensed Fontaine-Laffaille modules over K consists of condensed finite free Wcond(k)-modules M equipped with a condensed decreasing filtration Fil•cond M and condensed Frobenius-linear maps φi,cond: Filicond M → M satisfying φi,cond = piφ0,cond on Filicond M ∩ pFili+1cond M. The condensed Fontaine-Laffaille functor Tcris,cond: FLcond(K) → Repℤₚ,cond(GK,cond) is a condensed exact fully faithful tensor functor whose essential image is the condensed subcategory of condensed crystalline representations with Hodge-Tate weights in [0, p-2]. The condensed crystalline comparison for semi-stable reduction relates the condensed prismatic cohomology of a semi-stable formal scheme to its condensed log crystalline cohomology. Let 𝔛 be a semi-stable formal scheme over OK,cond with special fiber Xk,cond a condensed simple normal crossings divisor. The condensed log prismatic cohomology ℙ𝕣𝔛,condlog is a condensed prismatic complex over Ainf,cond(OK,cond) with a condensed log Nygaard filtration. The condensed Hyodo-Kato isomorphism is a condensed isomorphism ℙ𝕣𝔛,condlog ⊗Ainf,condL Bcris,cond ≃ Hlog-cris,cond⁰(Xk,cond/Wcond(k)) ⊗Wcond(k) Bcris,cond compatible with the condensed Frobenius and the condensed monodromy operator Ncond. The condensed monodromy operator Ncond on the condensed log crystalline cohomology satisfies Ncondφ = pφNcond and the condensed weight filtration W•,cond is the condensed filtration induced by Ncond. The condensed Fontaine-Laffaille module associated to a condensed crystalline representation V with Hodge-Tate weights in [0, p-2] is Mcond = (V ⊗ℤₚ Bcris,cond)GK,cond with the condensed filtration induced by the condensed Hodge filtration on BdR,cond. The condensed crystalline comparison for semi-stable reduction implies the condensed weight spectral sequence degenerates at E₂ and the condensed monodromy-weight conjecture holds in the condensed setting.

Condensed Approach to the p-adic Local Langlands Correspondence via the Condensed Breuil-Mézard Conjecture and the Condensed Patchings of Deformation Rings

The condensed Breuil-Mézard conjecture predicts a condensed equality between the condensed Hilbert-Samuel multiplicities of the condensed potentially semi-stable deformation rings and the condensed multiplicities of the condensed Serre weights in the condensed mod p local Langlands correspondence. Let ρ̄: Gℚₚ,cond → GLn,cond(𝔽̄p,cond) be a condensed continuous residual Galois representation. The condensed potentially semi-stable deformation ring Rcondps,τ,cond parametrizes condensed lifts of ρ̄ with fixed Hodge type τ and fixed inertial type. The condensed Breuil-Mézard cycle Zcond(ρ̄, τ) is the condensed cycle on the condensed reduced deformation ring Spec Rcondps,τ,cond/p defined by the condensed sum of condensed Hilbert-Samuel multiplicities of the condensed irreducible components. The condensed Serre weight conjecture (now theorem) gives a condensed set of condensed Serre weights Wcond(ρ̄) associated to ρ̄. The condensed Breuil-Mézard conjecture asserts that the condensed cycle Zcond(ρ̄, τ) equals the condensed sum over σ ∈ Wcond(ρ̄) of the condensed multiplicity mcond(σ, τ) times the condensed cycle [Spec Rcondσ,cond] where Rcondσ,cond is the condensed deformation ring of type σ. The condensed Taylor-Wiles-Kisin patching method constructs a condensed patched module M∞,cond over a condensed power series ring S∞,cond = Ocond[[x₁, ..., xg,cond]] with a condensed action of the condensed Hecke algebra. The condensed patched module M∞,cond is a condensed maximal Cohen-Macaulay module over S∞,cond and its condensed support is the condensed patched deformation ring R∞,cond. The condensed Calegari-Geraghty method extends the condensed patching to non-minimal deformation problems using the condensed Taylor-Wiles systems with condensed auxiliary primes. The condensed Breuil-Mézard conjecture for GL₂(ℚₚ) is proved by the condensed work of Hu-Wang and the condensed Emerton-Gee stack. The condensed patching of deformation rings realizes the condensed p-adic local Langlands correspondence for GL₂(ℚₚ) as a condensed equivalence between the condensed category of condensed (φ,Γ)-modules and the condensed category of condensed locally analytic representations, compatible with the condensed Breuil-Mézard cycles. The condensed multiplicity mcond(σ, τ) is computed by the condensed Kisin variety of the condensed potentially crystalline deformation ring.

  • Confusing the condensed eigenvariety ℰcond with the condensed spectral variety 𝒵cond: ℰcond is the condensed normalization of 𝒵cond, and the condensed map ℰcond → 𝒵cond is a condensed isomorphism only at smooth points of 𝒵cond.
  • Assuming the condensed relative prismatic cohomology ℙ𝕣X/S,cond is always a condensed perfect complex over Ainf,cond(S) without the proper smooth hypothesis: the condensed perfectness requires f: X → S to be proper smooth, and fails for non-proper or non-smooth morphisms.
  • Neglecting the condensed log structure in the condensed Hyodo-Kato isomorphism for semi-stable reduction: the condensed log prismatic cohomology ℙ𝕣𝔛,condlog is essential, and the condensed monodromy operator Ncond acts on the condensed log crystalline cohomology, not the condensed usual crystalline cohomology.
  • Treating the condensed Fontaine-Laffaille functor Tcris,cond as an equivalence of condensed categories: it is only fully faithful with essential image the condensed subcategory of condensed crystalline representations with Hodge-Tate weights in [0, p-2], not all condensed crystalline representations.
  • Confusing the condensed Arinkin-Gaitsgory induction Indcond with the condensed Hecke functor: Indcond is the condensed convolution with the condensed Hecke eigensheaf, while the condensed Hecke functor is the condensed convolution with the condensed spherical Hecke category.
  • Assuming the condensed Breuil-Mézard conjecture holds for all n and all Hodge types τ: it is proved for GL₂(ℚₚ) and some cases of GL₃(ℚₚ), but remains open in general for n ≥ 3 and arbitrary τ.
  • Overlooking the condensed base change property of the condensed relative prismatic cohomology: the condensed isomorphism ℙ𝕣X/S,cond ⊗Ainf,cond(S)L Ainf,cond(S') ≃ ℙ𝕣X×SS'/S',cond is a fundamental theorem, not a definition, and requires the condensed proper smooth hypothesis.
